# Exegra

A bidirectional, executable, visual specification environment for software systems, and its own first subject. The JSONC specification text is the single authority; the 3D scene, inspector, source editor and agent command line are views of, and editors for, one transactional semantic model.

Generated by Exegra from the semantic model. Semantic hash 0cb40db2b60a01. Do not edit; regenerate.

### Semantic core

Owns the canonical semantic model and every rule about it; runs unchanged in the browser and in Node.

- Category: Logical module

#### Responsibilities

- Derive the model from specification text and validate it
- Apply typed operations atomically as source-preserving text edits
- Evaluate claims and simulate state machines on the model

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/types.js`
- Notes: types.js holds JSDoc typedefs only. The directory src/core also contains projections.js, layout.js with the modules under layout/, and generate/, which this file classifies under visualization and tooling by responsibility, and own.js, claimed by queries; they import nothing outside src/core.

### Meta-model

Describes entity kinds, their properties, the built-in categories and relationship kinds and the top-level sections of a specification as data.

- Category: Data store
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)
- Owner: semantic-core

#### Responsibilities

- Define which properties each entity kind accepts and their types
- Define the built-in vocabulary (categories, relationship kinds, shapes, colours, projections) that a specification may extend
- Define the id pattern and the schema version (0.1)
- Define the claim kinds (structural, invariant, temporal, measured, requirement) and the view definition: filter (categories, relationship kinds, within, tags, entity kinds) and display (ports, colour by, reference depth, layout method)
- Define the record and copy kinds of the data model: field types, personal-data classes, roles, copy scopes (shared, per-session, per-principal, internal) and the units of freshness and erasure bounds

#### Relationships in

- Reads → Parser: Checks structure and property types against the entity definitions in metamodel.json.
- Reads → Transaction system: Reads the id pattern.
- Reads → Edit dialogs: Reads the id pattern and checks ids with it.
- Reads → Data model: The record and copy kinds and their enums are declared in the meta-model.
- Reads → Generation readiness: The type kind, the record and contract properties and the requirement realisation are declared in the meta-model.
- Reads → Specification store service: Takes the entity sections and their kinds from metamodel.json.
- Reads → Specification layers: Reads the id pattern and the sections of the specification format.

#### Known unknowns

- The built-in entity kinds, claim kinds, claim evaluation methods and projections are fixed per build. A specification can add entity kinds, properties of any kind, categories, relationship kinds and verification methods (carried out outside Exegra) through its vocabulary, but it cannot add an evaluation method that Exegra itself runs.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/metamodel.json`
- Notes: Hand-written data file.

### Claim method registry

Declares the supported claim evaluation methods with their parameters, and the five claim statuses.

- Category: Data store
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)
- Owner: semantic-core

#### Responsibilities

- List each method, the claim kinds it can evaluate and its typed parameters
- Define the claim status labels and tones that every consumer shows
- Declare `negatives` as a parameter of every trace method (state-invariant, precedes, eventually, never): a list of {scenario, faults}, each one of the claim's scenarios run with extra faults
- Declare the refines method: one implementation behavior and optionally the scenarios whose traces are checked

#### Relationships in

- Reads → Parser: Imports CLAIM_METHODS to check evaluation parameters.
- Reads → Validator: Checks each claim's method and parameters against the registry.
- Reads → Claim evaluator: Dispatches only on registered methods; an unregistered method stays not evaluated.
- Reads → System explorer: Reads claim status labels.
- Reads → Semantic inspector: Reads claim status labels and tones.
- Reads → Property controls: Describes claim evaluation methods in the evaluation editor.
- Reads → Verification panel: Reads claim status labels and tones.
- Reads → Artifact generators: Reads claim status labels.
- Calls → Application shell: Names a selected claim's status in the inspector's summary line.
- Calls → Agent command line: Reads the registry of claim methods for its usage and validation messages.

#### Known unknowns

- The registry declares methods but the evaluator implements them separately; adding an entry without code leaves the claim not evaluated.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/claim-methods.js`
- Notes: Hand-written data module used by the parser, validator, evaluator and inspector.

### Parser

The text layer of the specification: parses the JSONC source (comments, trailing commas, syntax errors with locations), hands the document to the model derivation in the Lean kernel and maps the raw paths it reports to lines and columns.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Parse JSONC with comments and trailing commas, reporting syntax errors with locations
- Encode the document for the kernel with every object's members in source order and repeated keys kept, and pass the meta-model and the claim method registry as data
- Map every raw path and part the kernel reports (node, key of a member, first character) to an offset, length, line and column, and put the line of the earlier declaration into an id.duplicate message
- Rebuild the model the rest of the shell reads (entities by id and kind, references, expressions, effective meta-model, vocabulary, semantic hash) from the kernel's reply, with prototype-free data objects as jsonc-parser builds them

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Derivation cache: parseSpec asks the cache for the derived model of a text before deriving it.
- Reads → Meta-model: Checks structure and property types against the entity definitions in metamodel.json.
- Reads → Claim method registry: Imports CLAIM_METHODS to check evaluation parameters.
- Depends on → jsonc-parser: Builds the syntax tree and reports syntax errors with the library.
- Calls → Lean call boundary: Derives every model with the Lean function model.derive.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Validator: analyzeSpec calls parseSpec, then validates the model.
- Calls → Transaction system: Re-derives the model after every text edit and parses a replacement source.
- Calls → Source editor: Parses the draft to find the location of an entity to reveal.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise parsing and normalization.
- Calls → Specification store service: Derives the model of an imported text for the references and the semantic hash of its rows.
- Calls → Specification layers: Parses the platform text alone for its model and semantic hash.

#### Known unknowns

- The whole text is re-parsed on every commit and on every source-editor analysis; there is no incremental parsing. Measured 2026-11-18 in Node 24 on Windows 10 (wasm64, median of 10 warm runs, parse and hash): specs/exegra (788 KB) 243 ms through the Lean kernel against 35 ms for the JavaScript it replaced; specs/benchmark (134 KB) 67 ms against 9 ms. Of the 243 ms about 95 ms is Exegra.Json.parse reading the request (WG-4797), 27 ms the derivation, 10 ms reference resolution, 33 ms the hash and 45 ms writing the reply. In Chromium (Playwright, tests/browser/lean-core.spec.js) the same parse and hash take 312 ms (median of 7).
- The schema.version error message tells the user to run an `exegra migrate` command that does not exist.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/parse.js`
- Notes: Outside the verdict path: it only parses text and maps locations; the model is derived by model-derivation in Lean.

### Validator

Applies whole-model semantic rules to a parsed model and reports errors, warnings and specification gaps; analyzeSpec composes parser and validator.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Check containment, behaviors, transitions, effects, faults, scenarios, claim parameters and views
- Accept an evaluation method the specification declares as a verification method (it takes no parameters), and report any other method this build does not run as unsupported
- Check every expression's references against the scope it is used in
- Report gaps (missing purpose, unconnected component, unused event, claim that can only rest on external evidence and has none) as info diagnostics; a requirement claim is not reported for lacking evidence
- Provide analyzeSpec: parse, then validate only when the structure is sound
- Check events that name a contract: a role is required, the payload fields are among the inputs (request) or outputs (response), every input that is not optional is present, an error carries a code among the contract's errors, and declared field types are assignable to the contract's; an unknown contract is a reference error
- Run the data model's consistency checks (record keys and copy fields name declared fields, bounds have units) as errors and its six data rules as warnings, each naming the record or copy and the field
- Check each negative of a trace claim: its scenario is one of the claim's scenarios, and its faults are a non-empty list of declared faults none of which that scenario already activates
- Report integrity rules: an invalid rule as an error, a vacuous rule as a warning, and each violation with the rule severity (integrity.violated), left out of analyzeSpec's blocking error count
- Check refinement declarations: only an implementation-level behavior refines, the refined behavior does not refine in turn, refines comes with a refinement mapping, the mapping names only states and transitions of the right sides and variables of the model, its expressions read only the implementation's variables, and every implementation state and transition and every model variable is mapped; a refines claim names one refining behavior and only scenarios that run it
- Check timer delays read at run time ({expr, simulate}): only on an implementation-level machine, expr an expression of a number over the machine variables, time and the event payload, and simulate the delay a simulated run uses (simulationInputV2 lowers the timer with it)
- Check time and integers in implementation-level machines (src/core/readiness-time.js, WG-4902): a variable compared with time holds a value set from time wherever it is read (ready.time-epoch); time - x is read only after x <= time (ready.time-difference); the scenarios running the machine agree on a time unit, fixed delays are whole milliseconds and a machine reading time runs in ms (ready.delay-unit); literals, integer initial values and scenario payload values are within their integer range, at most 2^53 - 1 (ready.number-bound)
- Check the realisation and durability of machines (src/core/realisation.js, WG-4922): behavior.realisation and contract.deployment (shipped or target, defaulting from the component's implementation status; an explicit shipped on a not-implemented component is behavior.realisation-subject or contract.deployment-subject) and storage.durability (record or memory; a memory machine names no record, creates none and declares no transaction, or storage.durability)
- Check residency (src/core/residency.js): follow each record a tenant class binds to its owner, the holder of each copy and both ends of each relationship that carries it, and report a component that cannot keep a tenant of the class in its permitted regions (residency.record-outside, residency.copy-outside, residency.flow-outside) unless an active residency exception names that copy or flow; a holder of bound data states its placement (residency.placement-missing) and a per-region one has an instance in every provisioned region (residency.region-uncovered); placement and regions agree (component.placement-regions); a tenant class has one selector and at least one region (tenant-class.selector, tenant-class.provisioned-in); an exception names one copy or flow of bound data, its durations are durations, a bounded one states its purge and a renewal window is shorter than its longest duration (residency.exception-target, residency.exception-duration, residency.exception-purge, residency.exception-renewal)
- Check quota pools (src/core/pools.js): at least one pooled limit with a unique lower-case name (pool.limits), exactly one of quota and a positive perMember (pool.limit-contribution), a pooled quota counted per tenant over the pool's window as a rate (pool.quota-scope, pool.quota-window, pool.limit-kind) and held by the pool's component (pool.held-by), usage and exemption events with the payload fields the pool reads (pool.usage-event, pool.event-payload), the window, over-share count and release (pool.window, pool.over-share, pool.release), 1 to 4 modelled members (pool.model-members), an exemption role that confers its capability (pool.exemption-capability) and machine ids no entity takes (pool.machine-id); a scenario runs a behavior or a pool (scenario.empty), and a claim expression reads only variables of the machines of pools every listed scenario runs (expr.reference)

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Derivation cache: analyzeSpec asks the cache for the semantic diagnostics of a text before validating it.
- Calls → Model queries: Imports the own-property lookup for the verification methods, claim methods and relationship kinds a specification names.
- Calls → Parser: analyzeSpec calls parseSpec, then validates the model.
- Calls → Expression language: Parses guards, effect values and claim expressions and lists their references.
- Reads → Claim method registry: Checks each claim's method and parameters against the registry.
- Calls → Fault model and environment: Runs the fault model rules (missing sources, bounds, realises outside the model) as part of validation.
- Calls → Contract conformance: Checks the payload of every contract event against the fields, types and errors of its contract.
- Calls → Data model: Runs the data rules and the record and copy consistency checks as part of validation.
- Calls → Integrity rules: Reports invalid rules, vacuous rules and every current violation as diagnostics.
- Calls → Metric claims: Validates metric-bound claims with the statistics of src/core/metric-stats.js.
- Calls → Generation readiness: Runs the type, record and realisation consistency checks, and the readiness rules when system.generation is set.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Transaction system: Project calls analyzeSpec on every candidate specification and refuses results with errors.
- Calls → Toolbar: Import analyzes the bundle's spec.jsonc with analyzeSpec and refuses it when it has syntax errors.
- Calls → Source editor: Analyzes the draft with analyzeSpec for its diagnostics.
- Calls → Specification checker: Analyzes the file with analyzeSpec.
- Calls → Benchmark generator: Checks that the generated specification analyzes.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the validation rules.
- Calls → Agent command line: Applies the evidence reference rule (src/core/evidence-ref.js) to the commands that run verification.
- Calls → Machine coverage: Reads whether a machine is shipped (effectiveRealisation).
- Calls → Specification store service: loadModelFromStore analyzes the exported text with analyzeSpec.
- Calls → Specification layers: analyzeSpec derives and validates the combined text.

#### Known unknowns

- A residency exception's expiry, continuous renewal and purge are recorded and shape-checked but not executed, so claims about them are not decided by exploration (WG-5282).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/validate.js`, `src/core/analyze.js`, `src/core/evidence-ref.js`, `src/core/readiness-machines.js`, `src/core/builtin-types.js`, `src/core/readiness-time.js`, `src/core/realisation.js`, `src/core/residency.js`
- Notes: The rules are hard-coded. Claim parameter checks read the claim method registry, the gap rules read `expected` flags of the effective meta-model and view filters are checked against the effective vocabulary.

### Expression language

A small side-effect-free expression language for guards, effect values and claim expressions; no host-language evaluation is involved.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Tokenize and parse expressions (implication, boolean and arithmetic operators, comparisons, string and number literals, dotted references with hyphenated ids)
- Evaluate an expression against a caller-supplied reference resolver
- List the references an expression uses so the validator can check scope
- Evaluate with the declared types of references when the caller gives them (WG-4902): checked integer arithmetic in u8, u16, u32, i32 and in u64 and i64 limited to +-(2^53 - 1), integer division truncating toward zero and remainder with the sign of the dividend, an absent option read as null; structural equality of lists and objects always; the same rules as eval in formal/Exegra/Expr.lean and the generated code (docs/semantics.md, Values, numbers and time)

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Validator: Parses guards, effect values and claim expressions and lists their references.
- Calls → Transaction system: Operations parse expressions when renaming ids that expressions mention.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Evaluates claim expressions against trace configurations.
- Calls → Proof export: Parses claim expressions and evaluates them on a counterexample's last configuration to explain it.
- Calls → Self-model replay: Evaluates guards, effect values and emitted payloads.
- Calls → Generation readiness: Parses the conditions, custom validation rules and audit captures of the cross-cutting constructs, and the arguments and navigation parameters of forms and actions, and lists their references.
- Calls → Machine coverage: Parses guards and effect values for their literals, their references to time and the variables guards read.
- Calls → Claim strength: Parses claim expressions, guards and effect values for the value analysis and the grounding.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/expr.js`
- Notes: The validator and the claim evaluator use src/core/expr.js. The simulation engine has its own implementation of the same language in Rust (engine/exegra-engine/src/expr.rs), held to the same parse and evaluation semantics and error messages by the conformance goldens.

### Normal form and hash

Produces the deterministic normal form of a model (independent of comments, whitespace, key order, top-level declaration order and source positions) and its 53-bit semantic hash, in the verified kernel.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Normal form: schema version, vocabulary and the top-level declarations sorted by id with every object's keys sorted as JavaScript sorts strings (UTF-16 code units)
- Semantic hash: cyrb53 over the UTF-16 code units of the normal form's JSON text, bit-identical to the JavaScript it replaced; used for revision identity, optimistic concurrency of transactions and claim results
- Prove that the hash depends only on semantic content: declaration order and presentation (Exegra.Model.Model.semanticHash_presentation) and key order at every depth (Exegra.Hash.semanticHash_key_order); what runs is the definition (Exegra.Hash.semanticHash'_eq)

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Lean call boundary: Dispatches hash.semantic and hash.normalize to the semantic hash and the normal form.
- Calls → Model derivation: Computes the semantic hash of every derived model.

#### Known unknowns

- The hash is not cryptographic (cyrb53); it identifies revisions and detects change, it does not resist collisions.
- The key-order theorem assumes distinct keys in every object; the derivation produces such values (it assigns members like JavaScript), but that is not itself stated as a theorem yet.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `formal/Exegra/Hash.lean`, `formal/Exegra/HashProps.lean`
- Notes: The Lean kernel of the hash only. The JavaScript that calls it is hash-binding (src/core/hash.js), a separate component, so this component's dependencies are only the Lean definitions it uses. Stable JSON output and SHA-256 are stable-serialization.

Answers containment, dependency, selection, reference and contract questions about a model for every consumer.

- Category: Logical module

#### Responsibilities

- Select components by id, category, kind, tag, trust zone or containment
