- Calls → Validator: Imports the own-property lookup for the verification methods, claim methods and relationship kinds a specification names.
- Calls → Transaction system: Finds declaration sites and owned entities when creating and deleting.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Builds the component graph and selects components for structural claims.
- Calls → Projection system: Resolves components, ancestors and descendants for projection.
- Calls → Toolbar: Labels search results.
- Calls → System explorer: Labels entities and finds ancestors.
- Calls → Semantic inspector: Lists relationships, dependencies, contracts, children and references of the selection.
- Calls → Property controls: Labels referenced entities and finds declaration sites.
- Calls → Edit dialogs: Labels entities and finds references.
- Calls → Simulator panel: Labels entities.
- Calls → Verification panel: Labels claims.
- Calls → Realisation panel: Labels components.
- Calls → Agent command line: Answers list, show, deps, contracts and plan-delete.
- Calls → Artifact generators: Lists children, relationships and contracts for the architecture document.
- Calls → Application shell: Labels the selection in the inspector's summary line.
- Calls → Data model: Builds the component graph to find a path between a copy's holder and a record's owner.
- Calls → Reader pages: Uses own() for keys the specification supplies.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/query.js`, `src/core/own.js`
- Notes: own.js is a one-function helper; it is claimed here because the lookup it provides is imported by the other core modules that read spec-supplied keys.

### Transaction system

Applies typed operations atomically as source-preserving text edits, with revisions, undo, redo, reload and commit notification; it is the only place where a project's state changes.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Check operation preconditions and compute JSONC edits that keep comments and formatting
- Reject any transaction whose resulting specification has validation errors (violations of integrity rules excepted) or whose layout would be unreadable; nothing changes on rejection
- Keep presentation edits (layout) out of the specification text
- Record history sufficient for undo and redo and increment a revision on every commit
- Adopt text that changed outside the process (reload): text with syntax errors is refused, any text that parses is adopted, including text with validation errors; the adoption clears the undo and redo stacks and is itself not undoable
- Leave the model and its semantic hash as they were when a commit changes only presentation text
- Notify subscribers synchronously after each commit
- Reject a transaction that adds a violation of an integrity rule of severity error that the project did not have before (the ratchet), with the counterexamples; existing violations do not block unrelated edits
- Re-derive the model once per transaction, not once per operation: keep an index of where every entity is declared that follows creations, property edits, removals, replacements of nested declarations and id changes, re-derive only for an operation that reads references, expressions or entity data (deletion, rename, source replacement) or after an edit the index cannot follow, and splice the edits of each declaration's lines into the text once; the resulting text, failures and verdict are those of re-deriving after every edit, and a transaction whose index is found not to match its model is replayed that way

#### Ports

| Name | Id | Direction | Contract | Protocol |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Apply transaction | transactions-apply-in | provides | Apply a transaction | in-process call (Project.apply) |

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Parser: Re-derives the model after every text edit and parses a replacement source.
- Calls → Validator: Project calls analyzeSpec on every candidate specification and refuses results with errors.
- Calls → Expression language: Operations parse expressions when renaming ids that expressions mention.
- Calls → Model queries: Finds declaration sites and owned entities when creating and deleting.
- Reads → Meta-model: Reads the id pattern.
- Depends on → jsonc-parser: Computes and applies the minimal text edits that keep comments and formatting.
- Data flow → Application session: Each commit delivers its TransactionResult and history entry to the listener the session subscribed.
- Calls → Integrity rules: Project.apply refuses a transaction that adds a violation of an error rule (the ratchet).
- Calls → Semantic hash binding: Computes the semantic hash of each committed model and the text hash of a project.
- Calls → Specification layers: A Project with product files analyses every candidate platform text with its product files layered on it.

#### Relationships in

- Submits edits to → Visual interaction and application entry: A drag becomes MovePresentationNode operations submitted through session.apply.
- Calls → Application session: Creates a Project per opened project and forwards apply, undo, redo and reload.
- Reads → Application session: Reads the committed model, diagnostics, texts and revision from the project; the UI reads them through the session.
- Subscribes → Application session: Registers its commit listener with Project.subscribe; the project knows it only as a callback.
- Submits edits to → Application shell: Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z and Ctrl+Y call undo and redo on the project through the session.
- Submits edits to → Toolbar: Reset layout submits MovePresentationNode with reset; undo and redo go through the session.
- Submits edits to → Semantic inspector: Every property change is a SetProperty operation submitted through session.apply.
- Calls → Edit dialogs: Imports planDelete to preview what a delete removes and repairs.
- Submits edits to → Edit dialogs: Submits CreateEntity, CreateRelationship, DeleteEntity and RenameEntity through session.apply.
- Reads → Source editor: Reads the committed spec text and revision from the project.
- Submits edits to → Source editor: Applying a draft submits a ReplaceSource operation through session.apply.
- Submits edits to → Agent command line: The apply command submits operations through Project.apply with origin cli; nothing edits specification text directly.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise Project and the operations.

#### Contracts bound

- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): provides, via `transactions-apply-in`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): provider, via `visual-interaction-submits-edits-transactions`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): provider, via `session-calls-transactions`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): provider, via `shell-submits-edits-transactions`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): provider, via `toolbar-submits-edits-transactions`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): provider, via `inspector-submits-edits-transactions`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): provider, via `dialogs-submits-edits-transactions`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): provider, via `source-editor-submits-edits-transactions`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): provider, via `agent-cli-submits-edits-transactions`

#### Known unknowns

- History, undo and redo stacks are unbounded in memory and are not persisted: undo is lost on reload and per CLI invocation.
- Only operation transactions and replacement sources are validated before they commit. Undo and redo restore text that was committed before and do not validate again, and reload commits any text that parses, so the model after a reload can carry validation errors.
- The session never passes expectRevision or expectHash; only the CLI uses the optimistic checks (--expect-hash).
- A deletion or a rename re-derives the model of the current text before it plans its edits, because references and expressions of entities edited earlier in the transaction are known only to a derivation: about 2.6 s each on a 7 MB specification (WG-4980).
- The validation of a transaction's result is a full validation: rule and example results are not cached per entity (WG-4974). Measured on a 7 MB specification, validation is about 0.9 s of a single-property apply; the model derivation is about 2.6 s.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/operations.js`, `src/core/project.js`, `src/core/text-edit.js`
- Notes: The operation table is hand-written; CreateEntity looks up declaration sites in the meta-model. Tx keeps the index of declarations, the windows over edited declarations and the pending appends; Project.apply's perOperation option runs the reference path that re-derives after every edit.

### Claim evaluator

Evaluates claims against a model and recorded evidence results, reporting a status, scope and counterexample that never overstate what was checked.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Evaluate structural claims (no-relationship, reachable, only-via, has-port, property-defined, acyclic) on the component graph; report a selector that selects nothing as inconclusive, never verified; reachable needs a path of at least one relationship (a target that is also a source does not count); only-via is inconclusive when no directed path of the kinds leads from any source to any target at all, and when every source is itself a via component (both hold by construction, not by the model)
- Evaluate invariant and temporal claims on traces of the listed scenarios (state-invariant, precedes, eventually, never), stating that only those traces were checked; never report a vacuous trace check as verified: an implication whose antecedent is never true, a never whose event cannot occur, a precedes whose then-event never happens and traces with zero steps are inconclusive; an eventually whose occurrence is only the scenario's own scripted input is inconclusive, because the input is not a reaction of the system
- Derive the status of external-evidence claims only from evidence entities and recorded execution results
- Report a claim whose method is a verification method declared by the specification with evaluator external:<method>: Exegra runs nothing and the status comes only from evidence
- Check a trace step by step for the simulator panel (checkTrace)
- Add the automatic contract-conformance checks of a trace to the checks of checkTrace (src/core/contract-trace.js), as failed checks with the step
- Evaluate data-model claims (method data-model, a list of rules) on the records and copies of the model: violated with the first finding as counterexample, verified when every listed rule had something to check and found nothing, inconclusive when a listed rule had nothing to check
- Run every declared negative of a trace claim (its scenario with the scenario's own faults plus the negative's) and report the claim verified only when it holds on its scenarios and each negative, at least one, violates it; a trace claim with no negative, or with a negative that does not definitely violate it, is inconclusive and the explanation says which
- Evaluate refines claims (src/core/refinement.js): in the trace of each scenario that runs the implementation behavior, check that its initial configurations and every transition it fires map through its refinement mapping to the transition its model would fire (from, to, event, guard true and first in declaration order, effects giving the mapped post-variables, the same emitted events) or to a stutter that changes no mapped state or variable and emits nothing; violated at the first failing step with the steps up to it as a trace and a reason; inconclusive when a run ends in an error, a model expression reads what the mapping does not define, or no step matched a model transition
- In refines claims, tell a transition's queued entries apart into emits and timers by its effects, compare timers only with a model transition that sets timers, and read other behaviors named by model expressions from the same trace configuration, through the machine that refines them when they do not run themselves

#### Ports

| Name | Id | Direction | Contract | Protocol |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Evaluate claims | claims-evaluate-in | provides | Evaluate claims | in-process call |

#### Relationships out

- Reads → Claim method registry: Dispatches only on registered methods; an unregistered method stays not evaluated.
- Calls → Model queries: Builds the component graph and selects components for structural claims.
- Calls → Expression language: Evaluates claim expressions against trace configurations.
- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs the listed scenarios for invariant and temporal claims.
- Calls → Refinement checker: Refinement checks are claims.
- Calls → Exhaustive explorer: Claims evaluated exhaustively within bounds (planned, WG-4650).
- Depends on → Fault model and environment: States the fault model in each claim result's scope.
- Calls → Implementation refinement checker: Refinement of implementation traces is a claim (planned, WG-4679).
- Calls → Proof export: Claims proved in Lean are reported as verified by proof (planned, WG-4686).
- Calls → Contract conformance: checkTrace adds the automatic checks of the contract events of a trace.
- Calls → Data model: Evaluates data-model claims from the findings of the data rules.
- Calls → Metric claims: Metric-bound claims are evaluated by the metric claims component (planned, WG-4715).
- Calls → Simulation contract: Derives the engine input of a scenario from the model before a trace claim runs it.
- Calls → Semantic hash binding: Stamps every result with the semantic hash it was computed from.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application session: Re-evaluates claims after a semantic change and checks the simulator's trace.
- Calls → Agent command line: Evaluates claims for claims, evaluate, trace and verify.
- Calls → Specification checker: Prints the status of every claim.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise claim evaluation.

#### Contracts bound

- Evaluate claims (`evaluate-claims`): provides, via `claims-evaluate-in`
- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): consumer, via `claim-evaluator-calls-simulation-engine`
- Evaluate claims (`evaluate-claims`): provider, via `session-calls-claim-evaluator`
- Evaluate claims (`evaluate-claims`): provider, via `agent-cli-calls-claim-evaluator`

#### Known unknowns

- Behavioral results hold for the listed scenario traces only; there is no exhaustive state-space exploration and no probabilistic evaluation.
- Claim evaluation runs on the caller's thread. In the application session.evaluate runs it on the main thread inside every semantic commit, and trace and metric claims run engine scenarios there (src/core/claims.js), unlike the Simulation tab, which runs its scenario in the engine worker. The cost on large models is not measured (WG-4775, finding F06).
- Refines claims are checked on the deterministic trace of each scenario with its own faults, not over every delivery order and fault subset the explorer enumerates: the explorer returns state-invariant verdicts, not steps (WG-4852).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/claims.js`, `src/core/concordance.js`, `src/core/refinement.js`

### Simulation engine

Executes the state machines of a scenario as a deterministic discrete-event simulation over logical time.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Order events by logical time and scheduling sequence, deliver each to every behavior of the scenario
- Fire at most one transition per behavior and event, evaluating guards against the configuration at the start of the step
- Apply faults (drop, delay, enable or disable a transition, inject an event; in format 2 also duplicate, lose and retry an event as the generated runtime would) and record them in the trace
- End a run as quiescent, step-limit (default 1000) or error
- Run as one engine everywhere: the Lean core (formal/), called as engine.run through the Lean call boundary and loaded only through src/core/engine.js in the browser and in Node (the Simulation tab runs it in a Web Worker so a long run never blocks the page), and as the native binary exegra-lean compiled from the same C

#### Ports

| Name | Id | Direction | Contract | Protocol |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Run scenario | simulation-run-in | provides | Run a scenario | in-process call |

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Simulation aspect registry: Runs the hooks of the aspects a specification enables (planned, WG-4665).
- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): The engine is checked against the formal semantics, which defines what it must compute (planned, WG-4685).
- Calls → Resource model: Settles resources after each step and runs service-done and wait-timeout entries (planned, WG-4664, WG-4715).
- Calls → Workload model: Queues the next arrival of a workload when one is taken, runs link transfers and timers (planned, WG-4715).
- Calls → Performance metrics: Accumulates the measured outputs of a run, in trace and metrics output modes (planned, WG-4715).
- Calls → Lean call boundary: Runs every engine input as the Lean function engine.run.
- Calls → Simulation contract: Takes the engine input that simulation-contract derives (sim-input.js) and checks the input before it runs.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Claim evaluator: Runs the listed scenarios for invariant and temporal claims.
- Calls → Application session: Runs the loaded scenario in the engine worker and steps through the finished trace with a cursor.
- Calls → Agent command line: Runs scenarios for simulate and trace.
- Calls → Specification checker: Runs every scenario and optionally prints a trace.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the simulation.
- Calls → Refinement checker: Runs the abstract and the refined behaviors of a component (planned, WG-4666).
- Calls → Exhaustive explorer: A Rust explorer with the same semantics as simulation, for state spaces too large for the Lean one, checked differentially against it (planned, WG-4650).
- Calls → Metric claims: Runs the claim's scenarios once per seed in metrics mode (planned, WG-4715).
- Observes → Traffic recorder: src/app/engine-runner.js records engine-run-posted, worker-run-done, engine-run-returned or engine-run-refused, worker-terminated and worker-load-failed through the record callback the session passes to createEngineRunner, so the engine runner imports nothing of the application.
- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: The composition root initialises the Lean engine (initEngine) before the first project opens.
- Calls → Machine coverage: Runs each scenario of a shipped machine, and each searched sequence, through runEngine.
- Calls → Claim strength: Runs the one scenario of a counter bound to decide restated-scenario.

#### Contracts bound

- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): provides, via `simulation-run-in`
- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): provider, via `claim-evaluator-calls-simulation-engine`
- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): provider, via `session-calls-simulation-engine`
- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): provider, via `agent-cli-calls-simulation-engine`
- Trace, format version 2 (`trace-v2`): consumer, via `simulation-engine-calls-performance-metrics`
- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): provider, via `metric-claims-calls-simulation-engine`

#### Known unknowns

- Format 2 (integer time units, instances, routes, resources and resource faults, workloads, links, timers, request classes and seeded randomness) runs in the Lean engine (formal/Exegra/Format2.lean, Api/Engine.lean); the Rust engine implements the same format as the differential oracle. crash-instance, meters and the cost metric are not built yet (planned: WG-4741).
- One configuration copy per step dominates the cost of large format-2 runs (about 10 to 40 us per step); the queueing fixtures (about 7 and 15 minutes) are gated by EXEGRA_FIXTURES=1 (planned: WG-4741).
- Behaviors are flat: a component cannot yet be simulated at a chosen depth, as its abstract behavior or as its children (planned: WG-4666).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/engine.js`, `src/app/engine-worker.js`, `src/app/engine-runner.js`
- Notes: engine/ holds the library and native binary (exegra-engine) and the WebAssembly crate (exegra-engine-wasm). npm run build:engine (tools/build-engine.js) compiles the WebAssembly crate and binds it with wasm-bindgen into src/engine/pkg/, which is generated, not committed, and left out of the traceability surface. src/core/engine.js is the only module that loads an engine, and it loads the Lean core through the call boundary (src/core/lean.js); the Rust engine in src/engine/pkg/ is loaded only by tools/rust-engine.js, the differential oracle. src/app/engine-runner.js and src/app/engine-worker.js run the Simulation tab's runs off the main thread.

### Projection system

Derives a flat scene (nodes, edges, legend, notes) from the model for a declared view with one of six projections (structural, claims, behavior, references, data, reader); a view is never a separate dataset and every node and edge is backed by a real entity.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Visualization (`visualization`)

#### Responsibilities

- List the views declared in the specification, plus an implicit view for each projection type without a declared view
- Project the structural, claims and behavior projections, applying view filters (categories, relationship kinds, within, tags) and display options
- Project references: the focused entity (the session's selection) and every entity within display.depth reference hops (1 to 3, in either direction, any entity kind, declared kinds included), at most 150 nodes with a note naming what was left out; filter.entityKinds restricts the kinds drawn, and without a focus it draws every entity of those kinds when there are at most 300, otherwise only a note asking for a selection
- Handle collapsed subsystems (lifted edges), hidden entities, isolation, claim focus, scenario focus and the current simulation step
- Take node shapes, colours and line styles from the effective vocabulary of the model; entities of declared kinds take the shape and colour their kind declares
- Derive the message flow of a simulation step (src/core/flows.js): the sender is the subject of the behavior whose transition emitted the event, or outside for a scenario stimulus or an injected fault; the receivers are the subjects of the behaviors that fired on it; the path follows the scene edge between their drawn nodes, and is marked unshown when only a relationship the view does not draw connects them, or undeclared when none does
- Project the Data view when the model declares records or copies: records, copies and the components that own or hold them, with edges from of, heldBy and owner; copies with data-rule findings are marked violated
- Register the reader projection, which draws no scene: its views are read on the reader surface

#### Ports

| Name | Id | Direction | Contract | Protocol |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Project scene | projection-scene-in | provides | Project a model into a scene | in-process call |

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Model queries: Resolves components, ancestors and descendants for projection.
- Projects onto → Three.js scene renderer: Derives the scene that the renderer draws; the renderer never reads the model.
- Calls → Data model: The Data projection draws records, copies, owners and holders, and marks copies with findings.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application session: Lists views and projects the active view with the interaction context.
- Calls → System explorer: Imports refLabel to label entities that carry a reference.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise projections.

#### Contracts bound

- Project a model into a scene (`project-scene`): provides, via `projection-scene-in`
- Project a model into a scene (`project-scene`): consumer, via `projection-system-projects-scene-renderer`
- Project a model into a scene (`project-scene`): provider, via `session-calls-projection-system`

#### Known unknowns

- The cost of projection on large models has no recorded measurement; the benchmark specification exists for that purpose but no result is recorded.
- The references projection draws nothing but a note until an entity is selected, unless the view filters to entity kinds.

#### Implementation

- Status: model-configured
- Modules: `src/core/projections.js`, `src/core/flows.js`
- Notes: Views are declared in the specification and shapes, colours and line styles come from the model's vocabulary; the projection code is hand-written.

### Layout engine

Computes deterministic layouts of a scene on the ground plane by one of four methods, recursively over containers, honouring pinned leaf positions, and can choose the method by scoring the results.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Visualization (`visualization`)

#### Responsibilities

- Offer the methods layered (dependency layers left to right), layered-down (front to back), packed (no flow direction, connected siblings in tight rows) and radial (best-connected node at the centre, rings around it); each arranges the siblings of one container level and the shared recursion encloses them in their container with padding
- Place pinned leaf nodes at their absolute [x, z] pin and keep their container enclosing them
- Tolerate cycles, self edges and edges across containers
- Shape the result towards a target width-to-depth ratio supplied by the caller (default 1.6)
- chooseLayout (method auto): compute every method, score each placement (aspect against the target, mean edge length, edge crossings, whitespace area, fixed weights) and return the lowest score, ties going to the earlier method, together with every score

#### Relationships out

- Projects onto → Three.js scene renderer: Derives the placement of the scene's nodes on the ground plane.

#### Relationships in

- Data flow → Application session: The projected scene, the pins of the active view, the requested method (auto or one of the four) and the viewport's width-to-height ratio travel to the layout engine; the placement, the method used and, for auto, the scores come back as the return value.
- Calls → Application session: Lays the scene out with the pins of the active view from the layout file: chooseLayout with the viewport's aspect when the view's layout is auto, layoutScene with the chosen method otherwise.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the layout.

#### Known unknowns

- Pins are absolute positions; nothing re-anchors them when the model changes.
- The score weights are fixed constants chosen by hand; no recorded measurement shows that the lowest score is the most readable layout. Crossings are counted on at most 400 sampled edges.
- Automatic layout computes all four methods on every recompute; its cost on large scenes has no recorded measurement.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/layout.js`, `src/core/layout/constants.js`, `src/core/layout/model.js`, `src/core/layout/solve.js`, `src/core/layout/layered.js`, `src/core/layout/packed.js`, `src/core/layout/radial.js`, `src/core/layout/score.js`
- Notes: layout.js is the entry (layoutScene, chooseLayout, LAYOUT_METHODS, scorePlacement); the other modules hold the shared recursion (solve.js, model.js), the four arrangers, the score and the shared sizes.

### Three.js scene renderer

Draws a SceneModel and its placement in 3D with Three.js, as a function of (scene, placement, selection, highlight, camera) with no domain state of its own.

- Category: Computational service
- Parent: Visualization (`visualization`)

#### Responsibilities

- Diff the scene incrementally by node and edge id and animate placement changes
- Draw node shapes, container slabs, edge curves with line styles, status colours and CSS2D labels with clutter management; the label layer is its own stacking context, so a label (even a message in flight, lifted above the other labels) never covers the interface around the scene
- Run a requestAnimationFrame loop that renders only when something changed, and report frame statistics
- Detect picking, hover, double click, dragging of leaf nodes and connect-mode clicks and report them through callbacks; it never edits the model. Touch: a tap allows 10 px of movement, a second finger makes the gesture a pinch or pan with no tap, and a node is dragged only after the finger has rested on it for 350 ms (a swipe that starts on a node orbits the camera)
- Animate a simulation step's message: a marker labelled with the event travels from the sender's node along the drawn edge to each receiver (backwards along it for a reply), drops in from above for a request from outside, travels straight for a relationship the view does not draw, and travels straight in the violation colour when no relationship is declared; only the current step's message is in flight, so stepping quickly or playing never piles markers up

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Three.js: Builds geometry, materials, lines and CSS2D labels with Three.js.
- Depends on → Web browser platform: Uses the canvas, WebGL, pointer events, ResizeObserver and requestAnimationFrame.
- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Invokes the onSelect, onHover, onActivate, onMoveEnd and onConnect callbacks supplied when it was created; it imports nothing from the application.

#### Relationships in

- Projects onto → Projection system: Derives the scene that the renderer draws; the renderer never reads the model.
- Projects onto → Layout engine: Derives the placement of the scene's nodes on the ground plane.
- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Creates the scene view and calls setScene, setSelection, setHighlight, setMode, setCameraMode, fit, pulse and the camera accessors.
- Observes → Viewport overlay: Shows the renderer's frame rate, frame time, draw calls, triangles and counts through the statistics provider.
- Projects onto → Simulation overlays: Overlay values drawn over the component view.

#### Contracts bound

- Project a model into a scene (`project-scene`): provider, via `projection-system-projects-scene-renderer`

#### Known unknowns

- No frame-rate or load-time measurement is recorded for the 396-component benchmark specification.
- Pointer, drag and connect handling live inside src/render/scene-view.js, which this file assigns to the renderer; the split between rendering and interaction is not a module boundary (see visual-interaction).

#### Implementation

- Status: model-configured
- Modules: `src/render/scene-view.js`, `src/render/geometry.js`, `src/render/curves.js`, `src/render/labels.js`, `src/render/palette.js`
- Notes: Node shapes, colours and line styles arrive in the scene from the model's vocabulary and are mapped to geometry and a palette; the renderer code is hand-written.

### Visual interaction and application entry

Turns gestures on the 3D scene into session actions and typed operations, rigs the camera and wires the session to the renderer and to every panel at start-up.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Visualization (`visualization`)

#### Responsibilities

- Perspective and top-down orthographic cameras with orbit controls, mode switching and animated fit
- Route renderer callbacks to the session: select and hover, activate (collapse a subsystem or fit), move (MovePresentationNode operations) and connect (open the connect dialog)
- Push every new scene, placement, selection and highlight from the session to the renderer, and pulse simulation steps
- Create the session, mount the shell and the panels, remember the camera per project and view in sessionStorage, report the scene viewport's width-to-height ratio to the session (after resizes settle) for the automatic layout, and expose window.__exegra for tests

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Three.js scene renderer: Creates the scene view and calls setScene, setSelection, setHighlight, setMode, setCameraMode, fit, pulse and the camera accessors.
- Depends on → Three.js: The camera rig builds cameras and OrbitControls.
- Depends on → Web browser platform: Listens for page events and schedules fits with requestAnimationFrame.
- Calls → Application session: Selects, hovers, toggles collapse, requests fits, sets mode, starts the session and reports the scene viewport's width-to-height ratio with setViewportAspect for the automatic layout.
- Subscribes → Application session: Listens for scene, selection, ui, project, view, fit and sim events to update the renderer.
- Submits edits to → Transaction system: A drag becomes MovePresentationNode operations submitted through session.apply.
- Calls → Edit dialogs: Opens the connect dialog after two successive node clicks in connect mode.
- Calls → Application shell: Mounts the shell, uses its host elements, shows destinations through it and has a tap in the scene bring the inspector forward.
- Calls → Semantic inspector: Mounts the inspector.
- Calls → Source editor: Mounts the source editor.
- Calls → Simulator panel: Mounts the simulator panel.
- Calls → Verification panel: Mounts the verification panel.
- Calls → Realisation panel: Mounts the realisation panel.
- Writes → Browser web storage: Remembers the camera per project and view in sessionStorage.
- Calls → Traffic recorder: src/app/main.js records page-loaded, the engine load, session-init-requested, camera events and step-pulsed.
- Calls → Reader surface: Mounts the reader surface.
- Calls → Persistence client: The composition root (src/app/main.js) constructs the persistence client and hands it to the session.
- Calls → Simulation engine: The composition root initialises the Lean engine (initEngine) before the first project opens.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Three.js scene renderer: Invokes the onSelect, onHover, onActivate, onMoveEnd and onConnect callbacks supplied when it was created; it imports nothing from the application.

#### Contracts bound

- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): consumer, via `visual-interaction-submits-edits-transactions`

#### Known unknowns

- This component holds the camera rig and the application entry (src/app/main.js) because traceability assigns each file to one component; the picking, drag and connect handling it relies on is in src/render/scene-view.js, claimed by scene-renderer.
- main.js is also the composition root: it mounts the panels, so this component has dependencies on them that have nothing to do with 3D interaction.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/app/main.js`, `src/render/camera-rig.js`

### Application session

The one object the UI talks to: it owns the project, the interaction state and everything derived from the model, and saves committed text asynchronously.

- Category: Computational service
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Open, list and switch projects: the new project is read first and the switch is refused while a save is pending, in conflict or in error; load shipped specs when persistence is unavailable
- Forward edits to the transaction system and log rejected edits
- On every commit: re-evaluate claims when the semantics changed, re-project (the selection is the focus of the references projection) and re-lay-out the scene, then notify subscribers (scene, model, claims, selection)
- Choose the layout of the active view: the viewer's preference for that view (setLayoutMethod, remembered in localStorage), else the view's display.layout, else auto; auto asks chooseLayout with the viewport's width-to-height ratio reported through setViewportAspect (clamped to 0.5 to 3.5, small changes ignored), the other choices ask layoutScene for that method; the pins of the view apply
- Save committed spec and layout text asynchronously with the base hash of the last read or write, report saving, saved, conflict, error or read-only, and resolve a conflict by reload or overwrite
- Watch the project files and adopt an external change with Project.reload: any text that parses is adopted, including text with validation errors; the adoption clears the undo and redo history and is not undoable; text with syntax errors is refused and the last valid model is kept
- Hold selection, hover, active view, collapsed, hidden and isolated sets, simulation state and the change log, and remember UI state in localStorage
- Derive the current simulation step's message flow and its path through the current scene, for the renderer and the simulator panel
- Run the loaded scenario off the main thread (engine worker) whenever it is loaded or its faults change, show it as running until the trace arrives, discard a run that a newer load replaced, and step, step back, seek, reset, play and run to the end through the finished trace with a cursor that reveals one recorded step at a time
- Record the session's real events in the traffic recorder (and install the persistence client's observer), and in Live mode replay the kept stream through the scenario live-session (src/core/replay.js) as events arrive, batched per microtask: the cursor follows the newest step unless the user stepped back, checks are checkTrace plus the replay's divergences, and a semantic commit replays the stream through the new model
- Re-derive the model from the product layers of a fresh read of the project when the watcher reports a changed, added or removed product file (src/app/disk-reload.js), keeping the platform text and layout in memory

#### Ports

| Name | Id | Direction | Contract | Protocol |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Apply transaction | session-apply-out | requires | Apply a transaction | in-process call |
| Write file | session-write-out | requires | Write a project file | persistence client call |
| Evaluate claims | session-claims-out | requires | Evaluate claims | in-process call |
| Project scene | session-project-out | requires | Project a model into a scene | in-process call |
| Run scenario | session-run-out | requires | Run a scenario | in-process call |

#### Relationships out

- Data flow → Layout engine: The projected scene, the pins of the active view, the requested method (auto or one of the four) and the viewport's width-to-height ratio travel to the layout engine; the placement, the method used and, for auto, the scores come back as the return value.
- Calls → Transaction system: Creates a Project per opened project and forwards apply, undo, redo and reload.
- Reads → Transaction system: Reads the committed model, diagnostics, texts and revision from the project; the UI reads them through the session.
- Subscribes → Transaction system: Registers its commit listener with Project.subscribe; the project knows it only as a callback.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Re-evaluates claims after a semantic change and checks the simulator's trace.
- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs the loaded scenario in the engine worker and steps through the finished trace with a cursor.
- Calls → Projection system: Lists views and projects the active view with the interaction context.
- Calls → Layout engine: Lays the scene out with the pins of the active view from the layout file: chooseLayout with the viewport's aspect when the view's layout is auto, layoutScene with the chosen method otherwise.
- Calls → Persistence client: Lists and reads projects, writes spec and layout text after commits and watches for external changes.
- Data flow → Persistence client: Committed spec and layout text and the base hash of the last read or write travel to the persistence client, after the commit and asynchronously.
- Writes → Browser web storage: Remembers view, selection, collapsed, hidden, isolate, camera mode, focus and the layout method chosen per view, per project, in localStorage.
- Calls → Simulation overlays: Derives the overlays of the scrubbed time (planned, WG-4671).
- Calls → Simulation contract: Derives the engine input for the scenario the Simulation tab loads.
- Calls → Contract conformance: Derives the request log of the loaded run for the simulator panel.
- Calls → Traffic recorder: Records the session's events, installs the persistence client's observer and reads the stream for Live mode.
- Calls → Self-model replay: Live mode replays the recorded stream through the scenario live-session (createReplay, replayChecks).
- Calls → Stable serialization and text digests: Hashes the spec and layout text with sha256Hex for the recorded events.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Selects, hovers, toggles collapse, requests fits, sets mode, starts the session and reports the scene viewport's width-to-height ratio with setViewportAspect for the automatic layout.
- Subscribes → Visual interaction and application entry: Listens for scene, selection, ui, project, view, fit and sim events to update the renderer.
- Data flow → Transaction system: Each commit delivers its TransactionResult and history entry to the listener the session subscribed.
- Data flow → Persistence client: Project files, recorded results and change notifications arrive at the session.
- Calls → Application shell: Passes the session to the panels it mounts and calls select, fit, undo and redo.
- Subscribes → Application shell: Listens to session events to show toasts and keep the destination summaries current.
- Calls → Toolbar: Switches project and view, sets modes and the camera mode, fits, chooses the layout method of the view with setLayoutMethod (a viewer preference, not an edit), selects search results, undoes and redoes, resolves save conflicts.
- Subscribes → Toolbar: Updates each toolbar group from the session events it depends on.
- Calls → System explorer: Selects entities and toggles collapse, hidden and isolate marks.
- Subscribes → System explorer: Refreshes on model, selection and ui events.
- Calls → Diagnostics and change log panels: Reads diagnostics and the log; selects or reveals the entity of a row.
- Subscribes → Diagnostics and change log panels: Refreshes on model and log events.
- Calls → Viewport overlay: Reads the scene's legend and notes.
- Subscribes → Viewport overlay: Refreshes on scene events.
- Calls → Semantic inspector: Reads the selection, model and claim results, selects linked entities and reveals source.
- Subscribes → Semantic inspector: Re-renders on selection, project, model and claims events.
- Calls → Edit dialogs: Reads the model and applies the dialog's operation.
- Calls → Source editor: Reads the committed text and applies the draft.
- Subscribes → Source editor: Refreshes on model and project events and reveals declarations on request.
- Calls → Simulator panel: Loads, steps, plays, seeks and resets the session's simulation and selects entities.
- Subscribes → Simulator panel: Refreshes on sim, model and project events.
- Calls → Verification panel: Reads claim results and recorded results; selects claims and switches to the claims view.
- Subscribes → Verification panel: Refreshes on claims, model, project and selection events.
- Calls → Realisation panel: Reads the model, claim results and recorded traceability; selects components.
- Subscribes → Realisation panel: Refreshes on model, project and claims events.
- Calls → Reader surface: Selects entities; reads the model, the views, the current view and the scene's projection.
- Subscribes → Reader surface: Redraws on scene and project events; marks the selection on selection events.

#### Contracts bound

- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): requires, via `session-apply-out`
- Write a project file (`write-file`): requires, via `session-write-out`
- Evaluate claims (`evaluate-claims`): requires, via `session-claims-out`
- Project a model into a scene (`project-scene`): requires, via `session-project-out`
- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): requires, via `session-run-out`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): consumer, via `session-calls-transactions`
- Evaluate claims (`evaluate-claims`): consumer, via `session-calls-claim-evaluator`
- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): consumer, via `session-calls-simulation-engine`
- Project a model into a scene (`project-scene`): consumer, via `session-calls-projection-system`
- Write a project file (`write-file`): consumer, via `session-calls-persistence-client`

#### Known unknowns

- A failed save leaves the pending flag set and stops writing until the next commit; there is no automatic retry.
- The layout method chosen for a view is stored only in the browser's localStorage under the project and view: it is not in the specification or the layout file, is not shared and is lost when storage is cleared.
- The session writes the current text, not one write per revision; the edit-flow machines in this file abstract that.
- Claim re-evaluation and re-projection run synchronously inside every commit with no measurement of their cost on large models.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/app/session.js`, `src/app/disk-reload.js`

### Application shell

The page frame, mobile first: the full-screen scene, the app bar, the navigation and the two panels (explorer and context) that the responsive layout places as sheets, a side panel or columns; toasts and keyboard shortcuts.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Create the responsive layout; mount the app bar, explorer, diagnostics, changes, overlay and simulation transport, and hand the other panels their host elements
- Keep a one-line summary of every destination current, which is what a peeking sheet shows (for the inspector: the selection's name, kind and status)
- Show errors from the session log as toasts
- Undo and redo shortcuts, the delete shortcut for the selection, F to fit, / to search, Escape to leave connect mode or clear the selection

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Passes the session to the panels it mounts and calls select, fit, undo and redo.
- Subscribes → Application session: Listens to session events to show toasts and keep the destination summaries current.
- Calls → Toolbar: Mounts the toolbar.
- Calls → System explorer: Mounts the explorer.
- Calls → Diagnostics and change log panels: Mounts the diagnostics and changes panels.
- Calls → Viewport overlay: Mounts the viewport overlay with a provider of render statistics.
- Calls → Edit dialogs: The Delete shortcut opens the delete dialog for the selection.
- Submits edits to → Transaction system: Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z and Ctrl+Y call undo and redo on the project through the session.
- Depends on → Stylesheets: index.html loads the stylesheet entry point main.css.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.
- Calls → Responsive layout: Creates the responsive layout, which places the panels as sheets, a side panel or columns for the viewport and shows destinations on request.
- Calls → Model queries: Labels the selection in the inspector's summary line.
- Calls → Claim method registry: Names a selected claim's status in the inspector's summary line.
- Calls → Simulator panel: Mounts the simulation transport.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Mounts the shell, uses its host elements, shows destinations through it and has a tap in the scene bring the inspector forward.

#### Contracts bound

- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): consumer, via `shell-submits-edits-transactions`

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `index.html`, `src/ui/shell.js`

### Toolbar

The app bar: project and view switching, search, a status badge with validation counts and the save state, and an overflow menu holding the remaining actions (undo and redo, editing tools, layout, camera and fit, legend and render statistics, export and import) below desktop width; on a desktop the actions sit inline. It also builds the floating scene controls (fit, the camera pair and the connect-mode indicator). The simulation's quick controls live in the docked transport.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Switch project and view: below 1200 px through one title button (the project small over the view) that opens a picker sheet listing the views and the projects, on a desktop through two selects; search entities in a full-screen sheet on a phone or inline from 768 px, and select and frame the result
- Create and delete entities and switch select or connect mode; show a connect-mode indicator in the floating scene controls that also leaves the mode
- Fit and switch the camera mode from the floating scene controls; undo and redo float above the sheet's peek height on a phone and sit in the bar from 768 px
- Choose the layout method of the active view (auto or one of the session's methods) through session.setLayoutMethod; the choice is a viewer preference, not an edit, and the selector's tooltip shows the scores that auto compared; reset layout (MovePresentationNode with reset)
- Show validation in a status badge that opens Diagnostics: below 1200 px one dot coloured by the worst status and the total count, on a desktop the three counts. Show the save state as a badge on the overflow button and a line in the overflow menu below 1200 px, and as a small button on a desktop when it needs attention; a conflict opens the save-conflict menu: reload from disk or overwrite disk
- Open and close the legend and render statistics cards of the overlay
- Export a project bundle and import one, replacing the project by name; an import is refused when the bundle's spec.jsonc has syntax errors, and a bundle with validation errors is only noted

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Switches project and view, sets modes and the camera mode, fits, chooses the layout method of the view with setLayoutMethod (a viewer preference, not an edit), selects search results, undoes and redoes, resolves save conflicts.
- Subscribes → Application session: Updates each toolbar group from the session events it depends on.
- Calls → Model queries: Labels search results.
- Calls → Validator: Import analyzes the bundle's spec.jsonc with analyzeSpec and refuses it when it has syntax errors.
- Calls → Edit dialogs: Opens the create and delete dialogs.
- Submits edits to → Transaction system: Reset layout submits MovePresentationNode with reset; undo and redo go through the session.
- Calls → Persistence client: Export reads a project bundle and Import writes one.
- Data flow → Persistence client: An imported bundle (spec.jsonc, layout.jsonc, results.json text) is sent to the persistence service, bypassing the transaction system.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.
- Calls → Responsive layout: The overflow menu and the save-conflict menu use the shared menu controller.
- Calls → Stable serialization and text digests: Hashes the specification of an exported or imported bundle for the recorded events.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application shell: Mounts the toolbar.

#### Contracts bound

- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): consumer, via `toolbar-submits-edits-transactions`

#### Known unknowns

- Import writes the project's files through the persistence service without passing the transaction system, a validation gate or a base-hash check (analyzeSpec is run on the bundle first, but only syntax errors stop it); the session then opens whatever was written, with its validation errors.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/toolbar.js`

### System explorer

A filterable tree of components and flat lists of every other entity kind, including kinds a specification declares, built to stay usable on models with thousands of entities, with selection, collapse, hide and isolate marks. It is a bottom sheet on a phone, a side panel from 768 px and a column of its own from 1200 px; rows and row actions are touch targets, and the selected row keeps its actions visible, so nothing depends on hover.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Show one section per entity kind present: the built-in kinds first, then the kinds the specification declares
- Build rows lazily: a closed section or group renders nothing until it is opened, and sections and groups with more than 60 matching rows start closed
- Render at most 200 rows per list and say how many matching rows are left out; narrow with a text filter (id, reference, name, statement, ends of relationships) and with component category chips
- Group claims by their area property when any claim declares one
- Select entities and toggle collapse, hide and isolate marks without rebuilding the rows

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Selects entities and toggles collapse, hidden and isolate marks.
- Subscribes → Application session: Refreshes on model, selection and ui events.
- Calls → Model queries: Labels entities and finds ancestors.
- Calls → Projection system: Imports refLabel to label entities that carry a reference.
- Reads → Claim method registry: Reads claim status labels.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application shell: Mounts the explorer.

#### Known unknowns

- Rows beyond the 200-row window of a list can only be reached by narrowing the filter; there is no paging.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/explorer.js`

### Diagnostics and change log panels

Show the current diagnostics (errors, warnings, gaps) and the session's change log of commits, saves and rejected edits. In a panel narrower than 640 px a diagnostic is a two-line row; from there up it is one line.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Reads diagnostics and the log; selects or reveals the entity of a row.
- Subscribes → Application session: Refreshes on model and log events.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application shell: Mounts the diagnostics and changes panels.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/activity.js`, `src/ui/detail.js`
- Notes: detail.js turns a log entry's detail (a string, or a rejected transaction's code and diagnostics) into text and is used by these panels and by the shell's toasts.

### Viewport overlay

Shows the active view's name and notes, an illustrative-example mark, the legend and the renderer's statistics over the 3D viewport. On a phone the legend and the statistics are not drawn until opened from the app bar's overflow menu, so they never cover the scene uninvited; from 768 px a closed card shows its summary, the legend starts open when it is short and the statistics start open.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Reads the scene's legend and notes.
- Subscribes → Application session: Refreshes on scene events.
- Observes → Three.js scene renderer: Shows the renderer's frame rate, frame time, draw calls, triangles and counts through the statistics provider.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application shell: Mounts the viewport overlay with a provider of render statistics.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/overlay.js`

### Semantic inspector

Shows the selected entity: model-derived properties editable through the transaction system, relationships, dependencies, contracts, claim results and referencing entities. It is the destination a tap in the scene opens, at peek on a phone.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Generate the property editor from the effective meta-model, so a property added through the vocabulary gets a working control
- Commit every property change as a SetProperty operation and show a rejection under the control
- Show claim evaluation results with scope, counterexample, assumptions and evidence, and open a counterexample in the simulator
- In Identity, show the id, a Reference row when the entity carries a ref and an Origin row when it carries a source, and a Rename id button that opens the rename dialog
- Offer connect, create-inside and delete actions through dialogs
- Place labels above their controls in a panel narrower than 420 px and beside them from there up; its small tables (states, transitions, scenario steps) are card lists in a narrow panel
- For a record, list its fields (type, personal class, reference, role) and its copies; for a copy, list its source records and the fields it holds, with personal and principal fields marked

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Reads the selection, model and claim results, selects linked entities and reveals source.
- Subscribes → Application session: Re-renders on selection, project, model and claims events.
- Calls → Model queries: Lists relationships, dependencies, contracts, children and references of the selection.
- Reads → Claim method registry: Reads claim status labels and tones.
- Calls → Property controls: Renders one control per meta-model property definition.
- Calls → Edit dialogs: Opens connect, create and delete dialogs.
- Submits edits to → Transaction system: Every property change is a SetProperty operation submitted through session.apply.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.
- Calls → Data model: Lists a record's fields and copies and a copy's source fields.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Mounts the inspector.

#### Contracts bound

- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): consumer, via `inspector-submits-edits-transactions`

#### Implementation

- Status: model-configured
- Modules: `src/ui/inspector.js`
- Notes: The property editor is generated from the effective meta-model by property-controls; the read-only sections are hand-written queries.

### Property controls

One generic form control per meta-model property definition, with no knowledge of any particular entity kind.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Render string, text, boolean, number, enum, reference, list, object and JSON-valued properties from their definitions
- Report rejected changes with their diagnostics under the control
- Render the draft fields of the create dialogs

#### Relationships out

- Reads → Claim method registry: Describes claim evaluation methods in the evaluation editor.
- Calls → Model queries: Labels referenced entities and finds declaration sites.
- Calls → Edit dialogs: Opens the rename, delete and nested-entity dialogs from a property control.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Semantic inspector: Renders one control per meta-model property definition.
- Calls → Edit dialogs: Renders draft fields and rejections.

#### Known unknowns

- src/ui/property-controls.js and src/ui/dialogs.js import each other.

#### Implementation

- Status: model-configured
- Modules: `src/ui/property-controls.js`
- Notes: Controls are derived from the effective meta-model at runtime; the control code is hand-written.

### Edit dialogs

Modal dialogs for the editing operations: create a component or any other entity, connect two components, delete with a cascade preview, rename an id. Full-screen sheets on a phone, centred cards from 768 px.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Check ids against the id pattern and for duplicates before submitting
- Preview what a delete removes and repairs using planDelete, and require cascade when other entities refer to the target
- Submit CreateEntity, CreateRelationship, DeleteEntity and RenameEntity through the session and show a rejection inside the dialog

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Reads the model and applies the dialog's operation.
- Calls → Model queries: Labels entities and finds references.
- Calls → Transaction system: Imports planDelete to preview what a delete removes and repairs.
- Reads → Meta-model: Reads the id pattern and checks ids with it.
- Calls → Property controls: Renders draft fields and rejections.
- Submits edits to → Transaction system: Submits CreateEntity, CreateRelationship, DeleteEntity and RenameEntity through session.apply.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Opens the connect dialog after two successive node clicks in connect mode.
- Calls → Application shell: The Delete shortcut opens the delete dialog for the selection.
- Calls → Toolbar: Opens the create and delete dialogs.
- Calls → Semantic inspector: Opens connect, create and delete dialogs.
- Calls → Property controls: Opens the rename, delete and nested-entity dialogs from a property control.

#### Contracts bound

- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): consumer, via `dialogs-submits-edits-transactions`

#### Known unknowns

- src/ui/dialogs.js and src/ui/property-controls.js import each other.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/dialogs.js`
- Notes: The create-any-entity dialog is generated from the meta-model; the other dialogs are hand-written.

### Source editor

A textarea over the committed specification text that keeps whatever the user types as a draft until the model accepts it as a ReplaceSource transaction.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Show the committed text and stay in sync with every commit made elsewhere
- Analyze the draft after a short pause and show its diagnostics; mark error and warning lines in the gutter
- Apply the draft through the transaction system; if it is rejected, keep the draft exactly as typed and show the diagnostics
- Warn when the model changed since the draft was started, and reveal the declaration of an entity selected elsewhere
- Take the full height of its panel (the full sheet on a phone); only the editor scrolls horizontally

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Reads the committed text and applies the draft.
- Subscribes → Application session: Refreshes on model and project events and reveals declarations on request.
- Calls → Validator: Analyzes the draft with analyzeSpec for its diagnostics.
- Calls → Parser: Parses the draft to find the location of an entity to reveal.
- Reads → Transaction system: Reads the committed spec text and revision from the project.
- Submits edits to → Transaction system: Applying a draft submits a ReplaceSource operation through session.apply.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Mounts the source editor.

#### Contracts bound

- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): consumer, via `source-editor-submits-edits-transactions`

#### Known unknowns

- It is a plain textarea: no syntax highlighting, completion or incremental analysis; the whole draft is analyzed on every pause.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/source-editor.js`

### Simulator panel

Select a scenario and its faults, step, play, rewind and seek through its trace, and show the step, the transitions that fired, ignored events and the claim checks of the trace so far, and the message of the step: who sent its event to whom, and whether a declared relationship carries it. A docked transport (step back, play or pause, step, the step counter and run to end) stays visible while a scenario is loaded, above the navigation on a phone and floating over the scene from 768 px, so the messages can be watched in the scene while stepping. In a panel narrower than 900 px its sections are one stack ordered for stepping: the current step (message, delivered event, fired transitions, violations), the timeline as a bounded scrolling list, the checks, then the run settings (scenario, faults, controls) with the scenario's description and behaviors collapsed under About this scenario; with no scenario loaded the scenario picker comes first. From 900 px they are three columns. From 768 px the floating transport hides while the panel itself is open. When the scenario has contract events, a Requests section lists them as a request log, one jump target per line, dimmed while still to come.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Offer a Live source next to the scenario select (session.simLive): the status line counts real events, divergences and awaited predictions; the timeline lists recorded events with logical and display-only wall-clock time, marking inferred events and divergences; a Divergences section lists every divergence with a jump to its step; the step column shows calibrations and divergences; at every size, with 44 px source buttons

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Loads, steps, plays, seeks and resets the session's simulation and selects entities.
- Subscribes → Application session: Refreshes on sim, model and project events.
- Calls → Model queries: Labels entities.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Mounts the simulator panel.
- Calls → Application shell: Mounts the simulation transport.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/sim-panel.js`, `src/ui/sim-transport.js`
- Notes: Holds no semantic state; the panel and the transport drive the session's simulation functions.

### Verification panel

Inventory of claims with their evaluated status, scope, counterexample, evidence and assumptions, with status, kind, method and text filters, built to stay usable on models with thousands of claims.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Count claims by status and show each claim's status, kind, evaluation method, scope and counterexample
- Filter by status, claim kind, evaluation method (named by the specification's verification methods where it declares them) and text
- Render at most 200 rows and say how many matching claims are not shown
- Show, when the specification declares verification methods, a line saying that claims verified by them are checked by tools outside Exegra and are not evaluated without recorded evidence
- Show the claims as a list of cards in a panel narrower than 640 px and as a table from there up

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Reads claim results and recorded results; selects claims and switches to the claims view.
- Subscribes → Application session: Refreshes on claims, model, project and selection events.
- Calls → Model queries: Labels claims.
- Reads → Claim method registry: Reads claim status labels and tones.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Mounts the verification panel.

#### Known unknowns

- It shows recorded results loaded with the project and cannot execute tests; execution happens only through `exegra verify`.
- Rows beyond the 200-row window can only be reached by filtering; there is no paging.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/verification-panel.js`

### Realisation panel

Shows how each component is realised, the bootstrap boundary, model and implementation discrepancies from the recorded traceability result, and generates artifacts from the model.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Summarize and filter components by declared realisation status; show per-component claim counts
- Show declared modules missing on disk and source files no component claims, from the traceability result recorded by `exegra verify`
- Generate the five artifacts in the browser and, under the development server, write them to the project's generated directory
- Show the components as a list of cards in a panel narrower than 640 px and as a table from there up

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Application session: Reads the model, claim results and recorded traceability; selects components.
- Subscribes → Application session: Refreshes on model, project and claims events.
- Calls → Model queries: Labels components.
- Calls → Artifact generators: Generates the five artifacts from the session's model and results in the browser.
- Calls → Persistence client: Writes generated artifacts to the project's generated directory (development server only).
- Data flow → Persistence client: The generated files' text travels to the persistence service; the specification is not part of it.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.
- Calls → Stable serialization and text digests: Hashes the generated files for the recorded events.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Mounts the realisation panel.

#### Known unknowns

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
