### Stylesheets

Layered CSS written for a phone first and widened with min-width queries: tokens (colours, type scale, the breakpoints, the 44 px touch target and the shell sizes), reset, base, layout (where the shell's parts sit at each size), panels (sheets, navigation, menus and the tables that become cards), touch targets in the states layer, and one file per component group.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Relationships in

- Depends on → Application shell: index.html loads the stylesheet entry point main.css.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/styles/activity.css`, `src/styles/base.css`, `src/styles/dialogs.css`, `src/styles/explorer.css`, `src/styles/inspector.css`, `src/styles/layout.css`, `src/styles/main.css`, `src/styles/overlay.css`, `src/styles/panels.css`, `src/styles/reader.css`, `src/styles/realisation.css`, `src/styles/reset.css`, `src/styles/scene.css`, `src/styles/sim.css`, `src/styles/source-editor.css`, `src/styles/states.css`, `src/styles/tokens.css`, `src/styles/toolbar.css`, `src/styles/utilities.css`, `src/styles/verification.css`

### Persistence client

The browser side of the persistence boundary: HTTP requests to the development-server middleware and a server-sent-events subscription to file changes.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Persistence (`persistence`)

#### Responsibilities

- List, read, write (with base hash), export and import projects and write generated artifacts
- Report unavailable persistence instead of pretending to save when the application is not served by the development server
- Subscribe to spec, layout and recorded-result changes of a project

#### Ports

| Name | Id | Direction | Contract | Protocol |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Write file | client-write-out | requires | Write a project file | HTTP POST /__exegra/write |

#### Relationships out

- Data flow → Application session: Project files, recorded results and change notifications arrive at the session.
- Calls → Development-server plugin: Sends HTTP requests to /__exegra/* and opens the events stream.
- Data flow → Development-server plugin: Spec and layout text with the base hash, generated files and import bundles travel in request bodies.
- Depends on → Web browser platform: Uses fetch and EventSource.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application session: Lists and reads projects, writes spec and layout text after commits and watches for external changes.
- Data flow → Application session: Committed spec and layout text and the base hash of the last read or write travel to the persistence client, after the commit and asynchronously.
- Calls → Toolbar: Export reads a project bundle and Import writes one.
- Data flow → Toolbar: An imported bundle (spec.jsonc, layout.jsonc, results.json text) is sent to the persistence service, bypassing the transaction system.
- Calls → Realisation panel: Writes generated artifacts to the project's generated directory (development server only).
- Data flow → Realisation panel: The generated files' text travels to the persistence service; the specification is not part of it.
- Data flow → Development-server plugin: Responses (file texts and hashes, write results, conflicts) and change events return to the client.
- Observes → Traffic recorder: The persistence client records each request (with its requestId) through the observer the session installs (observeTraffic), then the development server's events returned with the answer.
- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: The composition root (src/app/main.js) constructs the persistence client and hands it to the session.

#### Contracts bound

- Write a project file (`write-file`): requires, via `client-write-out`
- Write a project file (`write-file`): provider, via `session-calls-persistence-client`
- Write a project file (`write-file`): consumer, via `persistence-client-calls-dev-server-plugin`

#### Known unknowns

- No retry, queue or offline buffer: a failed request is reported once to the caller.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/persist/client.js`

### Development-server plugin

Vite middleware that exposes the workspace service over HTTP and server-sent events; development only.

- Category: Computational service
- Parent: Persistence (`persistence`)

#### Responsibilities

- Accept requests only from loopback hosts and origins carrying the x-exegra-client header (events excepted)
- Route projects, project, write, generated, export, import and events; map errors to JSON with an HTTP status, answering 409 for a write conflict
- Limit request bodies to 20 MB and keep event streams alive with periodic pings

#### Ports

| Name | Id | Direction | Contract | Protocol |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Write file | plugin-write-in | provides | Write a project file | HTTP POST /__exegra/write |
| Write file to workspace | plugin-write-out | requires | Write a project file | in-process call |

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Workspace service: Delegates every route to the workspace service.
- Data flow → Workspace service: Request bodies pass to the workspace service.
- Data flow → Persistence client: Responses (file texts and hashes, write results, conflicts) and change events return to the client.
- Depends on → Vite: Runs as middleware of the Vite development server and closes its streams when the server closes.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Persistence client: Sends HTTP requests to /__exegra/* and opens the events stream.
- Data flow → Persistence client: Spec and layout text with the base hash, generated files and import bundles travel in request bodies.
- Data flow → Workspace service: File change notifications (name and hash) pass to the plugin, which streams them as server-sent events.
- Calls → Build and development system: Registers the plugin with the specs directory.
- Observes → Traffic recorder: The development server returns its own events (ws-*, the answer, inferred external edits) in the x-exegra-trace header of each answer and as trace messages of the change stream; the persistence client appends them.

#### Contracts bound

- Write a project file (`write-file`): provides, via `plugin-write-in`
- Write a project file (`write-file`): requires, via `plugin-write-out`
- Write a project file (`write-file`): provider, via `persistence-client-calls-dev-server-plugin`
- Write a project file (`write-file`): consumer, via `dev-server-plugin-calls-workspace-service`

#### Known unknowns

- Protection rests on loopback host, loopback origin and a custom header only; any local process can read and write projects while the development server runs.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js`

### Workspace service

The Node filesystem boundary for projects under specs/: every read, write, export, import and watch of project files goes through it.

- Category: Computational service
- Parent: Persistence (`persistence`)

#### Responsibilities

- Validate project names and refuse any path that leaves the project directory
- Write spec or layout text only if the base hash equals the current file's hash, replacing the file atomically, serialized per project, appending a line to .history/journal.jsonl
- Read a project with the hashes of its files and its recorded results
- Read project.jsonc: the specification it links to, the realisation roots it declares and the product files it layers on the specification ("products": files or directories of *.jsonc, read in order, each with whether its product.imports.spec resolves to the specification), without ever writing it
- Write generated artifacts only under generated/, and record verification results
- Export and import project bundles, and watch a project's files for external changes
- Report a changed, added or removed product file (or a changed products list in project.jsonc) as a change of the file products, and read a project given inline as a specification file and product files with no registration (readInline)

#### Ports

| Name | Id | Direction | Contract | Protocol |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Write file | workspace-write-in | provides | Write a project file | in-process call |

#### Relationships out

- Data flow → Development-server plugin: File change notifications (name and hash) pass to the plugin, which streams them as server-sent events.
- Writes → Specification file: Replaces spec.jsonc atomically after a base-hash check, or on import.
- Writes → Layout file: Replaces layout.jsonc atomically after a base-hash check, or on import.
- Writes → Recorded results file: Replaces evidence/results.json when verification records results, or on import.
- Writes → Generated artifacts: Writes generated files under generated/ only, skipping files whose content is unchanged.
- Calls → File system: Reads, writes, renames, appends to the journal and watches directories with node:fs.
- Depends on → jsonc-parser: Reads a project's title from its specification.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Development-server plugin: Delegates every route to the workspace service.
- Data flow → Development-server plugin: Request bodies pass to the workspace service.
- Calls → Agent command line: Reads projects and writes changed files with the hashes read at the start; records verification results and generated artifacts.
- Data flow → Agent command line: The committed spec and layout text with their base hashes travel to the workspace service after a successful transaction.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the workspace service on temporary directories.
- Calls → Markdown importer: Writes the converted specification and its import map through the workspace service (tools/workspace.js).

#### Contracts bound

- Write a project file (`write-file`): provides, via `workspace-write-in`
- Write a project file (`write-file`): provider, via `dev-server-plugin-calls-workspace-service`

#### Known unknowns

- On Windows the atomic rename is retried and then replaced by an in-place write, which is not atomic.
- Import and recording of results write without a base-hash check, and import writes its files one after another, not as one atomic unit.
- The journal is read in full on every write to number its next line and is never truncated.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `tools/workspace.js`, `tools/spec-manifest.js`

### Specification file

specs/<project>/spec.jsonc: the JSONC text that is the serialized authority of a project's semantic model.

- Category: Data store
- Parent: Persistence (`persistence`)
- Owner: transactions

#### Relationships out

- Data flow → Generated artifacts: The generated documents are derived from the specification text (through the generators, run by the command line or the realisation panel). Declared when the data-model rule holder-reaches-owner found no path between the two stores.

#### Relationships in

- Writes → Workspace service: Replaces spec.jsonc atomically after a base-hash check, or on import.
- Reads → Specification checker: Reads the file given on the command line directly with node:fs.
- Writes → Benchmark generator: Writes specs/benchmark/spec.jsonc directly with node:fs.
- Data flow → Markdown importer: The generated text of the project's specification file travels to the file, deterministically, with the losses recorded in its system notes.
- Writes → Markdown importer: Writes the project's specification file directly with node:fs, bypassing the transaction system.
- Data flow → Register importer: The generated text of the project's specification file travels to the file, deterministically, with the losses recorded in its system notes.
- Writes → Register importer: Writes the project's specification file directly with node:fs, bypassing the transaction system.
- Reads → Specification store service: Reads the specification's bytes on import.
- Writes → Specification store service: export writes the stored text back to the specification, atomically, unless it changed since the store last synchronised with it.

#### Known unknowns

- Four paths change project files without a transaction or a journal line (WG-4775, finding F05): the bundle import (tools/workspace.js importProject, scenario import-bundle), tools/import-markdown.js (scenario markdown-import), tools/make-benchmark.js (specs/benchmark/spec.jsonc), and exegra apply when its layout write conflicts after the specification was written (scenario cli-apply-partial-write: the spec write has its journal line, but the transaction's two files are no longer written together). The first three write directly with node:fs or atomicWrite.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Notes: A data file, not code.

### Layout file

specs/<project>/layout.jsonc: presentation-only pins of leaf nodes per view; nothing in it carries architectural meaning.

- Category: Data store
- Parent: Persistence (`persistence`)
- Owner: transactions

#### Relationships in

- Writes → Workspace service: Replaces layout.jsonc atomically after a base-hash check, or on import.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Notes: A data file, not code.

### Recorded results file

specs/<project>/evidence/results.json: outcomes of executed test evidence and the last traceability result, written by `exegra verify`.

- Category: Data store
- Parent: Persistence (`persistence`)
- Owner: verification-pipeline

#### Relationships out

- Data flow → Generated artifacts: exegra generate and the Realisation panel derive the claims report and task list from claim results evaluated with the recorded results.

#### Relationships in

- Writes → Workspace service: Replaces evidence/results.json when verification records results, or on import.
- Writes → Game days and runtime monitors: Game-day and monitor results recorded as evidence.

#### Known unknowns

- Results are not tied to the model hash or the code revision they were recorded for.
- The claims key (record external-claim-outcomes) is read by claim evaluation and kept by exegra verify, but nothing in this repository writes it; only an external tool could (WG-4775, finding F03).
- A skipped run keeps the outcome recorded before, which may predate the current code; its ranAt shows when it was recorded.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Notes: A data file, not code.

### Generated artifacts

specs/<project>/generated/: manifest.json, ARCHITECTURE.md, claims-report.md, claims-report.json and tasks.json derived from the model.

- Category: Data store
- Parent: Persistence (`persistence`)
- Owner: generators

#### Relationships in

- Writes → Workspace service: Writes generated files under generated/ only, skipping files whose content is unchanged.
- Generates → Artifact generators: Produces the text of the five generated files; the workspace service writes them.
- Data flow → Specification file: The generated documents are derived from the specification text (through the generators, run by the command line or the realisation panel). Declared when the data-model rule holder-reaches-owner found no path between the two stores.
- Data flow → Recorded results file: exegra generate and the Realisation panel derive the claims report and task list from claim results evaluated with the recorded results.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Notes: Derived data files, not code and not read back by Exegra.

### Agent command line

A command line that prints one JSON document per command so an AI coding agent can read, check, simulate, generate, verify and change a specification through the same pipeline as the UI.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Agent interface (`agent-interface`)

#### Responsibilities

- Commands: projects, validate, list, show, deps, contracts, claims, evaluate, gaps, ops, apply, admit, plan-delete, simulate, trace, prove, integrity, prove-integrity, generate, traceability, verify, readiness, cover-machines, cover-functions, store, help
- Print a JSON envelope (ok, command, project, semanticHash, result or error with code and diagnostics) and use exit codes 0 ok, 1 command failed, 2 usage error
- Apply operations through Project.apply, then write the changed files with the base hashes read at the start; a stale --expect-hash, an invalid operation or a disk conflict leaves the files unchanged
- Run the verification pipeline: validation, traceability, execution of test evidence and recording of the results
- Include the request log of the run in the simulate result (requestLog), one line per contract event
- Copy a project's specification into its SQLite store and write it back as text (store import, store export), refusing an export over a specification that changed since the store last synchronised with it
- Register a specification file and product files as a project from a script (project add, remove, list: specs/<name>/project.jsonc with paths relative to the project directory, refusing an existing project without --force and a name that is not a project name), and take the project inline (--spec, --product) on the read-only commands, so a job needs no registration
- Acknowledge admit reasons by code (admit --accept, repeatable or comma separated): a reason that carries acknowledgeable true and whose code is accepted no longer blocks admission and is listed under acknowledged; a reason without it (a failure rather than a decision) cannot be accepted, and passing its code is the error admit.not-acknowledgeable
- Report in admit what a change does to privileges and guarantees as acknowledgeable reasons: admit.privilege-change for every capability added to a role, grant, delegation or token scope and every new or changed grant or delegation without maxDuration (capabilities removed are listed under diff.privilege.removed, not as a reason), and admit.guarantee-weakened for a copy class whose freshness or erasure bound is looser than another class of the same record, a changed or removed retention, integrity rule or claim (before and after)
- Report a new claim that is realised by code and cannot be decided at specification time (a requirement, a claim of an external verification method or of external-evidence) that names a milestone and has no result yet as pendingRealisation instead of a weak claim; a requirement without a milestone is the reason admit.requirement-without-milestone
- Admit a change set (admit): evaluate the specification before and after the operations are applied in memory through Project.apply, report the diff of claim statuses, validation diagnostics, readiness gaps and integrity violations, and admit it only when no claim got worse, no validation error or integrity violation is new and every claim it creates is verified or proved; report the check and grounding of every claim (counts before and after, and each claim it creates) and reject a claim the change creates that is holds-by-construction or restated-scenario (admit.claim-vacuous), single-trace (admit.claim-single-trace) or self-contained (admit.claim-self-contained), each reason marked acknowledgeable; write the specification, as apply does, only with --write and an admitted verdict
- Show the check and grounding of each claim (src/core/claim-strength.js) in the results of claims and evaluate

#### Ports

| Name | Id | Direction | Contract | Protocol |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Command | cli-command-in | provides | Agent command | process arguments and JSON on stdout |
| Apply transaction | cli-apply-out | requires | Apply a transaction | in-process call |

#### Relationships out

- Submits edits to → Transaction system: The apply command submits operations through Project.apply with origin cli; nothing edits specification text directly.
- Calls → Workspace service: Reads projects and writes changed files with the hashes read at the start; records verification results and generated artifacts.
- Data flow → Workspace service: The committed spec and layout text with their base hashes travel to the workspace service after a successful transaction.
- Calls → Model queries: Answers list, show, deps, contracts and plan-delete.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Evaluates claims for claims, evaluate, trace and verify.
- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs scenarios for simulate and trace.
- Calls → Artifact generators: The generate command derives the artifacts.
- Calls → Verification pipeline: The traceability and verify commands run the module-to-disk check.
- Calls → Test suites: verify executes test evidence by spawning node --test on a core test file.
- Calls → Playwright: verify executes browser test evidence by spawning Playwright, unless --skip-browser.
- Calls → File system: Reads operation files and checks paths.
- Calls → Simulation contract: simulate and trace derive the engine input the same way.
- Calls → Contract conformance: simulate lists the request log of the run.
- Calls → Proof export: The prove command explores a scenario and checks its state invariants in Lean.
- Calls → External realisation: traceability, generate and verify resolve the project's realisation roots; verify records external test evidence from their JUnit reports.
- Calls → Integrity rules: The integrity and prove-integrity commands.
- Calls → Stable serialization and text digests: Serialises results deterministically.
- Calls → Claim method registry: Reads the registry of claim methods for its usage and validation messages.
- Calls → Markdown importer: The markdown command renders the documents of a linked project through the importer's module.
- Calls → Validator: Applies the evidence reference rule (src/core/evidence-ref.js) to the commands that run verification.
- Calls → Generation readiness: The readiness command lists the gaps of a project.
- Calls → Machine coverage: The cover-machines command reports the coverage table and the findings and writes the generated scenarios as ops.json.
- Calls → Specification store service: store import and store export copy a project's specification into its store and back.
- Calls → Specification layers: Trying integrity rules on a layered project analyses the platform text with its product files.
- Calls → Claim strength: Adds the check and grounding to claims and evaluate, and the strength review to admit.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → AI coding agent: Runs one command per process and reads the JSON envelope.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests run commands of the agent command line.

#### Contracts bound

- Agent command (`agent-command`): provides, via `cli-command-in`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): requires, via `cli-apply-out`
- Agent command (`agent-command`): provider, via `ai-coding-agent-calls-agent-cli`
- Apply a transaction (`apply-transaction`): consumer, via `agent-cli-submits-edits-transactions`
- Evaluate claims (`evaluate-claims`): consumer, via `agent-cli-calls-claim-evaluator`
- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): consumer, via `agent-cli-calls-simulation-engine`

#### Known unknowns

- Each invocation builds a fresh Project from disk: there is no undo history across invocations and no long-running session.
- Writing the spec file and the layout file are two separate writes; if the second conflicts, the first has already been written.
- No MCP server or other protocol wrapper exists; the command line is the only agent-facing interface.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `cli/exegra.js`, `cli/commands.js`, `cli/admit.js`
- Notes: The `verify` command's pipeline (runVerify, runTestReference) lives in cli/commands.js; the traceability check it uses is in tools/traceability.js, claimed by verification-pipeline.

### Build and development system

Vite configuration: serves the application on 127.0.0.1 with the development-server plugin, builds a static bundle with relative asset URLs, and keeps saved specifications from triggering page reloads. The Pages workflow publishes that bundle, which opens Exegra's own specification first, to the repository's GitHub Pages site on every push to main.

- Category: Infrastructure
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Responsibilities

- Build a static bundle whose asset URLs are relative, so it loads at a domain root or under the repository's GitHub Pages sub-path
- On every push to main, build the Lean core to WebAssembly, validate Exegra's own specification and publish the bundle to GitHub Pages (.github/workflows/pages.yml); a specification that does not validate is not published

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Vite: Configures the server (127.0.0.1, specs not watched) and the build.
- Calls → Development-server plugin: Registers the plugin with the specs directory.

#### Known unknowns

- The GitHub Pages site is the production build: read-only, it shows the specifications as of the last push to main with the evidence results recorded in specs/exegra/evidence/results.json, and, the Lean core being wasm64, it does not run in stable Safari.
- The production build contains no persistence service: it bundles the shipped specs and is read-only.
- dist/ embeds as text the specification, layout and recorded results of every shipped project and the specification of every linked project whose specification lives outside specs/ (copy shipped-specifications); a linked project's specification, which is not in this repository, therefore ends up in the build output (WG-4775, finding F11).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `vite.config.js`, `.github/workflows/pages.yml`
- Notes: package.json scripts (dev, build, test, test:browser, typecheck, exegra, generate, verify) are not a scanned source file.

### Artifact generators

Deterministic pure functions from the model, claim results and diagnostics to five artifacts: manifest.json, ARCHITECTURE.md, claims-report.md, claims-report.json and tasks.json.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Responsibilities

- Derive each artifact byte-identically from the same inputs, without reading the file system, the clock or the environment
- Report a claim without a result as not evaluated, never as verified
- Derive a task list from violated and unevaluated claims, validation errors, not-implemented components, traceability findings, unknowns and gaps

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Model queries: Lists children, relationships and contracts for the architecture document.
- Reads → Claim method registry: Reads claim status labels.
- Generates → Generated artifacts: Produces the text of the five generated files; the workspace service writes them.
- Calls → Stable serialization and text digests: Writes generated files with sorted keys.
- Calls → Semantic hash binding: Stamps artifacts with the semantic hash.
- Calls → Claim strength: Shows the check and grounding of each claim in the claims report.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Realisation panel: Generates the five artifacts from the session's model and results in the browser.
- Calls → Agent command line: The generate command derives the artifacts.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the generators.

#### Known unknowns

- No generator emits code or any part of Exegra's own runtime; Exegra generates reports about the model, not the software it describes.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/generate/architecture-doc.js`, `src/core/generate/claims-report.js`, `src/core/generate/index.js`, `src/core/generate/manifest.js`, `src/core/generate/task-manifest.js`

### Verification pipeline

Compares the implementation modules that components declare with the files on disk and, driven by `exegra verify`, executes test evidence and records the results.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Responsibilities

- Report declared modules missing on disk and files under src, cli and tools (.js, .json, .css, .html) plus index.html and vite.config.js that no component claims, leaving out generated build output (src/engine/pkg/) and files whose name starts with local- (local data, ignored by git)
- Through `verify`: validate, check traceability, execute each evidence of kind test with node --test or Playwright and record outcomes and the traceability result in results.json, then re-evaluate claims with them
- Refuse an evidence reference with characters outside letters, digits, underscore, dot, slash, hyphen, space, hash and colon, and time a test run out after ten minutes
- Reuse the outcomes that `npm test` and `npm run test:browser` already recorded (reporters in tools/ writing .outcomes/, each stamped with a hash over the tracked files, the untracked files git does not ignore and the generated engine builds) instead of running the same test again: an evidence reference is answered from the recorded run only when the run was complete, the tree did not change during it and its stamp equals the stamp of the tree verify runs on; a reference that matches no recorded test, a failure, or a skipped browser test is run as before, and a unit test that skipped itself is recorded as skipped. results.json says for each outcome whether it was run or reused; `verify --fresh` reuses nothing

#### Relationships out

- Calls → File system: Scans src, cli, tools and the root files and checks that declared modules exist.
- Calls → External realisation: The traceability check delegates modules that name a root, and the scan of a root's include globs.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Agent command line: The traceability and verify commands run the module-to-disk check.

#### Known unknowns

- Only evidence of kind test is executed; measurement, document and review evidence stay author-entered.
- The traceability check compares paths only: it does not check that a module implements what its component claims, nor that relationships match imports. Its surface is src/, cli/ and tools/ with the extensions .js, .json, .css and .html, plus index.html and vite.config.js: a .mjs file is outside it (tools/bench-mm1.mjs was unclaimed until this correction), and formal/ and engine/ are outside it too, so an unclaimed Lean or Rust file is not reported.
- Browser tests need Chrome and the Vite development server; `verify --skip-browser` skips them and leaves their evidence without a recorded result.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `tools/traceability.js`, `tools/lean-theorems.js`, `tools/tree-stamp.js`, `tools/outcomes-node-reporter.js`, `tools/outcomes-playwright-reporter.js`, `tools/run-unit-tests.js`, `cli/outcomes.js`
- Notes: Orchestrated by the verify command in cli/commands.js (claimed by agent-cli).

### Specification checker

A read-only command that prints a specification file's diagnostics, scenario outcomes and claim results, and optionally a trace.

- Category: Infrastructure
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Validator: Analyzes the file with analyzeSpec.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Prints the status of every claim.
- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs every scenario and optionally prints a trace.
- Reads → Specification file: Reads the file given on the command line directly with node:fs.
- Calls → Semantic hash binding: Prints the semantic hash.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `tools/check-spec.js`

### Benchmark generator

Writes specs/benchmark/spec.jsonc, a deterministic synthetic specification (396 components and 507 relationships by default) for measuring layout and rendering.

- Category: Infrastructure
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Validator: Checks that the generated specification analyzes.
- Writes → Specification file: Writes specs/benchmark/spec.jsonc directly with node:fs.

#### Known unknowns

- It writes the benchmark specification directly with node:fs, not through the workspace service or the transaction system.

#### Implementation

- Modules: `tools/make-benchmark.js`

### Markdown importer

A command line tool that converts a Markdown specification, structured Markdown kept in another repository, into the specification file of a linked project, a specification that declares its own entity kinds and verification methods.

- Category: Infrastructure
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Responsibilities

- Read the Markdown (white paper, roadmap, the specification documents and the verification document) and the git commit of that repository, plus the project's import map (specs/<project>/import-map.jsonc), which supplies what Exegra requires and the Markdown does not state (the project's name and title, where those documents are, and a category per component); fail with a message naming a component that has no entry in the map
- Work one way and deterministically: the same input gives the same bytes, it never writes to the Markdown repository and it states nothing the Markdown does not; the Markdown stays the authority and the generated file says it must not be edited by hand
- Record what it recognised but could not map: each kind of loss with a count and examples is printed and also written into the generated specification's system notes
- With --check, compare the generated text with the file on disk and exit 1 when it is out of date; usage errors exit 2

#### Relationships out

- Reads → Markdown specification: Reads the Markdown documents and the repository's git commit; it never writes there.
- Data flow → Specification file: The generated text of the project's specification file travels to the file, deterministically, with the losses recorded in its system notes.
- Writes → Specification file: Writes the project's specification file directly with node:fs, bypassing the transaction system.
- Depends on → jsonc-parser: Reads the project's import map with the library.
- Calls → File system: Reads the Markdown tree and the import map, and writes or compares the generated file, with node:fs.
- Calls → Workspace service: Writes the converted specification and its import map through the workspace service (tools/workspace.js).

#### Relationships in

- Data flow → Markdown specification: The requirements, components, relationships, goals, decisions and roadmap stated in the Markdown travel to the importer, one way.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the importer on fixture Markdown and analyze its output.
- Calls → Register importer: Reuses the Markdown importer's text helpers, loss collection, coverage comparison and git commit reader.
- Calls → Agent command line: The markdown command renders the documents of a linked project through the importer's module.

#### Known unknowns

- It does not analyze, validate or evaluate what it writes (it imports only the jsonc-parser library); that the output analyzes without errors is checked by the tests, not by the tool.
- It writes the project's specification file directly with node:fs, not through the workspace service or the transaction system, and an edit made to that file through Exegra is overwritten by the next import.
- It reads no default source: the source directory is given with --source or as "source" in the import map, and the project with --project or with --map and --out.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `tools/import-markdown.js`
- Notes: Not part of the running application or the agent command line, except that the markdown command renders documents with its functions; it is run by hand when the Markdown specification changes.

### Screenshot tool

A development aid that loads the running application in Chrome, optionally runs a snippet, saves a screenshot and prints console errors.

- Category: Infrastructure
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Playwright: Drives Chrome to load the page and capture it.
- Observes → Vite: Loads the application served by the development server and reports console errors.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `tools/shot.mjs`

### Test suites

Core tests (node:test) over the semantic core, generators, workspace and agent command line, and Playwright browser tests of the running application: against scratch copies of this specification and of the multiplayer test fixture, and, when a project is linked locally, read-only against it (local-*.spec.js files, which are not tracked); the older browser tests run at desktop size and tests/browser/mobile.spec.js runs at phone and tablet size by touch. The browser tests run on two Playwright workers by default (EXEGRA_TEST_WORKERS overrides); each worker slot has its own pair of scratch projects (zz-test-exegra-wN, zz-test-multiplayer-wN), so edits, saves and change notices never cross workers.

- Category: Infrastructure
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Playwright: Browser tests run under Playwright.
- Depends on → Vite: Playwright starts the Vite development server for the browser tests.
- Calls → Transaction system: Core tests exercise Project and the operations.
- Calls → Parser: Core tests exercise parsing and normalization.
- Calls → Validator: Core tests exercise the validation rules.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Core tests exercise claim evaluation.
- Calls → Simulation engine: Core tests exercise the simulation.
- Calls → Projection system: Core tests exercise projections.
- Calls → Layout engine: Core tests exercise the layout.
- Calls → Artifact generators: Core tests exercise the generators.
- Calls → Workspace service: Core tests exercise the workspace service on temporary directories.
- Calls → Markdown importer: Core tests exercise the importer on fixture Markdown and analyze its output.
- Calls → Agent command line: Core tests run commands of the agent command line.
- Calls → Contract conformance: Core tests exercise the static and trace checks and the request log.
- Calls → Rust engine (differential oracle): The conformance and differential tests compare the Lean engine with the Rust engine.
- Calls → Register importer: Core tests exercise the register importer on a fixture and analyze its output.
- Calls → Formal semantics (Lean 4): The differential tool (tools/differential.js) runs the Lean executable and the Rust engine on the goldens and on generated inputs.
- Calls → Simulation contract: The conformance test and the golden runner read the engine input derived by sim-input.js and the goldens of the contract.
- Calls → Stable serialization and text digests: The golden recorder writes its traces with the stable JSON serializer.
- Calls → Specification store service: Core tests round-trip every specification and fixture through the store.
- Calls → Claim strength: Core tests exercise the check and grounding on fixtures from the Harbourline fold.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Agent command line: verify executes test evidence by spawning node --test on a core test file.

#### Known unknowns

- Tests are evidence only through `exegra verify`; recorded results are not tied to the code revision.
- No test measures performance, so no claim about frame rate or load time has evidence.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `playwright.config.js`, `tests/browser/exegra.spec.js`, `tests/browser/global-setup.js`, `tests/browser/global-teardown.js`, `tests/browser/interaction.spec.js`, `tests/browser/live.spec.js`, `tests/browser/mobile.spec.js`, `tests/browser/scratch.js`, `tests/browser/simulation-worker.spec.js`, `tests/core/claims.test.js`, `tests/core/claim-diagnostics.test.js`, `tests/core/concordance.test.js`, `tests/core/cli.test.js`, `tests/core/data-model.test.js`, `tests/core/fault-model.test.js`, `tests/core/generate.test.js`, `tests/core/import-markdown.test.js`, `tests/core/import-register.test.js`, `tests/core/layout.test.js`, `tests/core/model-kernel.test.js`, `tests/core/model.test.js`, `tests/core/outcome-reuse.test.js`, `tests/core/projections.test.js`, `tests/core/references.test.js`, `tests/core/replay.test.js`, `tests/core/roundtrip.test.js`, `tests/core/runtime-data-complete.test.js`, `tests/core/sim.test.js`, `tests/core/traffic.test.js`, `tests/core/vocabulary.test.js`, `tests/core/workspace.test.js`, `tests/fixtures/live/open-edit-save.json`, `tests/fixtures/parse-corpus/cases.json.gz`, `tests/oracle/parse-reference.js`, `tools/record-goldens.js`, `tools/conformance.js`, `tools/bench-mm1.mjs`, `tests/core/conformance.test.js`, `tools/differential.js`, `tests/core/readiness-machines.test.js`, `tests/fixtures/readiness-machines/spec.jsonc`, `tests/core/spec-store.test.js`

### Simulation aspect registry

Registers simulation aspects (chaos, load, cost and others) and runs their hooks inside the simulation core; a specification names the aspects it uses, with versions and parameters, as data.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Load aspects from the registry and from a plugins directory, by name and version
- Validate the simulation section of a specification against each aspect's declared vocabulary; never evaluate specification text as code
- Call aspect hooks at schedule, deliver, step and end, and record aspect metrics in the trace

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Chaos aspect: Registered aspect.
- Calls → Load aspect: Registered aspect.
- Calls → Cost aspect: Registered aspect.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs the hooks of the aspects a specification enables (planned, WG-4665).

#### Implementation

- Status: not-implemented
- Notes: Planned: WG-4665. Not built yet; its claims stay not evaluated until recorded evidence supports them.

### Refinement checker

Checks that a component's abstract behavior and the behaviors of its children agree at its boundary, so a simulation can go deeper region by region without drifting from the shallower model.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Run the refined behaviors, project their trace onto the component's boundary and check that the abstract behavior allows it
- Report the first diverging boundary event as a violated claim
- Choose per component whether a run uses the abstract behavior or its children, following the collapse state of the view

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs the abstract and the refined behaviors of a component (planned, WG-4666).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Claim evaluator: Refinement checks are claims.

#### Implementation

- Status: not-implemented
- Notes: Planned: WG-4666. Not built yet; its claims stay not evaluated until recorded evidence supports them.

### Chaos aspect

Seeded fault campaigns: kill instances, partition relationships, skew clocks, slow resources, across many runs of a scenario, each run evaluating the specification's checks.

- Category: Logical module

#### Responsibilities

- Draw failures from declared kinds, targets, rates and windows with a seed
- Record every injected failure in the trace
- Report each run's check results

- Status: not-implemented

Drives scenarios with arrival processes over instances and resources and records utilisation, queue depth, latency percentiles, saturation and backpressure; validated against queueing theory.

- Category: Logical module

### Simulation contract

What a simulation engine is judged by: the engine input Exegra derives from the validated model for one scenario and fault set, and the trace format the engine must produce. A golden conformance suite holds an input and a reference trace for every shipped scenario.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Derive the engine input from the model: the scenario, its behaviors with variables, states and transitions, the events and the faults, with expressions as strings, so an engine needs no knowledge of JSONC, the meta-model or validation
- Define the trace format field by field, with a format version
- Run any engine against the golden suite, in-process or as a command reading an input on stdin and writing a trace on stdout, and report the first differing step and field
- Give an implementation-level behavior the declared types of the references its expressions read (behaviorTypes: time as i64, each variable repr, each received payload field, lowered so a timestamp is i64, a duration u64, a declared id or scalar its representation, an option or optional field option), and name the time unit of a machine (machineTimeUnit)
- Derive the machines a quota pool runs as (src/core/pools.js, WG-5278): one per pooled limit and modelled member, <pool>-<limit>-m<k>, with subject the pool's holder, states normal and throttled and the pool variables; usage is admitted on headroom, or within the fair share when throttled; a window end throttles a member over its share for the stated consecutive windows unless its exemption applies from that window, releases after the stated windows under, resets a rate and advances the window; an exemption is recorded the first time it is set, and lifts a throttle only when retroactive. simulationInput adds the machines of the pools a scenario lists after its behaviors, and the trace check and the proof plan count them among the scenario's machines

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Resource model: Lowers resources, classes and service demand into the version-2 engine input (planned).
- Calls → Workload model: Lowers workloads, rates, durations, distributions and links into the version-2 engine input (planned).
- Calls → Stable serialization and text digests: Writes the goldens with sorted keys (tools/record-goldens.js).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application session: Derives the engine input for the scenario the Simulation tab loads.
- Calls → Agent command line: simulate and trace derive the engine input the same way.
- Calls → Proof export: Builds the scenario's engine input with simulationInput, exactly as simulate does.
- Calls → Self-model replay: Reads the behaviors of the scenario from the engine input (simulationInput).
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Derives the engine input of a scenario from the model before a trace claim runs it.
- Calls → Simulation engine: Takes the engine input that simulation-contract derives (sim-input.js) and checks the input before it runs.
- Calls → Test suites: The conformance test and the golden runner read the engine input derived by sim-input.js and the goldens of the contract.
- Calls → Machine coverage: Derives the engine input of a scenario, and of the machine alone with its routes, with simulationInput.

#### Contracts bound

- Engine input, format version 2 (`engine-input-v2`): consumer, via `simulation-contract-calls-resource-model`
- Engine input, format version 2 (`engine-input-v2`): consumer, via `simulation-contract-calls-workload-model`

#### Known unknowns

- Engine input and trace format 2 (contracts engine-input-v2 and trace-v2) are derived by simulationInput whenever a specification uses a format-2 construct (needsFormat2, simulationInputV2 in src/core/sim-input.js) and run by the engine's version-2 semantics (engine/exegra-engine/src/lib.rs dispatches on the version). Not yet: format-2 goldens (tests/conformance/goldens holds version 1 only), and the WG-4741 items: crash-instance, meters and the cost metric in the engine, and calibration, sensitivity, the operating envelope and revision binding of metric claims. A specification that uses no format-2 construct still derives version 1, unchanged.
- The machines a quota pool runs as are derived, not entities: renaming a pool does not rewrite claim expressions that name them, and the simulator panel and inspector list only declared behaviors (WG-5281).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/sim-input.js`, `tests/conformance/`, `src/core/pools.js`
- Notes: tests/conformance/ holds the fixture specification, the committed goldens and the README that lists what the fixture covers.

### Responsive layout

Mobile first: on a phone the 3D scene fills the screen and every panel is a bottom sheet over it; the same panels expand into a side panel on a tablet and into side-by-side columns on a desktop. One set of panel components serves every size.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Hold the destinations (explore, inspect, simulate, verify, and changes, diagnostics, source and realisation under More) in a bottom navigation bar on a phone and a navigation rail from 768 px wide, where every destination has its own rail button
- Present the active destination as a bottom sheet with peek, half and full detents on a phone: dragged on its header by touch or pointer, a flick snaps one detent on, a tap on the handle cycles the detents, tapping the active destination again or Escape collapses it, one sheet at a time
- From 768 px show the active destination as a side panel beside the scene with a collapse control and a width dragged on pointer devices; from 1200 px show the explorer as a column of its own and the context panel beside the scene without opening anything
- Use the same panel elements at every size: layout.js only sets data attributes and widths, layout.css places them, sheet.js drags the detents
- Bring the inspector forward when an entity is selected by any route, unless another panel is pinned open: at peek on a phone when no sheet is open or another only peeks, docked on a tablet when the side panel is closed
- Keep the scene interactive above or beside every panel, and dock the simulation transport above the navigation while a scenario is loaded
- Give every interactive control a touch target of at least 44 by 44 CSS pixels and respect the safe-area insets of the device
- Remember the context destination, the panel widths and the open state of the tablet and desktop panels in localStorage; a phone always starts with the scene and no sheet open
- Keep the page frame from scrolling: html, body and the app frame clip their overflow, programmatic focus never scrolls, and only the inside of a panel scrolls

#### Relationships out

- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds the navigation and the More menu with the shared helpers and icons.
- Writes → Browser web storage: Remembers the context destination, the panel widths and the open state of the tablet and desktop panels in localStorage.
- Calls → Traffic recorder: src/ui/layout.js records panel-toggled and layout-preferences-written.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application shell: Creates the responsive layout, which places the panels as sheets, a side panel or columns for the viewport and shows destinations on request.
- Calls → Toolbar: The overflow menu and the save-conflict menu use the shared menu controller.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/layout.js`, `src/ui/sheet.js`, `src/ui/menu.js`
- Notes: layout.js holds the destinations, the navigation and the panel states; sheet.js is the bottom-sheet controller; menu.js is the menu controller shared by More, the app bar's overflow menu and the save-conflict menu.

### Fault model and environment

Makes what can fail and what the environment guarantees part of the specification: environments, failure modes with sourced rates and durations, and fault models with concurrency bounds; every behavioral and reliability claim is evaluated under a named fault model.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Define the environment, failureMode and faultModel kinds and validate them, including a source for every rate, duration, guarantee and bound
- State the fault model in the scope of every claim evaluated under it
- Relate scenario faults to the failure modes they realise
- Report claims that name no fault model while the specification declares one (gap.no-fault-model)

#### Relationships in

- Depends on → Exhaustive explorer: Explores every subset of the scenario's faults; the concurrency bounds of its fault model are to be applied once the engine input carries them.
- Depends on → Claim evaluator: States the fault model in each claim result's scope.
- Depends on → Implementation refinement checker: Implementation runs draw faults from the fault model.
- Calls → Validator: Runs the fault model rules (missing sources, bounds, realises outside the model) as part of validation.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/fault-model.js`
- Notes: The environment, failureMode and faultModel kinds, the faultModel and realises properties and the any-kind reference ("ref": ["*"]) are declared in the meta-model and checked by the generic parser; fault-model.js holds the specific rules (missing sources, bounds, realises outside the model) and the scope text of a claim result.

### Exhaustive explorer

Explores every configuration of a scenario reachable within a bound, over every order of delivery of simultaneous events and every subset of the scenario's faults, with state deduplication. It reports that a state invariant holds in all N of them or returns a counterexample run, and it checks the temporal claims never, precedes and eventually on every complete run (a run ends at quiescence, at the step limit or in a failed step) by searching the product of the explorer's state with a small monitor. The explorer is defined in the Lean semantics and proved sound there; a faster explorer in the Rust engine (WG-4650) is to be checked differentially against it.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Define the nondeterminism a scenario admits as a successor relation over configurations: which of the pending entries with the head's time is delivered next, and which subset of the scenario's faults acts in the run; the deterministic step is one of the successors (step_mem_successors)
- Explore breadth-first with a visited set under a bound on steps and a budget of states, and return holds with the number N of distinct states visited, a counterexample (a fault choice and delivery choices), or incomplete when the budget runs out
- Prove that holds means the invariant holds in every state reachable within the bound (explore_sound) and that a counterexample is a run of the successor relation within the bound that ends with the invariant false (explore_counterexample_real), with only Lean's standard axioms
- Report "verified by Lean for all N configurations within the bound" distinctly from trace-level checks
- Define the temporal forms never, precedes and eventually over finite runs of the successor relation exactly as checkTrace reads them on a trace (a matcher is a delivery of an event or a firing of a transition; precedes allows the first event in the same step as a transition; eventually reads its expression in the final configuration of the run), and check them with a generic breadth-first search (Search.lean) over the explorer state with a monitor (Temporal.lean)
- Prove the monitor agrees with the trace-level definition of each form (never_monitor, precedes_monitor, seen_monitor), that the search replays real runs (greplay_nodes), that holds means the form holds on every complete run (tcheck_sound) and that a counterexample is a real run that violates it (tcheck_counterexample_real); paths cut off by the bound or the state budget, and runs that end without quiescence and would violate or cannot decide an eventually, are reported incomplete, never holds

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Simulation engine: A Rust explorer with the same semantics as simulation, for state spaces too large for the Lean one, checked differentially against it (planned, WG-4650).
- Depends on → Fault model and environment: Explores every subset of the scenario's faults; the concurrency bounds of its fault model are to be applied once the engine input carries them.
- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): The successor relation takes the semantics' deterministic step after each choice; the explorer runs the semantics' own step function.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Claim evaluator: Claims evaluated exhaustively within bounds (planned, WG-4650).
- Depends on → Deployment model: Cutover, rollback and migration claims are checked exhaustively.
- Depends on → Proof export: Counterexamples to induction come from bounded exploration.

#### Known unknowns

- The engine input (format version 1) carries no bound of a scenario's fault model, so every subset of the scenario's faults is explored; the concurrency bounds of fault models are not applied yet.
- A fault acts for the whole run or not at all: a fault without an occurrence cannot act on some deliveries of its event and not on others.
- Breadth-first order makes a counterexample one of the fewest steps, but that is not proved.
- The Rust explorer for large state spaces is not built yet (planned: WG-4650); it will be checked differentially against this one.
- Evaluation errors and failed steps end a path without being reported as such: a configuration whose invariant cannot be evaluated counts as a violation, and a failed step has no successor.
- Format 2 inputs are not explored yet (planned: WG-4664, WG-4715). The specified relation adds internal entries to the simultaneous-order choice, the new fault kinds to the mask, and one successor per choice value and per instance of an any route; sampled draws are fixed by a seed named first in the result, and a result is shown as verified only when the scenario has no sampled site. Scenarios with workloads need a short horizon and maxArrivals, and keyed populations of thousands of clients are beyond reach. When a timed fault acts is not explored (WG-4704).
- Temporal claims are checked without the claim's negatives and without the trace check's vacuity rules (a never whose matcher cannot occur in the scenario holds here without a note).
- A violating prefix of a never or precedes claim is reported as the counterexample, not extended to a complete run; any extension violates it too, but that is not a theorem.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `formal/Exegra/Explore.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Search.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Temporal.lean`
- Notes: formal/Exegra/Explore.lean: State (fault mask and Config, with decidable equality and hashing), successors, Reachable, the explorer explore with a Std.HashSet visited set, and the theorems step_mem_successors, explore_sound and explore_counterexample_real, whose axioms are pinned with #guard_msgs. The file sits inside formal/, which formal-semantics also declares. formal/Exegra/Search.lean: the generic breadth-first search over a node type with search_sound and search_counterexample_real. formal/Exegra/Temporal.lean: the forms, the monitor, runs (runRec), the product system, tcheck, tcheck_sound, tcheck_counterexample_real, tverdict and the exegra-lean temporal protocol; axioms pinned with #guard_msgs.

### Formal semantics (Lean 4)

The definition of the behavior language: the engine input, configurations, the expression language and the step relation, written in Lean 4 as an executable reference that the Rust engine is checked against, with its basic properties proved.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Evaluate a run to a trace in the documented format
- Prove determinism of the step function, at most one fired transition per behavior per step, and termination under the step limit
- Serve as the reference for differential checking of the Rust engine on goldens and generated inputs
- Run, as the engine of the application (engine.run and exegra-lean), the implementation Exegra.Impl (formal/Exegra/Impl/): expressions parsed at most once per run, arithmetic evaluated on unboxed floats, a step counter instead of measuring the list of steps, and an in-place JSON writer; Exegra.Impl.runAnyValue_eq proves it equal to the definition Exegra.runAnyValue on every input, so the theorems about the definition hold of what runs

#### Relationships in

- Depends on → Simulation engine: The engine is checked against the formal semantics, which defines what it must compute (planned, WG-4685).
- Depends on → Proof export: Proofs are stated against the formal semantics.
- Depends on → Exhaustive explorer: The successor relation takes the semantics' deterministic step after each choice; the explorer runs the semantics' own step function.
- Depends on → Resource model: Resources, grants, bounds and their invariants are defined in Lean first (planned, WG-4664).
- Depends on → Workload model: Arrivals, links, latency, timers and the SplitMix64 streams are defined in Lean first (planned, WG-4715).
- Depends on → Performance metrics: Metrics are defined in Lean as a function of the run (planned, WG-4715).
- Calls → Lean call boundary: Dispatches engine.run, engine.explore and engine.temporal to runAnyValue, exploreValue and temporalValue of the Lean core.
- Depends on → Integrity rules: Rules are proved in the formal/ Lean project, built with it.
- Calls → Test suites: The differential tool (tools/differential.js) runs the Lean executable and the Rust engine on the goldens and on generated inputs.

#### Known unknowns

- Format 2 is defined in formal/Exegra/Format2.lean for the constructs built so far (instances, routes, resources and resource faults, windows, workloads, links, timers, request lifecycle and measured outputs); formal/Exegra/Resource.lean proves the held-units and queue-bound invariants of a settled resource, the strict FIFO queue of a collapse included. Not defined yet: crash-instance with incarnations, meters and cost (planned: WG-4741).
- lift_agrees is stated in formal/Exegra/Lift.lean with its proof left as sorry (pinned on sorryAx); it is checked differentially by tools/differential.js --lift on every liftable golden and generated input (planned: WG-4664).
- The determinism property over seeds is not stated as a theorem for format 2 yet (planned: WG-4741).
- Exegra.Impl optimizes format 1; format 2 runs the definition's step semantics (V2.traceOf) with only the fast JSON writer, so its expressions are still parsed at every evaluation (planned: WG-4798). Exegra.Json.parse is a partial definition, shared by the definition and the implementation, so it is outside every theorem (planned: WG-4797).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `formal/`, `tools/gen-engine-inputs.js`
- Notes: A Lake project (Lean 4, toolchain pinned in formal/lean-toolchain, standard library only): Exegra/Value, Json, Number, Expr, Input, Trace and Semantics define the semantics, Exegra/Properties proves its properties, Main is the exegra-lean executable (engine input JSON on stdin, trace JSON on stdout). npm run build:formal runs lake build in formal/; formal/.lake/ is build output, ignored by git and outside the traceability surface. tools/differential.js compares the native Rust engine with exegra-lean on the goldens and on seeded inputs from tools/gen-engine-inputs.js. Exegra/Impl/ is the implementation the application runs: Impl/Expr.lean (the numeric fast evaluator, eval_numeric), Impl/Json.lean (the in-place writer, write'_eq), Impl/Semantics.lean (the compiled format-1 engine) and Impl/Refinement.lean (runAnyValue_eq and runAny_eq, axioms pinned with #guard_msgs). Expression parses are kept in Thunks: Thunk.get is Lean's memoizing primitive, logically x.fn (), and the implementation declares no implemented_by, extern or csimp of its own.

### Proof export

Exports a scenario's engine input and its state-invariant and temporal (never, precedes, eventually) claims to a Lean file whose theorems state the verified explorer's result per claim, checks the file with Lean and reports each claim as verified (for all N configurations within the bound, or on every complete run), a counterexample run, or incomplete, with the axioms the proof uses. Proving invariants for every reachable configuration at every depth, by induction, is planned (WG-4686).

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Build the scenario's engine input exactly as simulate does and collect the state-invariant and temporal claims that list the scenario and apply to it; a claim that names behaviors or an expression outside the scenario is reported not evaluable
- Write formal/generated/<scenario>.lean: the engine input text, its decoded Lean term checked against it (input_decodes), and per claim a theorem explore env (claimInv expr) bound maxStates = result, discharged by native_decide
- Check the file with Lake, loading the modules' compiled code, and report per claim the method lean-exhaustive, the bound, N, the theorem, the file, the axioms and what is trusted
- Render a counterexample as simulate shows a run: the step list with the delivery choices, the active faults, and the request-log lines of contract events
- For a temporal claim write the theorem tverdict form env bound maxStates = result (from the exegra-lean temporal protocol), whose meaning is tverdict_sound and tverdict_real, and render a violating run with the reason in the trace check's words

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Proofs are stated against the formal semantics.
- Depends on → Exhaustive explorer: Counterexamples to induction come from bounded exploration.
- Calls → Simulation contract: Builds the scenario's engine input with simulationInput, exactly as simulate does.
- Calls → Expression language: Parses claim expressions and evaluates them on a counterexample's last configuration to explain it.
- Calls → Contract conformance: Renders a counterexample's contract events as request-log lines.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Claim evaluator: Claims proved in Lean are reported as verified by proof (planned, WG-4686).
- Calls → Agent command line: The prove command explores a scenario and checks its state invariants in Lean.
- Calls → Integrity rules: prove-integrity uses the Lake runner of exegra prove (lakeAvailable, the exegra-lean binary).

#### Known unknowns

- Results are established by native_decide, so each theorem depends on an auxiliary axiom that trusts the Lean compiler and runtime. Kernel reduction (decide) is not feasible: the expression language's number literals go through Float.scaleB and the visited set hashes strings with String.hash, both opaque to the kernel.
- The unbounded proof of an invariant (by induction over the step relation, with the explorer supplying counterexamples to induction) is not built yet (planned: WG-4686).
- Claim results are reported by exegra prove only; evaluate and verify do not show them yet.
- Negatives of a claim are not explored in Lean: prove checks that a claim holds on every configuration of its scenarios, not that its negatives violate it (WG-4711).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/proof-export.js`, `tools/prove.js`, `formal/Exegra/Prove.lean`
- Notes: src/core/proof-export.js (pure) builds the exploration request, the Lean proof file and the per-claim results; tools/prove.js runs lake build, the exegra-lean explore protocol and lake env lean on the generated file (formal/generated/, ignored by git), with the modules' shared libraries (precompileModules) loaded; formal/Exegra/Prove.lean holds claimInv, decodesTo, the Lean term printer of an input and the explore protocol of exegra-lean. The command is exegra prove (cli/commands.js).

### Contract conformance

Lets an event be a request, a response or an error of a contract at a boundary, so that a simulated trace is checked and reads like a request log. Holds the type-string parser shared by the static and the trace checks.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Parse the type strings of payload fields and contract inputs and outputs (string, number, boolean, null, literals, unions, arrays); a type it cannot read is opaque and every value fits it
- Derive from a contract the fields and error codes it allows for a request, a response and an error
- Check a trace: for each step whose event is a contract event, the payload values fit the declared types, a request carries every input that is not optional, an error code is among the contract's errors, and a response or error answers an earlier unanswered request of the same contract (matched by the correlation field when the event declares one, otherwise by count); each violation is a failed automatic check carrying the step
- Render a trace as request-log lines (src/core/contract-trace.js, requestLog): the contract's binding (method and path, or operation) or else the event name for a request, 'ok' for a response and 'refused <code>' for an error; steps that are not contract events give no line
- Read traces and models only: the engine, its Lean semantics and the engine input format are untouched

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Validator: Checks the payload of every contract event against the fields, types and errors of its contract.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: checkTrace adds the automatic checks of the contract events of a trace.
- Calls → Application session: Derives the request log of the loaded run for the simulator panel.
- Calls → Agent command line: simulate lists the request log of the run.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the static and trace checks and the request log.
- Calls → Proof export: Renders a counterexample's contract events as request-log lines.

#### Known unknowns

- Object and generic types (for example {text, hash} or Operation[]) are opaque to the type check: their values are accepted unchecked.
- A response is paired with its request by one correlation field or by count; there is no timeout or ordering rule for unanswered requests at the end of a run.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/type-expr.js`, `src/core/contract-trace.js`

### Data model

Makes records, their fields, owning component, personal-data class, keys and the copies of them part of the specification, so that erasure, identity keying, shared-copy and freshness rules are computed over the model instead of read from hand-maintained tables.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Define the record kind (owner, key, fields with type, personal class, refersTo a record or the principal, role, retention) and the copy kind (of, fields, heldBy, scope, freshness, erasure, dependsOnAuthority), validated by the generic parser with positions
- Check that a record's key and a copy's fields name declared fields, and that a numeric freshness or erasure bound has a unit
- Compute the six data rules per copy, record and field: an erasure point on every copy holding a personal field or a principal reference; an erasure point or a bounded retention on every record holding a personal or credential field; no shared copy holding a principal reference, a personal field or authority-dependent data; party fields that refer to the principal by a non-name type; a freshness bound on every copy; a relationship path between a copy's holder and the owner of each record it copies
- Report every finding as a validation warning naming the entity and the field, and evaluate the data-model claim method from the same findings
- Answer the copies of a record and the fields a copy holds for the inspector and the Data projection

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Model queries: Builds the component graph to find a path between a copy's holder and a record's owner.
- Reads → Meta-model: The record and copy kinds and their enums are declared in the meta-model.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Validator: Runs the data rules and the record and copy consistency checks as part of validation.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Evaluates data-model claims from the findings of the data rules.
- Calls → Projection system: The Data projection draws records, copies, owners and holders, and marks copies with findings.
- Calls → Semantic inspector: Lists a record's fields and copies and a copy's source fields.
- Calls → Generation readiness: Reads the well-formed field declarations of a record.

#### Known unknowns

- A field counts as a name when its type text contains the word name or handle; there is no type system for field types, so a party field typed as an opaque string passes rule party-by-id (WG-4697).
- Retention is free text: nothing checks that a record's retention and its copies' erasure points agree, and a copy of a copy cannot be declared, since of names records only (WG-4697).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/data-model.js`
- Notes: The record and copy kinds are built-in meta-model kinds; the rules are hand-written in data-model.js and run from validate.js and claims.js.

### Resource model

Resources with capacity, request classes, queue disciplines and bounds, and the service demand of transitions: the part of format 2 that makes a pool, a connection limit or a CPU a queueing station.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Hold per resource its capacity, discipline (fifo or priority), request classes with priority, reserved units (at most the class limit), limit, queue bound and maximum wait, the overflow rule (refuse, drop, shed-oldest) and an optional load-dependent demand factor: a step table (the last row holds beyond it) or exact USL parameters evaluated at each grant
- Record the use, acquire and release effects in phase 2 and apply them when the step settles: release, enqueue, grant by discipline and eligibility, then class bounds in declaration order and the resource bound; holdings are tracked per instance, resource and class, and release names its class
- Draw service demand at the grant from the stream demand:<behavior>:<transition>:<effect> (effect index from 0), against the faults active at that time, and scale it once, rounding up, by the demand factor of the occupancy at the grant and by the combined scale-demand factor reduced to lowest terms (a part of 2^32 or more, or a service time of 2^53 or more, is a failed step); the service time is fixed at the grant
- Delete a request's wait-timeout entry on every path that removes it (grant, bound refusal or drop, shed, crash), so a wait-timeout step always finds its request
- Refuse statically a request whose units can never be granted, also after the fault mask is applied
- Apply scale-demand, set-capacity, set-class-limit, collapse-classes and replace-demand, untimed or within windows through fault-edge steps, the value in effect being a function of the active set (last in the input's faults order, product for scale-demand, any for collapse), without preempting held units; return waiting requests to their own classes when a collapse ends
- Apply crash-instance: an incarnation per instance, recorded on targeted, routed and from fields and on the instance's service-done and wait-timeout entries; at the crash remove timers, waiting requests with their timeouts and in-service requests with their service-done entries, release holdings, draw the restart time, broadcast notify; drop stale entries per behavior; restart edges restore only their own incarnation; a crash of a missing or down instance does nothing
- Keep held units within class limits and capacity, and waiting requests within their bounds, as invariants proved of the step; deadlock freedom is not guaranteed and is left to claims and the explorer

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Resources, grants, bounds and their invariants are defined in Lean first (planned, WG-4664).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Simulation engine: Settles resources after each step and runs service-done and wait-timeout entries (planned, WG-4664, WG-4715).
- Calls → Simulation contract: Lowers resources, classes and service demand into the version-2 engine input (planned).
- Depends on → Load aspect: Saturation and backpressure are those of the resources of format 2.

#### Contracts bound

- Engine input, format version 2 (`engine-input-v2`): provider, via `simulation-contract-calls-resource-model`

#### Known unknowns

- Built (WG-4664, WG-4729) in the Lean semantics and the Rust engine, resource faults included (set-capacity, set-class-limit, collapse-classes, scale-demand, replace-demand; untimed, or windowed through fault-edge steps), and derived from a specification's resources by simulationInput. crash-instance with incarnations is refused by both engines, and the grantable-units check is not repeated after the untimed fault mask (planned: WG-4741).
- Processor sharing is not part of format 2; CPU is a FCFS multi-server station (planned: WG-4719).
- Credit-based backpressure between components is not represented (planned: WG-4722); autoscaling and cold start are not represented (planned: WG-4720).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `formal/Exegra/Resource.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Random.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Format2.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Lift.lean`, `engine/exegra-engine/src/v2.rs`, `engine/exegra-engine/src/random.rs`
- Notes: WG-4664 part of format 2: defined in Lean (Resource, Random, Format2, Lift), ported to Rust (v2.rs, random.rs), and checked differentially on generated version-2 inputs (tools/gen-engine-inputs.js --v2, tools/differential.js). Its claims stay not evaluated until recorded evidence supports them.

### Workload model

Workloads that drive a scenario from outside its behaviors: open arrival processes (constant, Poisson, ramp, step, on/off, attack floods) with client and address identities and payload generators, closed populations with think time, populations of client behaviors, links with bandwidth, latency, ordering and fan-out, and timers.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Generate open arrivals as instants of b entries, one future instant per workload in the queue, computed from the scheduled time of the previous instant when its original first entry is taken (a delayed copy is never first, and a per-workload count of created instants creates each instant once); draw, in this order, the instant time (Poisson, spent draws included), the batch size, then per entry the client and the payload fields; stop at the horizon
- Validate phases (from < until, sorted, non-overlapping, repeat at least the last until; rates count instants) and schedule constant phases exactly, with j reset per phase and repetition, and Poisson phases (constant or ramp) by inverting the integrated rate against a unit exponential sample, with exact integer square roots and rounding to the nearest base unit: one draw per instant, no thinning and no rejection loop; refuse a phase whose mean interarrival is below 100 base units
- Attach the request id, the client and the address; group users onto addresses by a drawn group-size distribution (sizes below 1 count as 1)
- Run closed populations (issue with a workload-wide request counter, wait for the matching reply, think; a timeout ends the request with outcome timeout and a late reply is ignored) and population workloads whose clients are keyed instances of a behavior created at the start of the run
- Send emits over links: FIFO transfer with time ceil(size * perSecond / bandwidth), then at link-done the latency draw, the delivery entry and the next transfer; ordered links deliver in send order; fan-out keeps one bounded queue per receiver at the link's full bandwidth and drops its oldest waiting message, never the one in transfer
- Set, replace and cancel named timers by their stable id, so that a delayed timer is still cancelled
- Lower specification-level rates, durations and distributions to integers and tables on unbounded BigInt: the exponential and normal quantile tables defined by integer algorithms (exp and ln in 96-bit fixed point, a 192-bit fixed-point normal CDF with bisection), equal to the correctly rounded values and pinned by the SHA-256 a23cd2a9566c106701375d5cf75ef982b8db6f3e52c199c47e6239649c840950 of their canonical text; log-normal sigma at most 8; a lowered value of 2^53 base units or more is a lowering error
