#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Arrivals, links, latency, timers and the SplitMix64 streams are defined in Lean first (planned, WG-4715).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Simulation engine: Queues the next arrival of a workload when one is taken, runs link transfers and timers (planned, WG-4715).
- Calls → Simulation contract: Lowers workloads, rates, durations, distributions and links into the version-2 engine input (planned).
- Depends on → Load aspect: The load aspect drives scenarios with the workloads of format 2 (planned, WG-4668 on WG-4715).

#### Contracts bound

- Engine input, format version 2 (`engine-input-v2`): provider, via `simulation-contract-calls-workload-model`

#### Known unknowns

- Built (WG-4715, WG-4729): open, closed and population workloads, address groups, links (bandwidth, ordering, queue bounds, fan-out, latency) and timers in both engines, derived from a specification's workloads and links by simulationInput (src/core/sim-input.js).
- Packet-level networking and packet-rate floods are not represented: a link is a FIFO byte pipe with a latency distribution, one step per message (aggregated mode planned: WG-4718).
- Matchmaking gather is writable only as a hand-written behavior (primitive planned: WG-4721).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/distributions.js`, `formal/Exegra/Workload.lean`, `engine/exegra-engine/src/workload.rs`
- Notes: WG-4715, in progress. Built: the integer lowering of distributions (src/core/distributions.js; every reference output of docs/simulation.md and the pinned SHA-256 reproduced, tests/core/distributions.test.js); open workloads (constant and Poisson by inversion of the integrated rate with exact integer square roots, phases, ramps, repetition, batches, clients picked uniformly or round-robin, payload literals, samples, weighted choices and counters, maxArrivals, the first-entry rule with a per-workload count of created instants) defined in formal/Exegra/Workload.lean and Format2.lean, ported to the Rust engine (workload.rs, v2.rs) and checked differentially on generated workload inputs (tools/gen-engine-inputs.js, tools/differential.js). Proved: an instant is always before the horizon (nextTime_lt_horizon) and a workload never creates more arrival entries than maxArrivals (makeInstant_arrivals_le).

### Performance metrics

The measured outputs of a run as a pure function of its steps: throughput, outcomes, utilisation, queue length, waiting time, response-time percentiles, false positives, transition counts, link use and cost, per resource, class, link and boundary, over a declared window and in time series, all as integers; and meters, windowed measurements that behaviors read.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Follow the request lifecycle of docs/simulation.md: a request starts when its start entry is first taken as head, ends as error at once when that entry is dropped, crashed, stale or handled by no transition, ends at the first delivered end event with its key (delivered: head of a deliver step that does not fail), ends as timeout on a closed-client timeout, and is otherwise censored; count unkeyed starts, duplicate starts and late ends
- Give ok requests the sample end - start and every other outcome and censored request +infinity; count samples as measured requests started, so a lost or refused request still counts; waiting time is one sample per request enqueued, +infinity when removed without a grant
- Treat underpowered statistics by who starts the class: for open-started classes an underpowered run is left out and a null bucket skipped; otherwise both do not satisfy; time-to-recover always needs every bucket of its hold range non-null and satisfying
- Integrate piecewise-constant signals over [warmup, horizon), with the value in effect at warmup and the last value held to the horizon, and the utilisation denominator the integral of the capacity in effect
- Report nearest-rank percentiles, bucketed series on a grid that divides the window exactly, time-to-first and time-to-recover as durations t - after, and series aggregates max, min and mean; claims scoped to a phase or sub-window
- Compute meters over (t - window, t] from the start-of-step configuration, with null for an empty window or a zero denominator and +infinity samples counted in quantile meters
- Compute cost as counted quantities times exact rational prices, per price, in total and per bucket, for the cost aspect (WG-4669) to report
- Mark a run truncated when it ended at the step limit, in error, or stopped with a measured request open

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Metrics are defined in Lean as a function of the run (planned, WG-4715).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Simulation engine: Accumulates the measured outputs of a run, in trace and metrics output modes (planned, WG-4715).
- Reads → Metric claims: Takes one statistic per run from the measured outputs (planned).
- Reads → Cost aspect: Prices the counted quantities and reports cost per run, per bucket and per month (planned, WG-4669 on WG-4715).

#### Contracts bound

- Trace, format version 2 (`trace-v2`): provider, via `simulation-engine-calls-performance-metrics`

#### Known unknowns

- Built (WG-4715, WG-4729): resource metrics; request classes with the request lifecycle, response time, outcomes, throughput, legitimate refusals (false positives) and transition firings, over the window and per series bucket, in both engines. Not built: link and cost metrics, waiting time per class, resource signals per bucket, time-to-first and time-to-recover, meters, and truncation by an open measured request (planned: WG-4741). Warm-up is declared, not detected.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Notes: WG-4715, in progress. Built: the resource metrics of format 2 (utilisation as busy and capacity integrals and max held, queue length as integral and max, waiting time per request enqueued in the window with +infinity for a request removed without a grant or still waiting, nearest-rank quantiles at the input's exact decimals), defined over the step sequence in formal/Exegra/Format2.lean (Measured outputs), accumulated step by step in the Rust engine (v2.rs, Measure), output with the trace or alone with output metrics, and checked differentially on generated workload inputs. The request lifecycle, response time, throughput, outcomes, series, time-to metrics, meters and cost are not built.

### Metric claims

Claims over measured outputs, evaluated over many seeded runs with exact binomial and sign tests, reported with their sample count, seeds and confidence as model results (model-holds), never as verified or supported, and validated against recorded load runs.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Declare the claim method metric-bound: scenarios, metric (kind, subject, statistic, outcomes, window, series aggregate), op and bound, approx, or relativeTo another metric with its own window, fraction, confidence, runs, base seed, negatives (on the claim's scenarios or on variants declaring variantOf), control negatives and calibratedBy
- Run the scenario once per seed, run r using the r-th SplitMix64 output of the base seed (mod 2^64), in metrics mode, and take one statistic per run; a truncated run does not satisfy the bound; an underpowered run is left out when only open workloads start the class and counts as not satisfying otherwise; the test uses the thresholds of the N' runs kept
- Decide model-holds, fails or inconclusive by exact rational binomial tails with N fixed in advance (the minimum N is the smallest with beta^N <= alpha, compared exactly); for approx, use the sign-test order-statistic interval of the median of the per-run statistic, which needs at least 6 runs at 95%
- Report model-holds (never supported or verified) with the scope "model only; parameters as stated", runs, seeds, passes, N', fraction, confidence, the spread of the per-run statistic and the underpowered and truncated runs
- Require each negative to fail and each control negative to hold before reporting model-holds; a base scenario that fails makes the claim violated; fixture results test the engine and method and are not evidence about any system
- Check calibrations on hold-out load runs distinct from the fitting runs, with equal workload, capacities and window, a band widened over repeats, at least 6 kept runs and no truncated run, tolerance no wider than the margin computed from the beta-quantile of the per-run statistic, evidence not expired at the recorded asOf date and bound to the current component revisions; report unvalidated otherwise, and extrapolated outside the convex hull (exact rational linear programming) of the tested points with the same fault set
- Vary every assumed and to-be-measured parameter over its declared range, one at a time and then pairwise among the flagged ones, and list first what flips the verdict
- Refuse at validation a run count that could never hold, and a quantile or mean claim whose expected samples from declared open-workload rates are fewer than 30, per bucket for series claims

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs the claim's scenarios once per seed in metrics mode (planned, WG-4715).
- Reads → Performance metrics: Takes one statistic per run from the measured outputs (planned).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Claim evaluator: Metric-bound claims are evaluated by the metric claims component (planned, WG-4715).
- Calls → Validator: Validates metric-bound claims with the statistics of src/core/metric-stats.js.

#### Contracts bound

- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): consumer, via `metric-claims-calls-simulation-engine`

#### Known unknowns

- Built (WG-4715, WG-4729): the metric-bound method is registered and evaluated on format-2 scenarios derived from a specification, reporting model-holds, violated or inconclusive with N, the passes, the fraction and the confidence, with negatives and control negatives. Not built: calibration, sensitivity, the operating envelope and revision binding (asOf is only recorded) (planned: WG-4741).
- The status model-holds is implemented: it is a ClaimResult status of src/core/claims.js and the verdict of src/core/metric-stats.js, and metric-bound claims report it. Its final name and display follow WG-4703.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/metric-stats.js`, `src/core/metric-claims.js`
- Notes: Built: the statistics (src/core/metric-stats.js: exact binomial tails and verdicts, the minimum run count, passes to hold and to fail, runs to decide, the sign-test interval, underpowered statistics, the margin, run seeds and the claim status with negatives; tests/core/metric-stats.test.js) and the metric-bound runner over engine inputs (src/core/metric-claims.js: one metrics-mode run per run seed through runEngineText, on the thread that evaluates claims, which in the application is the main thread). The method is registered and simulationInput derives format 2 for it. Calibration, sensitivity, asOf and revision binding are not built (WG-4741).

### Traffic recorder

Records the events a running Exegra really produces, named by the event ids of this specification (requests and answers of the persistence client with the development server's own events, change notices, engine-worker messages, commits, claim evaluations, simulator controls, preference and camera storage writes), as a bounded stream with logical sequence numbers, so that the stream can be replayed through this specification (component self-model-replay) and shown in the simulator panel's Live mode.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Keep the last 5000 recorded events in memory as {seq, id, payload, at}: seq is the logical sequence number (1, 2, 3, ...) and the only time the replay and the claim evaluator see; at is performance.now(), kept in the application layer for display and stripped before anything reaches the core (src/app/traffic-recorder.js, forReplay); older events are dropped and counted
- Hand out request ids (nextRequestId), the correlation field requestId of the contract events of this specification; the persistence client sends it as x-exegra-request-id and the development server stamps its own events with it
- Receive events from the producers at the code locations the event descriptions name: src/app/main.js (page, engine load, camera), src/app/session.js (project open, list and read, commits, saves and their answers, external changes, claim evaluation, simulator controls, preference writes), src/app/engine-runner.js (worker messages), src/persist/client.js (requests, the server events of each answer, change notices), src/ui/toolbar.js (export, import), src/ui/realisation-panel.js (generate), src/ui/layout.js (layout preference writes)
- Record texts and bundles as their sha-256 (src/core/sha256.js), as this specification represents them, never the text itself
- Record a write to a store that leaves the store's modelled abstraction unchanged (the selection and view of exegra.session, whether the explorer is open in exegra.layout) as nothing: a stutter step of the self-model

#### Relationships out

- Observes → Persistence client: The persistence client records each request (with its requestId) through the observer the session installs (observeTraffic), then the development server's events returned with the answer.
- Observes → Development-server plugin: The development server returns its own events (ws-*, the answer, inferred external edits) in the x-exegra-trace header of each answer and as trace messages of the change stream; the persistence client appends them.
- Observes → Simulation engine: src/app/engine-runner.js records engine-run-posted, worker-run-done, engine-run-returned or engine-run-refused, worker-terminated and worker-load-failed through the record callback the session passes to createEngineRunner, so the engine runner imports nothing of the application.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application session: Records the session's events, installs the persistence client's observer and reads the stream for Live mode.
- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: src/app/main.js records page-loaded, the engine load, session-init-requested, camera events and step-pulsed.
- Calls → Responsive layout: src/ui/layout.js records panel-toggled and layout-preferences-written.

#### Known unknowns

- The stream is kept in memory only and is lost on reload; there is no durable record under specs/<project>/.history/ (WG-4780).
- The development server tells its own writes from external edits by the hashes it wrote. A change written by the server for another tab is therefore not seen as an external edit by this tab, and the replay reports its change notice as unpredicted (WG-4781).
- Command-line runs (cli/commands.js) record nothing: the stream covers the browser application and the development server only.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/app/traffic-recorder.js`
- Notes: Built (WG-4777). The producers live in the modules they observe; the server-side events are produced by tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js and returned with each answer.

### Self-model replay

Replays a recorded stream of real events through the behaviors of a scenario of this specification (live-session), one event at a time, and reports every divergence between what the running application did and what the self-model allows.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Execute the behaviors of the scenario with the step semantics of the engine (phase 1 choice reading the configuration before the step, phase 2 effects in scenario order, the expression language of src/core/expr.js, the engine input of src/core/sim-input.js), delivering the recorded events in recorded order with their recorded payloads; logical time is the recorded sequence number
- Keep the events the model emits as predictions instead of queueing them: a recorded event that some transition of the scenario emits must match a pending prediction of the same event (by its correlation field or requestId when both carry it, else the oldest); any interleaving of predictions that respects causality is accepted, because a real run interleaves independent chains (a network answer, a file-watch notice, a claim evaluation) in whatever order they happen
- Report divergences under the claim real-runs-replay-through-self-model: an event that is not an event of the specification (unknown-event), an event the model emits but did not predict here (unpredicted), a predicted event that no behavior consuming it accepts in its current state (not-allowed), a recorded payload value that differs from the predicted one (payload; correlation fields and values the transition gives as number literals, which stand for counts the model does not track, are not compared; nor are arrays and objects), a prediction not matched within 200 recorded events or by the end of a closed stream (missing), and an expression of the model that fails on the recorded payload (error)
- Calibrate instead of diverging when a predicted value reads a variable that no transition has written since the start of the stream: its declared initial value stands for the environment when the stream started (a file hash on disk, a stored preference), so it takes the recorded value; the step lists the calibration
- Return the replay as a trace (steps with fired, ignored, responsible, configuration, the pending predictions and the divergences of each step) that checkTrace, requestLog, the simulator panel and the scene read like a simulated one

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Simulation contract: Reads the behaviors of the scenario from the engine input (simulationInput).
- Calls → Expression language: Evaluates guards, effect values and emitted payloads.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application session: Live mode replays the recorded stream through the scenario live-session (createReplay, replayChecks).

#### Known unknowns

- The engine itself is not driven: it runs a scenario to quiescence and delivers simultaneous events in queue order, so the replay reimplements one engine step in JavaScript without faults. Agreement with the engine is by construction from the same engine input and expression language, not checked against the Rust engine.
- A stream that starts mid-session (after the recorder dropped events, past 5000) is replayed from the declared initial configuration; divergences near its start can come from the missing prefix.
- Divergences a longer real session still shows (WG-4782): the camera saved after a view switch with nothing stored is the camera the fit animation produced, not the last camera the model tracked (camera-keep); a restored camera is saved again with its last digit changed by the view's round trip (camera-apply); a commit that changes only comments or formatting re-evaluates no claims, while claims-on-commit predicts an evaluation for every change of the specification text (missing claims-evaluation-requested); a terminated engine worker never finishes its run, while engine-worker-flow still emits worker-run-done for it (missing).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/replay.js`

### Lean call boundary

The one way the JavaScript shell calls the Lean core: a JSON request {fn, args} to a JSON reply {ok} or {error}, through the Lean core compiled to WebAssembly.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Keep the registry of callable Lean functions (Exegra.Api.registry): each module port lists its functions in its own file under formal/Exegra/Api/ and appends the list; the C glue, the exported symbol and the JS binding never change
- Answer every request with a reply: an unknown function, a malformed request, a refusal and a trap are errors as values, and a trap discards the instance
- Load the module synchronously in Node and after an awaited fetch in the browser or a worker (src/core/lean.js)
- Build the module reproducibly from pinned inputs (npm run build:formal-wasm): emscripten 7.0.0, the Lean runtime at the toolchain tag with the committed patch, libuv v1.48.0, Init/Std C from the toolchain, Exegra C from lake; wasm64 only

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Dispatches engine.run, engine.explore and engine.temporal to runAnyValue, exploreValue and temporalValue of the Lean core.
- Calls → Model derivation: Dispatches model.derive to Exegra.Model.deriveValue.
- Calls → Normal form and hash: Dispatches hash.semantic and hash.normalize to the semantic hash and the normal form.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs every engine input as the Lean function engine.run.
- Calls → Parser: Derives every model with the Lean function model.derive.
- Calls → Semantic hash binding: Hashes and normalizes a model that does not carry its hash with hash.semantic and hash.normalize.

#### Known unknowns

- Stable Safari does not support Memory64, so the Lean core, and with it the engine, does not run there until Safari ships it (decision 2026-11-18). A wasm32 build would need its C from a 32-bit Lean compiler (spike/lean-wasm/README.md, wasm32 caveats).
- The Lean engine is still slower than the Rust engine it replaced on the goldens, now mostly in reading JSON. Measured 2026-11-18 in Node 24 on Windows 10 (wasm64, best of 10 runs): live-session 17.0 ms (was 21.5 ms; Rust 1.95 ms), app-start-and-open 14.1 ms (was 24.1 ms; Rust 2.4 ms). Natively about two thirds of that is Exegra.Json.parse, a partial definition nothing can be proved about yet; a fast parser proved equal to a total definitional parser is WG-4797. Format 2 runs the definition's step semantics, with its expressions parsed at every evaluation (WG-4798). Loading and initializing the module takes about 119 ms once per process.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `formal/Exegra/Api.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Api/`, `formal/wasm/`, `src/core/lean.js`, `tools/build-formal-wasm.js`
- Notes: The Lean side (formal/Exegra/Api.lean, formal/Exegra/Api/) is the logic; src/core/lean.js and formal/wasm/glue.c are glue in the trusted base. Built into src/engine/lean/ (generated, not committed); downloads and checkout-independent objects are cached under %LOCALAPPDATA%\exegra\cache\formal-wasm (EXEGRA_CACHE_DIR overrides).

### Rust engine (differential oracle)

The former simulation engine, the Rust crate engine/exegra-engine, kept as an independent implementation the Lean engine is compared against: as WebAssembly in the conformance tests and as the native exegra-sim in the differential check.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Responsibilities

- Reproduce every golden trace, as WebAssembly (tools/rust-engine.js) and as the native binary
- Give byte-identical traces to the Lean engine on the goldens and on generated inputs (tests/core/conformance.test.js, tests/core/formal-semantics.test.js)

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Test suites: The conformance and differential tests compare the Lean engine with the Rust engine.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `engine/`, `tools/build-engine.js`, `tools/rust-engine.js`

### Register importer

A command line tool that converts one large Markdown specification file, written as headings, prose and register tables, into the specification file of a linked project: every table row becomes an entity of a kind the import map declares (or a generic table row), and every entity names the line it came from.

- Category: Infrastructure
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Responsibilities

- Read one Markdown file and the project's import map (specs/<project>/import-map.jsonc with "format": "register"), which declares the entity kinds, the table rules (header cells, optional heading scope, the id column or id prefix, a property per column, reference columns), component categories and aliases, and which identifier patterns count as mentions
- Hold every line: headings become sections whose bodies keep prose verbatim, and each imported table row and table header is replaced in its section body by one {{entity:<id>}} marker line; write nothing unless the file rebuilds line for line with each marker expanded to the lines it came from
- Make every row entity render back to its cells: a row rendered from its properties must give the same cells as the source row, or the import fails naming the entity
- Give every imported entity a source property (path:line in the source repository) and, when the row carries an identifier, a ref property with the identifier as written; identifiers and citations named in a row become its mentions (references to imported entities) and citations (as written)
- Record what it recognised but could not map (a row whose cell count differs from its header, an unresolved mention, a duplicate identifier, a reference column naming no entity) as losses with counts and examples, printed and written into the generated specification's system notes
- Merge the draft fragments the map lists ("drafts"): authored entities that are not from the Markdown, each given origin "draft" and the file and line of its declaration as source, citing in basis the imported entities it answers; drafts never change the Markdown the sections render

#### Relationships out

- Reads → Markdown specification: Reads the Markdown file and the repository's git commit; it never writes there.
- Data flow → Specification file: The generated text of the project's specification file travels to the file, deterministically, with the losses recorded in its system notes.
- Writes → Specification file: Writes the project's specification file directly with node:fs, bypassing the transaction system.
- Depends on → jsonc-parser: Reads the import map and re-reads the generated text with the library.
- Calls → Markdown importer: Reuses the Markdown importer's text helpers, loss collection, coverage comparison and git commit reader.
- Calls → File system: Reads the Markdown file and the import map, and writes or compares the generated file, with node:fs.

#### Relationships in

- Data flow → Markdown specification: The headings, prose and register tables of the Markdown file travel to the importer, one way.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the register importer on a fixture and analyze its output.

#### Known unknowns

- It writes the project's specification file directly with node:fs, not through the workspace service or the transaction system, like the Markdown importer; once a map says "authority": "jsonc" it refuses to import over that file (only --out writes elsewhere), so edits made in Exegra are not overwritten.
- It does not analyze or validate what it writes; that the output analyzes without errors is checked by the tests and by exegra validate.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `tools/import-register.js`
- Notes: Run by hand when the Markdown file changes; one way, as the Markdown importer.

### External realisation

Lets a project be realised in other repositories: reads the realisation roots a project's project.jsonc names, checks modules declared in a root against that root, and turns a root's JUnit reports into pass, fail or not-run results for test evidence that points into it.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Tooling (`tooling`)

#### Responsibilities

- Resolve the roots of project.jsonc (alias, path relative to the project directory or absolute, include and exclude globs, test runs, environmental patterns) and refuse a malformed declaration
- Check a module written <alias>:<path> against that root, report a module naming an undeclared alias as missing, and report unclaimed files only for a root that declares include globs
- Through `verify`: run each test command a root declares (unless --ingest-only), then read its JUnit report, and record each external test evidence as pass, fail or not-run by matching file, describe path and test name
- Record a test as not-run, never as failed, when it is absent from the report, skipped, reported as an error, or failed with a message matching the root's environmental patterns

#### Relationships out

- Calls → File system: Checks modules and scans files in a root; reads JUnit reports.
- Calls → Root test runner: verify spawns the test command a root declares, without a shell, unless --ingest-only.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Agent command line: traceability, generate and verify resolve the project's realisation roots; verify records external test evidence from their JUnit reports.
- Calls → Verification pipeline: The traceability check delegates modules that name a root, and the scan of a root's include globs.

#### Known unknowns

- Only JUnit XML is read; a root whose runner cannot write JUnit cannot provide test evidence.
- Results are not tied to the commit of the root they were recorded from (WK-393 follow-up).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `tools/realisation-roots.js`, `tools/junit.js`

### Root test runner

The test runner of a realisation root (bun test, for example), started by `exegra verify` through the command project.jsonc declares for that root, writing a JUnit report.

- Category: External system

#### Responsibilities

- Run a root's tests and write a JUnit XML report

#### Relationships in

- Calls → External realisation: verify spawns the test command a root declares, without a shell, unless --ingest-only.

### Integrity rules

Declarative consistency rules over a whole specification (the built-in integrityRule kind), with derived relations defined by recursive clauses (the built-in integrityRelation kind, stratified Datalog with a least-model meaning), evaluated on every edit with counterexamples, enforced by the transaction system as a ratchet, and proved or refuted in Lean with kernel-checked proofs.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Parse a rule of the rule language: forall and exists over the entities of some kinds, all entities, or the values of a term, with an optional where filter; property access that follows references through lists; kindof; ==, !=, in, not in, nonempty; !, &&, || and =>; string, number and boolean literals and literal sets; and atoms rel(t1, ..., tn) of derived relations. No arithmetic, and relations range over finite candidate tuples, so evaluation is total
- Check a rule statically: every variable bound, every kind declared, every property declared on some kind; an invalid rule is a validation error
- Check the derived relations (the built-in integrityRelation kind: params ranging over entity kinds, clauses in the rule language) and stratify them: the strata are the strongly connected components of the dependency graph in topological order, and within a component every atom of the component occurs positively (! flips, the left side of => flips, a forall filter flips, an exists filter does not). Negative recursion (integrity.relation-unstratified), a wrong number of arguments (integrity.relation-arity), an unknown relation (integrity.relation-unknown), a free variable that is not a parameter (integrity.relation-unbound), a parameter kind that does not exist (integrity.relation-kind), a clause that does not parse (integrity.relation-syntax), malformed parameters (integrity.relation-params) and more than 100000 candidate tuples (integrity.relation-size) are errors that make the relation, every relation that uses it and every rule that uses it invalid
- Compute each valid relation as its least model over the product of its parameter domains: stratum by stratum, lowest first, iterating the clauses from the empty table until nothing is added; only the strata of the relations the rules use are computed, each at most once per model; an atom holds when some choice of one value from each argument term is a tuple of the relation
- Evaluate every rule of a model once (memoized per model): holds with the number of instances checked under its leading universal quantifiers, or violated with every counterexample as variable bindings with entity ids and lines; a rule whose quantifier ranges over nothing is reported vacuous
- Report each violation as a diagnostic of the rule severity at the first entity it names (integrity.violated), left out of the blocking error count
- Tell the transaction system which violations of error rules a transaction adds (the ratchet): a violation is identified by its rule and its bindings
- Export a rule to Lean: only the entities it can reach and the properties it reads (through the clauses of the relations it uses, and the domains of their parameters), every value interned to a natural number, the entities whose ids are the kinds of exported entities when a formula takes kindof, the entities as a balanced search tree, the program of the relations it uses in stratum order and their tables; write formal/generated/integrity/<rule>.lean with prog_wf (stratified), prog_solved (the checker reaches this table) and holds (proof by reflection) or one refutation per witness, each by decide +kernel and each about Sat under the least-model meaning Least
- Run exegra prove-integrity: the Lean checker (exegra-lean integrity) must find the program stratified and solved, its relation tables must equal the JavaScript tables, and it must agree with the JavaScript evaluator on the verdict and every counterexample; Lean must accept the file, and every theorem may depend on the standard axioms only; each Lean process runs under a time limit (--timeout, default 300 s) and a rule whose relations have more than --max-candidates candidate tuples (default 500) is not sent to the kernel; otherwise, or past a limit, the rule is incomplete

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Proof export: prove-integrity uses the Lake runner of exegra prove (lakeAvailable, the exegra-lean binary).
- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Rules are proved in the formal/ Lean project, built with it.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Validator: Reports invalid rules, vacuous rules and every current violation as diagnostics.
- Calls → Transaction system: Project.apply refuses a transaction that adds a violation of an error rule (the ratchet).
- Calls → Agent command line: The integrity and prove-integrity commands.
- Calls → Claim strength: Parses integrity rules to find the kinds they quantify over.

#### Known unknowns

- Property access reads top-level properties of entities only: a nested object property such as implementation.status or evaluate.method cannot be addressed (follow-up of WK-396).
- Entity ids written as literals in a rule are not references: a rename does not rewrite them and a delete does not see them (follow-up of WK-396).
- Undo, redo and reload are not checked by the ratchet: they restore or adopt text that was valid before, and may bring back a violation.
- The Lean facts are exported by JavaScript: that they are the specification is checked differentially (exegra-lean integrity against the evaluator), not proved.
- Derived relations are evaluated by naive iteration: every candidate tuple of the product of the parameter domains, each clause through the general formula evaluator, with no semi-naive evaluation and no indexing of atoms. Relations above 100000 candidates are refused, and a relation over Exegra's own relationships and components would slow every edit, so Exegra's own specification declares none yet; the examples are in tests/fixtures/integrity/relations.jsonc (WG-5267).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/integrity.js`, `src/core/integrity-export.js`, `tools/prove-integrity.js`, `formal/Exegra/Integrity.lean`
- Notes: formal/Exegra/Integrity.lean: Facts (a search tree), Term, Formula (with relation atoms), Sat under an interpretation (the meaning), eval and eval_iff, refutes and refutes_sound, Facts.find_complete, cex; derived relations: Program, Least (the impredicative least-model meaning, stratum by stratum), solve (the fixpoint checker with fuel), wf and posF (stratification, decided by the kernel), sat_mono, stratum_closed, stratum_le_closed, least_eq_table, holds_of_eval and violated_of_refutes; the exegra-lean integrity protocol; axioms pinned with #guard_msgs. The commands are exegra integrity and exegra prove-integrity (cli/commands.js).

### Reader pages

Derives the page of a reader view from the model and the view's reader blocks: text, counts by a classified property, tables and lists with grouping, facets and conditions, document sections with the tables their markers place, and an index of kinds. Knows no particular specification.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Resolve each block of view.reader.blocks into plain page data whose every row, count and section is an entity of the model
- Classify a text property by the block's rules (first matching pattern wins) into a value, a label and a tone
- Follow dotted paths through references and nested objects for grouping, columns, facets and conditions
- Split a section body into prose and the tables its {{entity:id}} markers place, and list a document's sections in reading order
- Give a view without blocks the system's description, an index of its kinds and the outline of its first document, and give each kind a page of its own

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Model queries: Uses own() for keys the specification supplies.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Reader surface: Derives the page of the current reader view, a kind's page and section bodies.

#### Known unknowns

- Pages are derived in full on every scene change; a very large kind is cut to the first 300 matching rows on the surface, not paged.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/reader.js`

### Reader surface

Shows the page of the current reader view in place of the scene, with the scene's controls and overlay hidden, and turns every entity on it into a link that selects the entity through the session, so the inspector shows and edits it.

- Category: Client
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Replace the scene while the current view uses the reader projection and give it back otherwise
- Draw text, counts, tables, lists, document outlines and sections, and the index of kinds with a page per kind
- Keep each view's facets, text filter, show-all choice, open kind and open section while the model changes
- Mark the selected entity on the page without redrawing it, and redraw on scene and project events
- Link refs written in prose (an entity's ref property) to their entities

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Reader pages: Derives the page of the current reader view, a kind's page and section bodies.
- Calls → Application session: Selects entities; reads the model, the views, the current view and the scene's projection.
- Subscribes → Application session: Redraws on scene and project events; marks the selection on selection events.
- Calls → DOM helpers: Builds its elements with the shared helpers.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: Mounts the reader surface.

#### Known unknowns

- Filters live only in memory; they are not remembered across reloads.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/ui/reader.js`

### Model derivation

Derives the semantic model from the specification's JSON document, in the verified kernel: the effective meta-model, the entities by kind with their data, every reference and expression, references resolved against the entities, and the meta-model's structural checks, with the diagnostic codes and messages of the JavaScript parser it replaced. Every location is a raw path into the document, which the parser maps to a source position.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Build the effective meta-model from the built-in meta-model and the vocabulary section (categories, relationship kinds, entity kinds with their sections and properties, properties on existing kinds, verification methods), reporting malformed, unknown and duplicate definitions
- Check every section, entity and property against the effective meta-model and report each violation with its code, message, JSON path and raw path
- Index every reference (properties of type ref, effects, claim evaluation parameters) and every expression with its JSON path, and resolve the references against the entities by id and kind
- Follow the JavaScript semantics the parser had exactly: a map of members per object read on every access (property.duplicate each time), getNodeValue for entity data and the vocabulary, String() and template text of arbitrary JSON values
- Prove what the model guarantees: with no reference diagnostic every reference names an entity of an allowed kind (Exegra.Model.derive_refs_resolve), and model.derive answers every argument (Exegra.Api.call_model_derive_total)

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Normal form and hash: Computes the semantic hash of every derived model.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Lean call boundary: Dispatches model.derive to Exegra.Model.deriveValue.

#### Known unknowns

- Two differences from the JavaScript parser where Lean strings cannot follow: a lone surrogate in a string becomes U+FFFD before the call, and the label of a declared entity kind is lower-cased in ASCII only in the entity.type and id.missing messages. The JSON reply carries -0 as 0 and a number beyond the double range as null; the parser then takes entity data and the vocabulary from the syntax tree.
- Recursion over the document is bounded by fuel (twice the document depth plus 8); no theorem states that the bound is never reached, the differential test (tests/core/model-kernel.test.js) is the evidence that it is not.
- The id check implements the pattern ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*(-[a-z0-9_]+)*$ of src/core/metamodel.json in Lean; it does not read idPattern from the meta-model it is given (it only quotes it in the message).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `formal/Exegra/Model.lean`, `formal/Exegra/ModelProps.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Api/Model.lean`
- Notes: Called by the parser (src/core/parse.js) as model.derive with {spec, metamodel, methods}: the document with every object as {"o": [[key, value], ...]} (members in source order, repeated keys kept), the meta-model and the claim method registry as data. The reply carries diagnostics and the model with raw paths ("3.0.12") and parts (node, key, start) for every location, and the semantic hash of the model. Differential evidence against the JavaScript it replaced: tests/core/model-kernel.test.js with tests/oracle/parse-reference.js.

### Stable serialization and text digests

Deterministic JSON text for written files and command results (object keys sorted at every level) and SHA-256 of texts for recorded events. Output presentation, outside the verdict path.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Stable JSON stringification with sorted keys for generated files, traces and command results
- SHA-256 of a text as lowercase hex, equal to the workspace's hash of the UTF-8 bytes

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application session: Hashes the spec and layout text with sha256Hex for the recorded events.
- Calls → Toolbar: Hashes the specification of an exported or imported bundle for the recorded events.
- Calls → Realisation panel: Hashes the generated files for the recorded events.
- Calls → Agent command line: Serialises results deterministically.
- Calls → Artifact generators: Writes generated files with sorted keys.
- Calls → Simulation contract: Writes the goldens with sorted keys (tools/record-goldens.js).
- Calls → Test suites: The golden recorder writes its traces with the stable JSON serializer.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/stable-json.js`, `src/core/sha256.js`

### Semantic hash binding

The JavaScript side of the semantic hash: passes a model that does not carry its hash to the Lean hash function through the call boundary, and returns the hash and the normal form to the callers of src/core/hash.js.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Call hash.semantic and hash.normalize at the Lean call boundary with the top-level declarations of a model that was not derived by parseSpec
- Return the hash Lean computed with the model for a model derived by parseSpec, without recomputing it

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Lean call boundary: Hashes and normalizes a model that does not carry its hash with hash.semantic and hash.normalize.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Transaction system: Computes the semantic hash of each committed model and the text hash of a project.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Stamps every result with the semantic hash it was computed from.
- Calls → Artifact generators: Stamps artifacts with the semantic hash.
- Calls → Specification checker: Prints the semantic hash.

#### Known unknowns

- Not in the verdict path in the strict sense: a claim's result is stamped with this binding's hash, so a binding error would misstamp a result without changing a verdict. The stamp is compared with the Lean-derived hash only by the callers that use it (for example transactions' expectHash checks).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/hash.js`
- Notes: src/core/hash.js is the binding only; it holds no hash logic. It imports the call boundary (src/core/lean.js) and nothing else from src/core.

### Generation readiness

Says what a code generator needs to know about data and interfaces (declared types, record constraints and schema versions, contract details and hand-written holes, the realisation of every requirement) and about the cross-cutting patterns requirements repeat (policies, validations, retentions, quotas, retries, audits) and about the user interface and its journeys (screens, forms, actions, live bindings, user journeys), and checks it: consistency rules that always apply, and readiness rules that are errors only when the specification sets system.generation.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)
- Owner: semantic-core

#### Responsibilities

- Check the type entities (representation, enum values, struct fields), the record constraints (set-null needs optional, unique and index fields are declared, changes list versions 1 to schemaVersion) and that a construction requirement is named in the models property of some claim or names a construct in realisedBy, always as errors
- Compute the readiness gaps (codes ready.*) of records, contracts, events, requirements and cross-cutting constructs, report them as validation errors when system.generation is set and list them for the readiness command otherwise
- Give each type name the class an expression can use it as (bool, number, string, bytes, struct, list) and type an expression from the classes of its references, with the operator rules the evaluator applies
- Check the cross-cutting constructs (policy, validation, retention, quota, retry, audit): exactly one target, references that resolve to declared inputs, outputs, payload and record fields, expressions that parse, stay in scope and type against the declared fields, durations, positive limits and attempts, rule parameters that fit the rule and the target
- Check the user interface constructs (screen, form, action, liveBinding, uiScenario): route patterns that are well formed and unique, view-model inputs supplied by route parameters, form fields and action arguments that fill declared contract inputs with fitting controls and well-typed expressions over route parameters, view-model outputs and the principal, navigations that give exactly the target route parameters, refresh paths into the view model, and journey steps of one known kind each that name existing screens, forms, fields, events and browsers and select by WAI-ARIA role and non-empty accessible name only
- Check the journey steps of user journeys and per-row actions: element selectors by role, exact or patterned accessible name, heading level and within; page title, attribute, count and absent-text expectations; raw-path and tenant-host navigation, reload, back and no-reload markers; request logs, direct API calls, cookie checks and replays; captured values defined before every ${name} use and captured once; emulation, overflow and placement checks; timeouts; and per-row actions over a view-model list of a declared struct, whose labels read only fields of the rows
- Check the third set of user interface constructs: select and radio options taken from a view-model list (optionsFrom), at most one catch-all screen with no address of its own, navigation addresses and parameters that read query.<param> and form.<field>, live-binding conditions over the event payload and the view model (expressions, {value, in} membership, any and all), toast placeholders that read names in scope shown as text, derived form fields (derive, until edited, slug transform, no cycles), and action results kept in declared screen-local values of the same type instead of navigating
- Check telemetry: the services of system.telemetry, each event's logging (an allowlist of payload fields that exist, are scalars, are neither marked sensitive nor of a sensitive type, and are written under names of their own, by a declared service under a unique log name, or not logged with a reason), that every domain event states its logging when system.generation is set, and that a journey's expectLog step names a logged event, its service and members of its line only
- Check the fourth set of user interface constructs: request encoding on contract inputs (trim, split, as, empty), form initial values (a path, or the first item of a list whose field equals an expression), field normalization, second calls (then), view-model overlays on success, refusal prefixes and announcements, action confirmation announcements, alsoOn on every screen of one authentication, and error messages (by check kind or error code, placeholders that fit their kind, no two messages wording the same thing for the same forms and actions), with the default refusal message as a readiness gap when generation is set
- Check that every value a scenario feeds a machine (a step payload, an inject-event fault payload) has the type its event declares: uuid format, integer bounds, enum values, required and undeclared fields, option fields (scenario.input-type, an error when system.generation is set, a warning otherwise)
- Check the metadata model (src/core/tenant-data.js): once a type has metamodel tenant-schema every record states its scope, platform or tenant-meta (record.scope-missing); a record that names a tenant object type or carries tenant field values (an object-id or values type, or a map keyed by a field-id type) is a tenant-meta record and never a fixed platform record for tenant domain data (record.tenant-data-fixed); a tenant-meta record, and only it, is described by the tenant-schema type (record.described-by)
- Check the language entities (src/core/language.js), and evaluate, check and print their expressions for function examples with the kernel's results, error texts and Display (src/core/language-eval.js, pinned by the shared case table tests/fixtures/language/kernel-cases.json): an expression language users of the specified system write, stated as an AST union, a value union, shapes, scopes and operators each bound to a primitive of the fixed catalogue pinned to the Strata kernel; operator names unique (language.operator-duplicate), no word or symbol claimed twice (language.token-clash), tokens of the right lexical class for their form and forms the primitive is written in (language.syntax), precedence and associativity on binary and prefix operators only, one associativity per level (language.precedence), primitives from the catalogue (language.primitive-unknown), operands agreeing with the primitive arity and the AST variant (language.arity), shapes declared (language.shape-unknown), null handling equal to the primitive's (language.nulls), operators naming variants and cases of the AST with at most one per variant or case (language.variant), scopes well formed and read (language.scope); with system.generation every AST variant and case has an operator and every operator a primitive (ready.language-variant-uncovered, ready.language-primitive-missing)
- Check the function entities (src/core/function.js, src/core/function-syntax.js, src/core/function-value.js, src/core/language-eval.js): pure functions with typed parameters, a return type (the built-in unit for a function that gives nothing), an optional error union, lets and a body in the function body language (the guard language with field access, if, let, match, struct and union constructors, list literals, the unit value (), the JSON null where json is expected, variants and fields named like keywords written after a dot, as patterns and before a colon, map, filter, find, any, all, fold, sortBy (by a number, string, bool, enum value or a list, option, struct or union of those, ordered as Rust's derived Ord: lists item by item), take, concat, keys, values, get, insert, remove, format with the {} (Display), {:?} (Rust's derived Debug) and {:json} (serde_json text) placeholders, eval (with an optional lookup that following a reference reads), check and print of a declared language, the string operations startsWith, endsWith, length, trim, chars, snakeCase, parseTime and the ASCII character classes, call, fail and try); bodies parse (function.expr-syntax), name what exists (function.expr-reference) and type against the declared types (function.expr-type), matches are exhaustive (function.match-exhaustive), the call graph is acyclic but for structural self-recursion on a strict sub-value of a union parameter (function.recursion), fail and try use the declared error union (function.error-unknown), parameters, returns and lets are well formed (function.param), and every example is evaluated in JavaScript and gives its result or error (function.example-failed); with system.generation every function has a success example and one per error variant it can fail with (ready.function-example-missing)
- Hold the built-in representation types (unit among them, the type of one value, which no id or scalar represents) and decide when a type name resolves: a built-in, a declared type, list<T>, set<T> or option<T> of a name that resolves, or map<K, V> of a key and a value that resolve, with type texts split at the comma outside angle brackets so generic types nest (map<K, map<K2, V>>, list<map<K, V>>)
- Check the capability model (src/core/capabilities.js): every capability that a contract requires (auth.requires), a plan entitles, a role or grant confers, a delegation or token scope passes on and a feature flag gates is a capability (capability.unknown); with system.generation every contract that is not local requires one (capability.required); every capability is conferred by a role or grant and is core or entitled by a plan (capability.unreachable); a delegation passes on only what its source holds and a grant confers only what its issuer role holds (delegation.exceeds); grant and delegation durations are durations (capability.duration)
- Check example fixtures (src/core/function.js, src/core/function-fixtures.js): exampleFixture entities declare a value of a type once, and a function example's argument, result or error may be {fixture: id} or {fixture: id, with: {dotted path: value}}, resolved before the example is evaluated; an undeclared fixture is refused (example.fixture-unknown), a fixture whose value is not its type or that, with its overrides, is not the type it stands for (example.fixture-type), and an override path that does not lead into the value (example.fixture-override)
- Measure the branch coverage of function examples (src/core/function-coverage.js, WG-4965): with the evaluator's branch recorder attached, every example is evaluated and each branch it takes is recorded (both branches of if, each match arm, the value and error paths of try, fail); with system.generation every branch of every function is taken by some example (ready.function-branch-uncovered, one gap per function naming each branch with its line and column; not for a function that misses an example or has one that fails, nor while any function has a finding; severity info when system.generation.functionBranches is gap)
- Check union, map, set and constraint types (src/core/type-shapes.js): a union has named variants whose fields resolve (type.union-variants, type.union-field), a newtype variant has one field and a tuple variant two or more positional fields, none optional, its wire encoding (external, internal, adjacent or untagged tag, tagField, contentField, variantCase) agrees with its variants, an internal tag writing no newtype or tuple variant, an untagged union with no tag field, content field or variant case, no two variants written as one JSON shape and a json newtype last (type.encoding), a map key is a string, an integer or an id or scalar over one, also inside a list, set, option or another map (type.map-key), a struct does not contain itself through struct fields alone (type.recursion), and constraints (pattern, minLength, maxLength) sit only on an id or scalar whose representation takes them (type.constraints); a union or map value has the opaque class of json and a set the class list in guard expressions, and a scenario value of a set type repeats no item
- Check derived and facade contracts (src/core/derivation.js): a derivation exactly on a contract with realisation derived, of kind pure, read (optionally with a need loop, insufficient and needRounds together) or command with the fields of its kind, and optionally header: true, the load with no needs first, whose outputs the needs function reads, then the needs loaded in one round no need round counts (derivation.kind); functions whose parameters are inputs (or load outputs) of the same name and type, whose results are the outputs and whose error variants are errors of the contract, an insufficient-state variant carrying one need, a fault error without payload (derivation.signature); a load contract that is not a target and whose inputs are filled from the inputs and the needs and whose outputs and errors fit (derivation.load); a commit contract that is not a target and whose inputs are filled, whose conflict error exists and whose outputs and errors fit (derivation.commit); need rounds and conflict retries as non-negative integers (derivation.bounds); a facade with no implementing component whose bffRoute method, path and shape exactly one served contract (not a facade or a target) states (contract.facade-route); with system.generation every function a derivation names is declared (ready.derived-function-missing)
- Check supplied inputs (src/core/supplied-inputs.js, src/core/ui.js): binding.suppliedBy names declared inputs of a contract and says each comes from the route or the session (contract.supplied-by), never from the body, so no form field or argument and no action argument fills one (form.supplied-input, action.supplied-input), and a form or action need not fill it
- Write and read the wire form of values (src/core/wire.js): every union tag (external, internal, adjacent, untagged), newtype and tuple variants, variant case and wire-cased field names, so a value round-trips through its JSON; and run a read or command derivation against a store (runDerivation in src/core/derivation.js), reporting the loads, need rounds and conflict retries

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Data model: Reads the well-formed field declarations of a record.
- Reads → Meta-model: The type kind, the record and contract properties and the requirement realisation are declared in the meta-model.
- Calls → Expression language: Parses the conditions, custom validation rules and audit captures of the cross-cutting constructs, and the arguments and navigation parameters of forms and actions, and lists their references.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Validator: Runs the type, record and realisation consistency checks, and the readiness rules when system.generation is set.
- Calls → Agent command line: The readiness command lists the gaps of a project.
- Calls → Machine coverage: Resolves the declared type of a payload field (typeStructure) to choose representative values.
- Calls → Specification layers: Reads type texts with parseTypeName to find the platform types a product names.

#### Known unknowns

- Only the data and interface rules exist here; the rules for implementation-level machines and refinement are added by their own tasks under WG-4838.
- The cross-cutting constructs are checked, but no generator turns them into code yet (WG-4838).
- The user interface constructs are checked, but no generator builds the frontend kernel or end-to-end browser tests from them yet (WG-4838).
- A language entity is checked here but not mirrored in Lean: its claims are checked by tests, not proved. Generating its parser, printer, checker and evaluator is specc's work (WG-4931); stating the Strata kernel language in Strata's specification is WG-4930.
- Derived contracts are checked by shape, names and types here; whether a derivation means what the contract's postconditions say is not checked by Exegra but by tests of the generated code. Emitting their implementations is specc's work (WG-4935, Strata side).
- A function is checked and its examples are evaluated here in JavaScript, but functions are not mirrored in Lean: their claims are checked by examples and tests, not proved. Compiling functions into a pure crate is specc's work (WG-4934). The JavaScript evaluator of a language follows a reference (through) only through a lookup an example supplies, which keys a reference by its field name alone; the case table states what the kernel does without one.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/builtin-types.js`, `src/core/codegen-model.js`, `src/core/readiness.js`, `src/core/constructs.js`, `src/core/ui.js`, `src/core/ui-steps.js`, `src/core/telemetry.js`, `src/core/scenario-input.js`, `src/core/type-shapes.js`, `src/core/language.js`, `src/core/function.js`, `src/core/function-syntax.js`, `src/core/function-value.js`, `src/core/language-eval.js`, `src/core/tenant-data.js`, `src/core/access.js`, `src/core/derivation.js`, `src/core/capabilities.js`, `src/core/function-fixtures.js`, `src/core/function-coverage.js`, `src/core/supplied-inputs.js`, `src/core/wire.js`
- Notes: Hand-written; run from validate.js and from the readiness command of cli/commands.js.

### Machine coverage

Measures which transitions and guard outcomes of each shipped implementation machine the specification's scenarios exercise, searches for event sequences that reach the rest, and reports a transition nothing reaches (docs/coverage.md, WG-4965).

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Select the implementation-level behaviors whose realisation is shipped (src/core/realisation.js) and list their items: each transition that is not faultOnly, and the true and false outcome of each guard
- Read the items a trace exercises: a fired transition and its guard true; the guarded transitions of the same state and event declared before it, false; every considered guard of an event ignored with reason guard-false, false
- Run the scenarios that list each machine and record which scenario covers each item
- Search, for the items left open, a bounded breadth-first space of action sequences on the machine alone: deliveries of its input events (not those it emits itself) with representative payloads from the declared types (scenario values, guard literals and their neighbours, both bools, enum values, null for optional fields; one instance for an instanced machine), and waits past the time thresholds of time-reading expressions or to the next pending event; a coarse round first (control state, guard variables, pending event ids), then the full state with widening payload choice; bounded by actions and by engine runs per machine
- Keep a greedy cover of the runs that covered something new as generated scenarios cover-<machine>-<n>, and turn them into CreateEntity operations of kind scenario tagged generated-coverage
- Report a transition no scenario and no searched sequence reaches as machine.transition-unreachable (a warning, an error with system.generation), saying whether its source state is entered by no transition, the search exhausted its states, or it stopped at a bound

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs each scenario of a shipped machine, and each searched sequence, through runEngine.
- Calls → Simulation contract: Derives the engine input of a scenario, and of the machine alone with its routes, with simulationInput.
- Calls → Validator: Reads whether a machine is shipped (effectiveRealisation).
- Calls → Expression language: Parses guards and effect values for their literals, their references to time and the variables guards read.
- Calls → Generation readiness: Resolves the declared type of a payload field (typeStructure) to choose representative values.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Agent command line: The cover-machines command reports the coverage table and the findings and writes the generated scenarios as ops.json.

#### Known unknowns

- The search is bounded and its payloads are representative, not exhaustive: a transition reported unreachable within the bound may be reached by a longer sequence or another payload; only the structural case (a source state nothing enters) is certain (WG-4965).
- A guard outcome nothing reaches is listed as open in the report, not as a finding; deciding that the outcome is impossible (complementary guards) is left to the reader (WG-4965).
- The verified explorer of formal/ (engine.explore) explores the interleavings and fault subsets of one scenario, not sequences of inputs, so the search runs the engine once per sequence instead (WG-4965).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/machine-coverage.js`
- Notes: Run by the cover-machines command of cli/commands.js.

### SQLite (node:sqlite)

The SQLite database engine built into Node (node:sqlite, DatabaseSync), which holds specification stores.

- Category: External system

#### Responsibilities

- Store tables in one database file, run transactions, and let several processes read one database in WAL mode while one writes

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Specification store service: Holds the rows in a database opened with DatabaseSync in WAL mode.

### Specification store

<spec>.sqlite beside a project's specification: the SQLite store of that specification (tables entity, trivia, ref and meta), rebuilt from the committed JSONC and never committed (.gitignore).

- Category: Data store
- Parent: Persistence (`persistence`)
- Owner: spec-store

#### Relationships in

- Writes → Specification store service: Replaces every row of the store in one transaction on import.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Notes: A data file, not code.

### Specification store service

Holds a project's specification in SQLite with an exact round trip: the JSONC text is cut into verbatim rows, one per entity and one per stretch of text between entities, and the rows in position order are the text again, byte for byte.

- Category: Computational service
- Parent: Persistence (`persistence`)

#### Responsibilities

- Import: parse the text once with jsonc-parser and cut it into rows: one entity row per item of an entity section (the meta-model's sections and those a vocabulary's entityKinds declare), one row per other top-level value stored whole (system, exegra, vocabulary, a section with no entity kind), and trivia rows for everything between them (keys, brackets, commas, comments, whitespace, line endings, the text before the root and after it), so every code unit belongs to exactly one row
- Index each entity row by section, id, kind and component, and record its JSON value, the sha-256 of that value, and the ids it references (also in table ref, indexed by target), from the model the parser derives
- Record in meta the store format and version, the project, the semantic hash, the unit positions count (UTF-16 code units of the imported text) and the sha-256 of the text last synchronised
- Refuse, with a StoreError and nothing stored, a text that does not parse, is not well-formed Unicode, or is not valid UTF-8, and any import whose rows would not export to the same text, which is checked inside the import transaction
- Export: concatenate the rows in position order; write a file atomically, and refuse to overwrite the specification when it changed since the store last synchronised with it
- Give the model, diagnostics and semantic hash of the stored specification by the same analysis as its text (loadModelFromStore)
- Open every database in WAL mode, so several processes can read while one writes

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Parser: Derives the model of an imported text for the references and the semantic hash of its rows.
- Calls → Validator: loadModelFromStore analyzes the exported text with analyzeSpec.
- Reads → Meta-model: Takes the entity sections and their kinds from metamodel.json.
- Depends on → jsonc-parser: Parses the text into a tree whose offsets cut the rows.
- Calls → SQLite (node:sqlite): Holds the rows in a database opened with DatabaseSync in WAL mode.
- Writes → Specification store: Replaces every row of the store in one transaction on import.
- Reads → Specification file: Reads the specification's bytes on import.
- Writes → Specification file: export writes the stored text back to the specification, atomically, unless it changed since the store last synchronised with it.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Agent command line: store import and store export copy a project's specification into its store and back.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests round-trip every specification and fixture through the store.

#### Known unknowns

- apply does not go through the store yet: a transaction edits the JSONC text and the store is imported again; edits as SQLite transactions that re-render only the edited rows wait for the batched transactions of WG-4974 (WG-4979, phase 2).
- The model is derived from the exported text by the Lean kernel, not assembled from the rows, and validation is not incremental by entity hash yet (WG-4979).
- Positions are the code-unit offsets of the imported text; inserting rows between existing ones needs a renumbering or fractional scheme, to be chosen in phase 2 (WG-4979).
- A leading byte order mark is stored and given back, but the parser reports it as a syntax error, so such a specification does not validate, from the text or from the store alike.
- specc, Concordance and the application do not read the store yet; they read the JSONC text (WG-4979).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `tools/spec-store.js`

### Specification layers

Reads a layered specification (WG-4989): the platform specification plus product files that import the platform's entities by id and add tenant metadata of their own, checked ahead of the verified kernel and analysed as one model with every location in its own file.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Read each product file on its own: syntax, the product header (id, name, imports.spec naming the platform specification, imports.entities naming the platform entities it uses) and its sections
- Allow a product to declare only tenant metadata (ARC-113 of the platform): the entity sections records, types, screens, forms, actions, liveBindings, errorMessages, behaviors, events, contracts, roles, scenarios, uiScenarios, claims, evidence and capabilities (the one platform section a product adds to, D-6590), and the typed sections objects, policies, segments, settings, ledgerUnits, ledgerPools, ledgerLimits, mailTemplates, noticeTemplates, tokens, triggers, automations and operations; refuse every other section with layer.kind-not-allowed
- Splice the product entities of the entity sections into the platform text at the end of their sections, keeping the platform text byte for byte, and have analyzeSpec derive and validate the combined model; the typed sections are not spliced
- Map every location and JSON path the analysis reports back to the file it is in (loc.file names a product file) and restate the diagnostics a layered specification gives a meaning: an id the platform or another product declares (layer.redefines-entity), a reference no layer declares (layer.reference-unknown)
- Check the imports: an imported id the platform does not declare (layer.import-unknown), a platform entity used by a reference, a type text or a capability list without being imported (layer.import-missing), a reference into another product file (layer.cross-product-reference), an imports.spec that is not the project's platform file (layer.import-mismatch); every refusal says what is wrong and what to do
- Keep the platform's semantic hash the hash of the platform text parsed alone, so adding a product file changes neither it nor anything generated from the platform; report the combined model's hash as the layered hash
- Refuse a product entity that claims what only the platform may have (layer.platform-power): a role at platform level or with anonymous, service or external holders, a contract that is public with the anonymous scheme, is implemented by a platform component or carries a realisation of generated, hand or derived, a behavior at implementation level, an event that is a domain event, an elapsed-time event or states a delivery guarantee, a capability that is core, a setting that is set at or locked by the platform level
- Refuse two product files with one product id (layer.product-id-duplicate), a product entity id that fails the id pattern (layer.entity-id-invalid) and a section item with no id (layer.entity-id-missing)
- Refuse a platform entity that names a product entity, by a typed reference, a type text or a capability list (layer.platform-references-product), so the platform never depends on a product
- Take the product files as they are on disk when a project reloads (Project.reload), so a long-lived session shows the layered model of the current product files
- Check each value of a typed section against the platform type its section maps to (src/core/layer-values.js, one table and one generic check: struct fields required and optional, union variants by tag, enums, lists, maps, ids by pattern and length, scalars by representation, the authoring text of an expression): a value of the wrong shape is refused with layer.value-invalid and a union value the platform union lacks with layer.outside-catalogue (the published catalogue of INT-153: trigger-event, automation-step, tenant-operation-step)
- Refuse an entity id that is not in the namespace of its product, the product id with each hyphen replaced by an underscore and two underscores (layer.namespace-missing, D-6582), and resolve the names the typed values hold against what the product files declare: an undeclared name in the product namespace is layer.reference-unknown and a name another product declares is layer.cross-product-reference, which also confines a product capability to its product
- Report a product capability as unreachable only when no role or grant confers it: installing the product entitles it, and a product file cannot add a plan (D-6590)
- Parse and check the expression text of every typed value whose platform type is expr or expression (src/core/layer-expr.js: the grammar and static check of the tenant rule language, in the scope the value is written in: the fields of the object type it belongs to, the params an operation or an event binds, the roles of the platform and of the product, and what a reference hop may follow): a text that does not parse is layer.expr-syntax, a field the object type lacks layer.expr-unknown-field, a role nobody declares layer.expr-unknown-role, a param the scope does not bind layer.expr-unknown-param and a shape the operator does not accept layer.expr-type, each naming the product entity and the position in the text. The grammar and typing include the nine operators of the language: checked + - * on integers (a timestamp plus or minus an integer is a timestamp; Time + Time is refused, ms_between gives the difference), ms_between, days, hours, minutes, concat and text, with a - before a digit a sign only where no operand ends before it, and an operand of the wrong shape is layer.expr-type with the message "<op> does not accept a <Shape> operand". A step of an operation reads the id of an earlier create step as a param (D-6548). tests/core/layer-expr-drift.test.js probes every operator a language entity declares against this parser and compares it with the language checker on the shared case table, so an operator added to the language fails a test until layer-expr.js handles it (WG-5120)
- Refuse an object type that has a field with a personal declaration and no retention rule (layer.retention-missing, D-6584)
- Accept the product header members version (three whole numbers joined by dots) and platform ({min, below?}: a platform release, and a later release from which the product is not supported), and refuse a malformed one with layer.header-invalid, as specc does (WG-5174)

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Parser: Parses the platform text alone for its model and semantic hash.
- Calls → Validator: analyzeSpec derives and validates the combined text.
- Calls → Generation readiness: Reads type texts with parseTypeName to find the platform types a product names.
- Reads → Meta-model: Reads the id pattern and the sections of the specification format.
- Depends on → jsonc-parser: Parses product files and the platform's syntax tree with the library.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Transaction system: A Project with product files analyses every candidate platform text with its product files layered on it.
- Calls → Agent command line: Trying integrity rules on a layered project analyses the platform text with its product files.

#### Known unknowns

- The browser application opens a project without its product files: src/app/session.js builds the Project from the specification alone, and the workspace watcher does not watch product files (follow-up of WG-4989).
- Whether a product's claims and scenarios are about its own entities is not checked; any claim or scenario may be declared in a product file.
- The product sections are a fixed list in src/core/layers.js and src/core/layer-values.js; nothing yet generates the tenant bundle a product installs through the platform's install-bundle contract. The check does not parse the block text of a template. The scope of an automation's expressions is not known here (the params come from the triggers that start it and the outputs of its steps), so its fields and params are accepted and only its syntax, shapes and roles are checked.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/layers.js`, `src/core/layer-values.js`, `src/core/layer-expr.js`
- Notes: Outside the verdict path, like the parser: it composes and checks texts before the model derivation in Lean, whose sources and build are unchanged.

### Derivation cache
