A persistent, on-disk cache of the stages of model derivation that run in the Lean kernel, so that repeated Exegra processes over the same specification skip them (WG-5636). It wraps derivation from outside: the cached value is what parseSpec and analyzeSpec compute, and a hit is observably identical to a miss.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Key every entry by the SHA-256 of the entry format, the package version, the content hash of Exegra's derivation sources (every file of src/core and of the Lean build in src/engine/lean, by path and bytes), the stage name and the stage's inputs: the specification text (for a layered project the text that already holds every product file's entities) and the options that change the result (claimsOnly). No modification time is read, so editing a specification, a product file, the file project.jsonc names or Exegra itself is a miss
- Cache two stages: parseSpec (the model and its diagnostics, only when the text is usable) and the semantic diagnostics of analyzeSpec (only when analyzeSpec parsed the text itself, never for a caller's own parsed model)
- Encode a stage's value faithfully as tagged JSON (Maps, Sets, objects without a prototype, -0, infinities, undefined, shared references and cycles) or refuse it and cache nothing: a value the encoding cannot reproduce exactly (a function, a class instance, a hole, an accessor) is computed every time
- Store each entry in one file under the cache directory (EXEGRA_MODEL_CACHE_DIR, else model/ under EXEGRA_CACHE_DIR, else the user cache directory: %LOCALAPPDATA%/exegra/cache/model, $XDG_CACHE_HOME/exegra/model or ~/.cache/exegra/model), with a header that repeats the key, the byte count and the SHA-256 of the body
- Write through a temporary sibling and a rename, so a concurrent reader sees a whole entry or none and two writers of one key leave one valid entry; a failed write is ignored
- Treat an entry that is missing, truncated, altered, of another format or under another key as a miss and replace it, never as an error
- Bound the directory: past EXEGRA_MODEL_CACHE_MAX_BYTES (default 512 MiB) delete the least recently used entries down to three quarters of the bound, and delete temporary files a dead writer left more than an hour ago; a hit refreshes the entry's recency
- Do nothing when EXEGRA_NO_CACHE is set (to anything but empty, 0 or false), when the sources cannot be read or outside Node; EXEGRA_CACHE_TRACE=1 reports each hit, miss and store on stderr

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Parser: parseSpec asks the cache for the derived model of a text before deriving it.
- Calls → Validator: analyzeSpec asks the cache for the semantic diagnostics of a text before validating it.

#### Known unknowns

- The evaluation of claims is not cached: it runs the verified engine, and a claims run still takes the time of its scenarios after the derivation stages hit. Measured on the Strata specification (about 10,000 entities): validate 13.9 s cold, 1.0 s warm; claims 16.4 s cold, 7.8 s warm.
- The diagnostics of a validate hit are rebuilt as copies: their loc objects are equal to the entities' but no longer the same objects. Nothing reads the identity.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/derivation-cache.js`
- Notes: Outside the verdict path: it stores and returns the values of the derivation without changing them; the derivation itself (Lean and the shell code around it) is untouched.

### Claim strength

Says how strong the check behind each claim status is and whether what it reads is the platform's own entities: a static, deterministic analysis of the model that never changes a status (decision dec-claim-check-and-grounding).

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Classify the check of every claim: holds-by-construction (a never, state-invariant or precedes claim that every value nominal transitions can give its references satisfies, or whose occurrence only fault transitions or guards that can never hold produce; naming the variables and the fault transitions), restated-scenario (a counter compared with a literal equal to the extreme its one scenario reaches), single-trace, explored (from supplied exploration results), refined, and the method-based checks structural, data-model, lean-theorem, sampled, test, evidence and none
- Classify the grounding of every claim and integrity rule: bound, partly-bound, self-contained or not-applicable, from what the behaviors it reads are anchored to (a refined existing behavior, a record, a contract event, an anchored behavior) and the payload fields of contract-less events they branch on, or the kinds an integrity rule quantifies over; under a baseline an entity the change adds anchors nothing
- Count claims by check and grounding, and integrity rules by grounding

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs the one scenario of a counter bound to decide restated-scenario.
- Calls → Expression language: Parses claim expressions, guards and effect values for the value analysis and the grounding.
- Calls → Integrity rules: Parses integrity rules to find the kinds they quantify over.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Agent command line: Adds the check and grounding to claims and evaluate, and the strength review to admit.
- Calls → Artifact generators: Shows the check and grounding of each claim in the claims report.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the check and grounding on fixtures from the Harbourline fold.

#### Known unknowns

- Exhaustive exploration results (exegra prove) are not recorded in a project, so a trace claim is reported as explored only when the caller passes them; claims, evaluate, the claims report and admit never do yet (WG-5280).
- The value analysis ignores guards when it collects the values and states nominal runs reach and does not follow arithmetic or keyed instances, so vacuity that depends on them is not found; restated-scenario examines only a plain comparison with a literal.
- Grounding is structural: an anchored behavior may still branch on a field that means nothing, and the expression language cannot read roles, capabilities or grants, so the access model reaches a behavior only through a contract's auth.requires.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/claim-strength.js`

| claim-evaluator-calls-proof-export | calls | Claim evaluator | Proof export | Claims proved in Lean are reported as verified by proof (planned, WG-4686). |

| test-suites-calls-contract-conformance | calls | Test suites | Contract conformance | Core tests exercise the static and trace checks and the request log. |

| claim-evaluator-calls-data-model | calls | Claim evaluator | Data model | Evaluates data-model claims from the findings of the data rules. |

| data-model-reads-metamodel | reads | Data model | Meta-model | The record and copy kinds and their enums are declared in the meta-model. |
| projection-system-calls-data-model | calls | Projection system | Data model | The Data projection draws records, copies, owners and holders, and marks copies with findings. |
| inspector-calls-data-model | calls | Semantic inspector | Data model | Lists a record's fields and copies and a copy's source fields. |

Declared when the data-model rule holder-reaches-owner found no path between the two stores. |

| proof-export-calls-contract-conformance | calls | Proof export | Contract conformance | Renders a counterexample's contract events as request-log lines. |

| simulation-engine-calls-workload-model | calls | Simulation engine | Workload model | Queues the next arrival of a workload when one is taken, runs link transfers and timers (planned, WG-4715). |
| simulation-engine-calls-performance-metrics | calls | Simulation engine | Performance metrics | Accumulates the measured outputs of a run, in trace and metrics output modes (planned, WG-4715). |

| simulation-contract-calls-workload-model | calls | Simulation contract | Workload model | Lowers workloads, rates, durations, distributions and links into the version-2 engine input (planned). |
| metric-claims-calls-simulation-engine | calls | Metric claims | Simulation engine | Runs the claim's scenarios once per seed in metrics mode (planned, WG-4715). |
| metric-claims-reads-performance-metrics | reads | Metric claims | Performance metrics | Takes one statistic per run from the measured outputs (planned). |
| claim-evaluator-calls-metric-claims | calls | Claim evaluator | Metric claims | Metric-bound claims are evaluated by the metric claims component (planned, WG-4715). |
| load-aspect-depends-on-workload-model | depends-on | Load aspect | Workload model | The load aspect drives scenarios with the workloads of format 2 (planned, WG-4668 on WG-4715). |
| load-aspect-depends-on-resource-model | depends-on | Load aspect | Resource model | Saturation and backpressure are those of the resources of format 2. |
| cost-aspect-reads-performance-metrics | reads | Cost aspect | Performance metrics | Prices the counted quantities and reports cost per run, per bucket and per month (planned, WG-4669 on WG-4715). |

| hash-binding-calls-lean-call-boundary | calls | Semantic hash binding | Lean call boundary | Hashes and normalizes a model that does not carry its hash with hash.semantic and hash.normalize. |
| validator-calls-generation-readiness | calls | Validator | Generation readiness | Runs the type, record and realisation consistency checks, and the readiness rules when system.generation is set. |
| generation-readiness-calls-data-model | calls | Generation readiness | Data model | Reads the well-formed field declarations of a record. |
| generation-readiness-reads-metamodel | reads | Generation readiness | Meta-model | The type kind, the record and contract properties and the requirement realisation are declared in the meta-model. |
| agent-cli-calls-generation-readiness | calls | Agent command line | Generation readiness | The readiness command lists the gaps of a project. |
| generation-readiness-calls-expression-language | calls | Generation readiness | Expression language | Parses the conditions, custom validation rules and audit captures of the cross-cutting constructs, and the arguments and navigation parameters of forms and actions, and lists their references. |
| machine-coverage-calls-simulation-engine | calls | Machine coverage | Simulation engine | Runs each scenario of a shipped machine, and each searched sequence, through runEngine. |
| machine-coverage-calls-simulation-contract | calls | Machine coverage | Simulation contract | Derives the engine input of a scenario, and of the machine alone with its routes, with simulationInput. |
| machine-coverage-calls-validator | calls | Machine coverage | Validator | Reads whether a machine is shipped (effectiveRealisation). |
| machine-coverage-calls-expression-language | calls | Machine coverage | Expression language | Parses guards and effect values for their literals, their references to time and the variables guards read. |
| machine-coverage-calls-generation-readiness | calls | Machine coverage | Generation readiness | Resolves the declared type of a payload field (typeStructure) to choose representative values. |
| agent-cli-calls-machine-coverage | calls | Agent command line | Machine coverage | The cover-machines command reports the coverage table and the findings and writes the generated scenarios as ops.json. |
| agent-cli-calls-spec-store | calls | Agent command line | Specification store service | store import and store export copy a project's specification into its store and back. |
| spec-store-calls-parser | calls | Specification store service | Parser | Derives the model of an imported text for the references and the semantic hash of its rows. |
| spec-store-calls-validator | calls | Specification store service | Validator | loadModelFromStore analyzes the exported text with analyzeSpec. |
| spec-store-reads-metamodel | reads | Specification store service | Meta-model | Takes the entity sections and their kinds from metamodel.json. |
| spec-store-depends-on-jsonc-parser | depends-on | Specification store service | jsonc-parser | Parses the text into a tree whose offsets cut the rows. |
| spec-store-calls-sqlite-engine | calls | Specification store service | SQLite (node:sqlite) | Holds the rows in a database opened with DatabaseSync in WAL mode. |
| spec-store-writes-specification-store-file | writes | Specification store service | Specification store | Replaces every row of the store in one transaction on import. |
| spec-store-reads-specification-file | reads | Specification store service | Specification file | Reads the specification's bytes on import. |
| spec-store-writes-specification-file | writes | Specification store service | Specification file | export writes the stored text back to the specification, atomically, unless it changed since the store last synchronised with it. |
| test-suites-calls-spec-store | calls | Test suites | Specification store service | Core tests round-trip every specification and fixture through the store. |
| spec-layers-calls-parser | calls | Specification layers | Parser | Parses the platform text alone for its model and semantic hash. |
| spec-layers-calls-validator | calls | Specification layers | Validator | analyzeSpec derives and validates the combined text. |
| spec-layers-calls-generation-readiness | calls | Specification layers | Generation readiness | Reads type texts with parseTypeName to find the platform types a product names. |
| spec-layers-reads-metamodel | reads | Specification layers | Meta-model | Reads the id pattern and the sections of the specification format. |
| spec-layers-depends-on-jsonc-parser | depends-on | Specification layers | jsonc-parser | Parses product files and the platform's syntax tree with the library. |
| transactions-calls-spec-layers | calls | Transaction system | Specification layers | A Project with product files analyses every candidate platform text with its product files layered on it. |
| agent-cli-calls-spec-layers | calls | Agent command line | Specification layers | Trying integrity rules on a layered project analyses the platform text with its product files. |
| claim-strength-calls-simulation-engine | calls | Claim strength | Simulation engine | Runs the one scenario of a counter bound to decide restated-scenario. |
| claim-strength-calls-expression-language | calls | Claim strength | Expression language | Parses claim expressions, guards and effect values for the value analysis and the grounding. |
| claim-strength-calls-integrity-rules | calls | Claim strength | Integrity rules | Parses integrity rules to find the kinds they quantify over. |
| agent-cli-calls-claim-strength | calls | Agent command line | Claim strength | Adds the check and grounding to claims and evaluate, and the strength review to admit. |
| generators-calls-claim-strength | calls | Artifact generators | Claim strength | Shows the check and grounding of each claim in the claims report. |
| test-suites-calls-claim-strength | calls | Test suites | Claim strength | Core tests exercise the check and grounding on fixtures from the Harbourline fold. |

## Contracts

### Apply a transaction

Project.apply: the single entry through which a project's specification or layout changes (UI, source editor, agent command line). Defined by src/core/project.js and src/core/operations.js.

#### Inputs

- operations (Operation | Operation[]): Typed operations: CreateEntity, DeleteEntity, RenameEntity, SetProperty, CreateRelationship, DeleteRelationship, MovePresentationNode, AddClaim, ModifyClaim, AddState, AddTransition, ReplaceSource, ReplaceLayout.
- label (string): Optional history label.
- origin (string): Optional origin of the change (ui by default, cli, undo, redo, external).
- expectRevision / expectHash (number / string): Optional optimistic concurrency guards against the project's revision or semantic hash.

#### Outputs

- result (TransactionResult): ok, revision after the call, semantic hash, affected entity ids, semanticChanged and the diagnostics of the resulting (or rejected) state; on failure also code and message.
- commit event ({type: 'commit', result, entry}): Delivered synchronously to every subscriber after a successful commit; the history entry says whether spec and layout changed.

#### Preconditions

- The operation list is not empty and every operation names a known type
- When expectRevision or expectHash is given, it equals the project's current revision or semantic hash
- A current model exists unless every operation is ReplaceSource or ReplaceLayout
- Each operation's own preconditions hold (the entity exists and has the right kind, new ids match the id pattern and are unused, parameters have the right shape)

#### Postconditions

- Atomicity: when the result is not ok, the specification text, layout text, model, revision, history and undo stacks are exactly as before
- The resulting specification, if the specification text was edited, has no validation errors; a transaction whose result would have errors is rejected
- On success the revision has increased by exactly one, a new immutable model snapshot derived from the new text exists and the undo stack gained one entry (the redo stack is cleared)
- Presentation operations (MovePresentationNode, ReplaceLayout) never change the specification text or the semantic hash
- Existing comments and formatting outside the edited ranges are preserved

#### Errors

- op.empty: The transaction has no operation.
- op.unknown: An operation has an unknown type.
- revision.stale: expectRevision or expectHash does not match the project.
- model.unavailable: The current source has syntax errors and an operation other than ReplaceSource or ReplaceLayout was submitted.
- validation.failed: The resulting specification would have validation errors, or a replacement source has syntax errors.
- layout.invalid: The resulting layout would be unreadable.
- op.param / op.kind: An operation parameter is missing or has the wrong shape, or an entity kind cannot be created.
- entity.unknown / entity.kind: An id names no entity, or an entity of the wrong kind.
- id.format / id.duplicate: A new id breaks the id pattern or is already used.
- delete.system / delete.referenced: The system entity cannot be deleted; an entity that others refer to is deleted without cascade.
- edit.syntax / edit.not-array: A text edit would leave the specification syntactically invalid, or targets something that is not an array.

#### Obligations

- Callers must show the rejection and its diagnostics to the user or agent; a rejected transaction is never retried implicitly.

### Write a project file

Writing spec.jsonc or layout.jsonc of a project: persistence client, POST /__exegra/write of the development-server plugin, and the workspace service's write function in tools/workspace.js.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name matching ^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$.
- file ('spec' | 'layout'): Which project file.
- text (string): Complete new file text, at most 16 MB. In simulated traces the text stands for itself by its hash.
- baseHash (string | null): sha-256 hash of the file text the caller last read or wrote; null means the caller expects the file to be absent.
- meta ({label, origin}): Optional journal annotations.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- success ({ok: true, hash}): The hash of the text now on disk.
- conflict ({ok: false, conflict: true, current: {text, hash}}): The file's current content, returned instead of writing; HTTP status 409.
- failure ({ok: false, error, code}): Any other refusal or failure, with the error code (HTTP 400, 403, 404, 413 or 500).
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Preconditions

- The project name is valid and the target path stays inside the project directory
- The file is spec or layout and the text is a string within the size limit
- Over HTTP: the Host and any Origin are loopback and the x-exegra-client header is present

#### Postconditions

- Optimistic concurrency: the file is replaced only if the sha-256 of its current text equals baseHash (absent equals null); otherwise it is left untouched and the conflict result carries its current content
- Replacement writes a temporary sibling file and renames it over the target; writes of one project are serialized
- After the replace, one line is appended to .history/journal.jsonl; if the append fails the file stays replaced, no line is written and the write is answered as a failure (record write-journal)

#### Errors

- conflict: The file on disk differs from the base hash.
- name.invalid / path.unsafe: The project name is invalid or the path escapes the project.
- file.unknown / input.invalid / too-large: The file is not spec or layout, an input has the wrong type, or the text exceeds 16 MB.
- project.invalid / not-found: project.jsonc is malformed or names a missing file (HTTP 400, thrown by the workspace).
- body.invalid / body.too-large: The body is not a JSON object (400) or is larger than 20 MB (413).
- route.unknown: No such endpoint (404).
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

#### Obligations

- A client that receives a conflict must not overwrite silently: the session keeps the conflict until the user chooses to reload from disk or overwrite disk.

### Project a model into a scene

project(model, viewId, ctx) in src/core/projections.js, followed by layoutScene(scene, pinned, {method, aspect}) or chooseLayout(scene, pinned, {aspect}) in src/core/layout.js.

#### Inputs

- model (Model): The committed model snapshot.
- viewId (string): A declared view, or the id of an implicit view; an unknown id falls back to the first view.
- ctx (ProjectionContext): Collapsed and hidden sets, isolated subsystem, focused claim and scenario, the focused entity (for the references projection), claim results, simulation trace and step.
- layout options ({method?: string, aspect?: number}): The layout method (layered, layered-down, packed, radial or auto) and the target width-to-depth ratio of the view; defaults are layered and 1.6.
- pinned (Record<sceneNodeId, [x, z]>): Absolute positions of leaf nodes from the layout file.

#### Outputs

- scene (SceneModel): Nodes, edges, legend and notes.
- placement (Placement): A rectangle on the ground plane for every node; chooseLayout also returns the method it chose and the score of every method.

#### Postconditions

- Every node and every edge of the scene is backed by an entity of the model (it carries that entity's id)
- Pure and deterministic: the same inputs give the same scene and placement, and the model is not changed
- Position carries no meaning: containment and relationships do

#### Obligations

- The renderer draws only what a scene and a placement contain and reads nothing from the model.

### Evaluate claims

evaluateClaims, evaluateClaim and checkTrace in src/core/claims.js.

#### Inputs

- model (Model): The model whose claims are evaluated.
- recorded (Recorded | null): Recorded execution results of evidence, keyed by evidence id (results), and outcomes an external tool recorded per claim (claims, see record external-claim-outcomes).

#### Outputs

- results (ClaimResult[]): One result per claim in declaration order: status, evaluator, semantic hash, scope, explanation, assumptions, involved entities, counterexample and evidence.

#### Postconditions

- A status is verified only when an evaluator checked the claim on the model; a selector that selects nothing is inconclusive, not verified
- A claim whose method is not registered is not evaluated
- Trace-based statuses state that only the listed scenarios' traces were checked
- Evidence can only make a model-evaluated claim worse (a recorded fail or contradicting evidence turns verified into violated), never better
- An external-evidence claim is supported only by a recorded passing test or by non-test evidence, and says whether Exegra executed anything
- A metric-bound claim is reported model-holds, violated or inconclusive with its run count and confidence, never verified or supported
- Deterministic: no clock, randomness or I/O. Trace and metric claims run the engine synchronously on the caller's thread; in the application that is the main thread, inside every semantic commit

#### Errors

- unknown claim: evaluateClaim is called with an id that is not a claim; it throws.
- evaluator failure: An exception while evaluating a claim becomes an inconclusive result rather than aborting the evaluation.

### Run a scenario

runScenario, createSimulation and createTraceCursor in src/core/engine.js, on the Rust engine compiled to WebAssembly; semantics are specified in docs/module-contracts.md.

#### Inputs

- model (Model): The model holding the behaviors.
- scenarioId (string): A scenario of the model.
- faults (string[]): Optional fault ids replacing the scenario's own list.

#### Outputs

- trace (Trace): Initial configuration, every step with fired transitions, ignored events, faults and the configuration after it, the outcome and the final configuration.

#### Preconditions

- The scenario, every behavior it lists and every active fault exist in the model

#### Postconditions

- Deterministic: the same inputs give deep-equal traces
- The run ends quiescent (queue empty), at the step limit (maxSteps, default 1000) or in error (an expression failed; the failing step carries the error)
- Recorded steps are not changed by later steps

#### Errors

- unknown identifier: The scenario, a listed behavior or an active fault does not exist; a plain Error is thrown.

### Agent command

One invocation of `node cli/exegra.js <command> [args] [--project <name>] [--specs <dir>] [--pretty]`; described in docs/agent-interface.md.

#### Inputs

- command (string): projects, validate, list, show, deps, contracts, claims, evaluate, gaps, ops, apply, plan-delete, simulate, prove, trace, generate, markdown, traceability, verify or help.
- options (flags): Command options and positionals; --project defaults to exegra and --specs to the repository's specs directory. apply reads its operations from --file, --ops or stdin. In simulated traces options carries the one argument a scenario needs.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire: one process answers one invocation; the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775) numbers invocations.

#### Outputs

- envelope ({ok: true, command: string, project: string, semanticHash: string, result}): One JSON document on stdout (cli/exegra.js main, built by runCommand in cli/commands.js); the failure envelope {ok: false, command, project, error: {code, message}, diagnostics} is the error role.
- progress notes ({notes: string[]}): Lines on stderr such as "verify: running <ref>" (ctx.log); not part of the stdout document.
- exit code ({exit: number}): 0 ok, 1 the command failed, 2 usage error (exitCodeOf).
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id assigned by the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775).

#### Postconditions

- Every mutation is applied through Project.apply; no command edits specification text directly
- A rejected or stale transaction, or a disk conflict, leaves the files unchanged apart from the case noted on agent-cli (spec written, layout conflicting)

#### Errors

- usage.unknown-command / usage.invalid-argument / usage.missing-argument: Missing or unknown command or argument (exit code 2).
- revision.stale / write.conflict: The expected hash does not match, or a file changed on disk since it was read; for apply, a layout conflict after the specification was written says so in the message.
- validation.failed / model.unavailable / op.unknown / op.empty / op.param / entity.unknown / entity.kind: The transaction was rejected, or the project has syntax errors, or an argument names no entity of the right kind.
- project.not-found / simulate.failed / verify.failed / generate.unavailable: The project does not exist, the engine refused or could not run the scenario, the specification has syntax errors, or the generators are missing.
- markdown.none / markdown.render / markdown.unknown / markdown.path: The markdown command has nothing to render, fails, is asked for an unknown document or would leave its output directory.
- internal.error: Any other exception.

### Engine input, format version 2

Derived (WG-4664, WG-4715, WG-4729): simulationInput returns {format: "exegra-sim-input", version: 2} for a specification using resources, workloads, links, instances, timers or seeded randomness (needsFormat2 in src/core/sim-input.js), and the Rust engine runs it with its version-2 semantics (engine/exegra-engine/src/lib.rs, v2.rs). Every version-1 field keeps its meaning with times as integers of the base unit. Remaining (WG-4741): crash-instance, meters and the cost metric are not run, and there are no format-2 goldens. Normative definition: docs/simulation.md, "Engine input and trace format version 2".

#### Inputs

- version (2): Version-1 inputs are still run by the version-1 semantics; any other value is refused.
- time ({unit: "tick" | "s" | "ms" | "us" | "ns", perSecond: null | 1 | 1000 | 1000000 | 1000000000}): Base unit; times and durations are integers in [0, 2^53). Unit tick allows no rate or bandwidth.
- scenario.seed (decimal string | null): Unsigned 64-bit seed without sign or leading zeros; required when any element draws; not needed when the only random sites are choice values.
- scenario.stepLimit (integer in [1, 2^53)): maxSteps of the specification.
- scenario.horizon / warmup / stopAt (integer | null): Horizon required with workloads; warmup < horizon <= stopAt.
- scenario.output ("trace" | "metrics"): Full trace, or metrics and final configuration only.
- scenario.steps[].target ({behavior, instance}): Optional addressee of an input step.
- behaviors[].instances ({count: integer} | {key: string, max: integer}): Counted or keyed instances; keyed machine keys keep the canonical JSON of the key, quotes included.
- transitions[].effects (Effect[]): Adds emit with after as integer or distribution, target "reply", via, size and fanout; use, acquire, release with class; timer, cancel; set with {sample: dist}.
- routes ({event, behavior, by: "key" | "round-robin" | "random" | "any" | "least-busy", field?, resource?}[]): Instance routing decided when the entry is created; down instances are skipped except by key; any is enumerated by the explorer.
- resources ({id, kind, capacity, discipline: "fifo" | "priority", queueBound, overflow: "refuse" | "drop" | "shed-oldest", demandFactor: [n, num, den][] | {usl: {alpha: [num, den], beta: [num, den]}} | null, classes: {id, priority, reserved, limit, queueBound, maxWait}[]}[]): Resources with capacity, request classes, queue rules and load-dependent demand (a step table whose last row holds beyond it, or USL parameters evaluated exactly at each grant).
- links ({id, from, to, latency: Dist | null, bandwidth: integer | null, queueBound: integer | null, ordered: boolean, fanout: boolean}[]): Bytes per second, a latency distribution, ordering and per-receiver fan-out queues.
- workloads ({id, kind: "open" | "closed" | "population", ...}[]): Open: event, process, phases, repeat, batch, clients {count, pick, addresses}, legitimate, maxArrivals, payload. Closed: event, population, think, start, reply, timeout, payload, legitimate. Population: behavior, count, start, clients, legitimate. Phases: from < until, sorted, non-overlapping; rates count instants.
- unitExponential ([u, x][]): The pinned unit-mean exponential quantile table at scale 2^-64 on the standard grid (x below 2^72), present when a workload is Poisson.
- requests ({id, start: {event, workload?}, key, ends: {event, outcome: "ok" | "refused" | "timeout" | "error"}[]}[]): Request classes measured by the metrics.
- meters ({id, of, measure: "count" | "rate" | "ratio" | "quantile", window, q?}[]): Windowed measurements readable in expressions.
- prices ({id, counts, price: {num, den}, currency}[]): Exact rational prices per counted unit (a delivery, an ended request, a byte, a resource unit held for one second, an instance up for one second).
- metrics ({quantiles: string[], series?: {bucket}}): Quantiles as exact decimals (default 0.5, 0.9, 0.99) and an optional series bucket dividing horizon - warmup.
- faults[] (Fault): Optional window {at, until} (not on crash-instance); adds scale-demand, set-capacity, set-class-limit, collapse-classes, replace-demand and crash-instance {behavior, instance, at, restartAfter?, notify?}.
- Dist ({kind: "fixed", value} | {kind: "uniform", lo, hi} | {kind: "table", points: [u, x][]} | {kind: "choice", values}): Integer distributions; continuous ones are lowered to tables with the pinned quantile tables and 96-bit fixed-point exp and ln.

#### Outputs

- trace (Trace (version 2)): See contract trace-v2.

#### Preconditions

- The input was derived from a validated specification, or is a generated input of the version-2 generator
- Every integer is within its bound (docs/simulation.md, Integers and bounds; distribution tables below 2^53, unitExponential below 2^72); a specification duration that is not a whole number of base units is a validation error, never rounded
- No reserved name is used, ids of behaviors, resources, meters, links and workloads are distinct, and every reference resolves
- Phases are valid: from < until, sorted, non-overlapping, and repeat at least the last until
- Reserved units sum to at most capacity, each class's reserved units are at most its limit, and every request can be granted, for every fault mask the scenario admits
- A keyed behavior's max is at least the sum of the counts of the populations on it
- A seed is present when any element draws

#### Postconditions

- Two calls of simulationInput on the same model give identical documents
- A specification that uses no format-2 construct still derives a version-1 input, unchanged

#### Errors

- refusal: In order after the version-1 refusals: unknown time unit; Field "<path>" must be an integer in [<lo>, <hi>].; a malformed seed; a reserved name; an unknown id; a malformed table; a Poisson phase whose mean interarrival is below 100 base units; a rate or bandwidth under unit tick; reserved units above capacity or above a class limit, or a request that can never be granted; a window on crash-instance; a population larger than its behavior's max; Input samples "<stream>" but declares no seed.; via together with after; workloads without a horizon, warmup >= horizon or stopAt < horizon.

### Trace, format version 2

Returned by the Rust engine for a version-2 input (engine/exegra-engine/src/v2.rs) and read by the metric-bound claims (src/core/metric-claims.js): the version-1 trace with integer times, instances, resource and link state, draws and metrics. A version-1 input still returns a version-1 trace. Remaining (WG-4741): crash-instance, meters and the cost metric. Normative definition: docs/simulation.md, "Engine input and trace format version 2".

#### Inputs

- input (engine-input-v2): The engine input.

#### Outputs

- version, timeUnit, seed (2, string, decimal string | null): Copied from the input.
- configuration ({time, machines, pending, resources, links, timers, workloads, meters, routeCounters, streams, down, incarnations}): Machines keyed <behavior> for a single undeclared instance, else <behavior>#<index or canonical JSON key>; holdings per instance and class; timers by stable id; draw counters per stream; pending entries carry scheduledAt and, when addressed to an instance, its incarnation.
- steps[].kind ("deliver" | "drop" | "delay" | "service-done" | "wait-timeout" | "link-done" | "fault-edge" | "client-timeout"): Internal entries are steps of their own and count against the step limit.
- steps[].event ({id, payload, seq, source, scheduledAt, target?, from?, routes?, timer?, incarnation?}): Sources add arrival, timer, resource, link and fault-edge kinds.
- steps[].fired[].instance / ignored[].instance (number | string): The instance the transition ran on; ignored reason adds no-instance.
- steps[].resources ({resource, class, request, action, at, waited?, served?, reason?}[]): enqueued, granted, completed, released, refused, shed, dropped, timed-out, in the order they happened.
- steps[].draws ({stream, index, value}[]): Every random draw of the step in order (for an arrival instant: arrival, batch, then client and payload per entry); value as a decimal string.
- steps[].faults[] ({fault, effect}[]): Adds effects scaled, capacity, limit, collapsed, replaced, crashed, stale and restarted.
- outcome, truncated ("quiescent" | "step-limit" | "error" | "stopped", boolean): A truncated run never satisfies a metric claim.
- metrics (Metrics): Integer measured outputs over [warmup, horizon), from the request lifecycle of docs/simulation.md: requests start when their start entry is first taken as head, a lost start ends as error, only ok requests are finite samples, and counts of unkeyed, duplicate and late ends are reported; nearest-rank percentiles and optional series.
- initialDraws ({stream, index, value}[]): Draws made at the start of the run, in order.

#### Preconditions

- The input is a valid version-2 engine input

#### Postconditions

- Deterministic: the same input, seed and faults give deep-equal traces and metrics
- Every number in the trace other than variable and payload values is an integer
- Held units stay within class limits and capacity, and waiting requests within their bounds, at every settled step (a mid-run capacity cut keeps held units until released)
- The lift of a version-1 input that meets the side conditions of lift_agrees gives this trace minus the version-2 fields equal to the version-1 trace
- With output metrics, the metrics equal those computed from the full trace of the same input

#### Errors

- failed step: Version-1 failures, plus: Value <what> exceeds 2^53.; Value demand scale of "<r>" exceeds 2^32.; Reference "<path>" is ambiguous: "<behavior>" has <n> instances.; Aggregate "<path>" needs numbers, got <type>.; Behavior "<b>" would exceed <M> instances.; Route of "<event>" to "<behavior>" needs payload field "<field>".; Instance "<key>" releases <n> units of "<r>" class "<c>" but holds <m>.; Effect replies to "<event>", which has no requester.; Size of emit "<event>" must be an integer in [0, 2^40), got <value>.

### Read a project

GET /__exegra/project?name=: the persistence client's readProject (src/persist/client.js), the development-server route and tools/workspace.js read().

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- files ({spec: {text, hash, path}, layout: {text, hash} | null, recorded: object | null}): The specification with its sha-256 and its path relative to the repository, the layout or null, and the parsed evidence/results.json or null. In simulated traces spec and layout carry the hashes only.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Preconditions

- The project name is valid
- Over HTTP: the Host and any Origin are loopback and the x-exegra-client header is present

#### Postconditions

- Nothing on disk changes
- The hashes are those of the texts returned

#### Errors

- not-found: The project has no specification (HTTP 400).
- name.invalid / project.invalid: Invalid name or malformed project.jsonc (HTTP 400).
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### List projects

GET /__exegra/projects: src/persist/client.js listProjects, tools/workspace.js listProjects (every directory under specs/ with a spec.jsonc or a project.jsonc).

#### Inputs

- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- list ({projects: {name, title}[]}): Project names with the title read from each specification.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Postconditions

- Nothing on disk changes

#### Errors

- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### Subscribe to project changes

GET /__exegra/events?name=: a server-sent-events stream (src/persist/client.js watchProject, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js openEvents, tools/workspace.js watch). The stream answers with no response of its own; each change is a one-way message of contract file-change-notice.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name.

#### Preconditions

- The Host and any Origin are loopback (the x-exegra-client header is not required for the stream, since EventSource cannot send it)

#### Postconditions

- The subscription takes the current hashes of spec, layout and recorded results as its baseline; a change made while no stream is open is never notified
- A ping comment keeps the stream alive every 25 s; the browser reopens a dropped stream

#### Errors

- name.invalid: Invalid project name, before any header is sent.

### File change notice

One server-sent event "change" with data {file, hash}: written by tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js openEvents when tools/workspace.js watch() sees a tracked file's hash change after an 80 ms debounce. Own writes are notified too; the session filters them by hash.

#### Inputs

- file ('spec' | 'layout' | 'recorded' | 'products'): The file whose hash changed.
- hash (string | null): Its new sha-256, or null when it was removed.

#### Postconditions

- A one-way message: nothing answers it
- Changes under .history/, generated/ and *.tmp are not notified

### Export a project bundle

GET /__exegra/export?name=: src/persist/client.js exportProject, tools/workspace.js exportProject.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- bundle ({format: 'exegra-project', version: 1, name: string, files}): files: {"spec.jsonc", "layout.jsonc"?, "evidence/results.json"?} as read from disk at that moment. In simulated traces files carries the specification's hash.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Postconditions

- Nothing on disk changes
- The bundle holds the files on disk, not the session's in-memory text: an edit whose save has not landed is not in it

#### Errors

- not-found: The project has no specification (HTTP 400).
- name.invalid: Invalid project name.
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### Import a project bundle

POST /__exegra/import {name, bundle}: src/persist/client.js importProject, tools/workspace.js importProject. Outside the transaction system: claim edits-only-via-transactions is violated by this path.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name to create or replace.
- bundle (object): An exegra-project bundle. In simulated traces it carries the specification's hash.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- done ({ok: true}): Every file of the bundle was written.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Preconditions

- The bundle passes validateBundle (format, version, known file names, strings within 5 MB); the toolbar has refused a specification with syntax errors (analyzeSpec), but not one with validation errors

#### Postconditions

- The files present in the bundle are overwritten one after another with atomic renames, without a base-hash check and without a journal line; files absent from the bundle are left alone
- A running session sees the change through its watcher like any external change

#### Errors

- bundle.invalid / too-large: The bundle is malformed: HTTP 400 {ok: false, error} with no code field; the recorder labels it bundle.invalid.
- name.invalid / body.invalid / body.too-large: Invalid name or body.
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### Write generated artifacts

POST /__exegra/generated {name, files}: src/persist/client.js writeGenerated (from the Realisation panel), tools/workspace.js writeGenerated.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name.
- files (object): Relative .json or .md paths under generated/ to their text. In simulated traces it carries the semantic hash the documents were derived from.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- written ({ok: true, written: string[]}): The files whose content changed and were rewritten.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Postconditions

- Each changed file is replaced atomically under specs/<project>/generated/; unchanged files are skipped
- No base-hash check, no journal line, no change notice (the watcher ignores generated/)

#### Errors

- path.unsafe / input.invalid: A key is not a relative .json or .md path inside generated/, or a value is not a string.
- name.invalid / body.invalid / body.too-large: Invalid name or body.
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### Run in the engine worker

The messages between src/app/engine-runner.js and src/app/engine-worker.js: one engine input as JSON text in, one trace as JSON text or an error out, paired by id.

#### Inputs

- id (number): The runner's sequence number for the job.
- input (string): JSON text of the SimulationInput (format 1 or 2). In simulated traces the scenario id stands for it.

#### Outputs

- reply ({id: number, ok: true, trace: string}): JSON text of the trace.

#### Postconditions

- A reply carries the id of its job; the runner ignores a reply whose id is no longer waiting
- A new run while one is waiting terminates the worker, so the old job is never answered; the session also discards a run whose token is stale

#### Errors

- engine.refused: The engine refused the input or could not be loaded: {id, ok: false, error}; the recorder labels it engine.refused.

## Behaviors

### Interaction layer: submit an edit and show its outcome

The scene, inspector or a dialog submits a typed operation and later shows the result: accepted, or the rejection with its diagnostics.

- Subject: Visual interaction and application entry
- Initial state: Idle

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| refusalShown | boolean | false |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| interaction-idle | Idle | no |
| interaction-awaiting | Awaiting the transaction result | no |
| interaction-done | Edit accepted | yes |
| interaction-refused | Edit refused and rejection shown | yes |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| interaction-submit | Idle | Awaiting the transaction result | Edit requested | - | emit operation-submitted | no |
| interaction-accept | Awaiting the transaction result | Edit accepted | Transaction committed | - | - | no |
| interaction-refuse | Awaiting the transaction result | Edit refused and rejection shown | Transaction rejected | - | set refusalShown = true | no |

### Source editor: apply a draft and stay in sync

The editor shows the committed text. Applying a draft submits ReplaceSource; a rejection keeps the draft as typed. After any commit the editor shows the committed revision.

- Subject: Source editor
- Initial state: In sync with the model

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| shownRevision | number | 1 |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| source-in-sync | In sync with the model | no |
| source-applying | Applying a draft | no |
| source-draft-kept | Draft kept after a rejection | yes |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| source-apply | In sync with the model | Applying a draft | Source apply requested | - | emit operation-submitted | no |
| source-refresh-after-apply | Applying a draft | In sync with the model | Model published | - | set shownRevision = event.revision | no |
| source-keep-draft | Applying a draft | Draft kept after a rejection | Transaction rejected | - | - | no |
| source-refresh-after-visual-edit | In sync with the model | In sync with the model | Model published | - | set shownRevision = event.revision | no |

### Transaction system: check, validate, commit or reject

Preconditions are checked and text edits computed; the candidate is parsed and validated. With no errors it commits and the revision increments; otherwise it is rejected and nothing changes. Precondition failures, which reject before any text is computed, are abstracted into the rejection.

- Subject: Transaction system
- Initial state: Idle

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| revision | number | 1 |
| rejected | boolean | false |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| tx-idle | Idle | no |
| tx-validating | Candidate being parsed and validated | no |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| tx-begin | Idle | Candidate being parsed and validated | Operation submitted | - | emit text-edits-computed | no |
| tx-commit-unvalidated | Candidate being parsed and validated | Idle | Candidate model parsed | - | set revision = revision + 1; set rejected = false; emit transaction-committed with revision = revision | yes |
| tx-reject-partially-applied | Candidate being parsed and validated | Idle | Validation failed | - | set revision = revision + 1; set rejected = true; emit transaction-rejected | yes |
| tx-commit | Candidate being parsed and validated | Idle | Validation passed | - | set revision = revision + 1; set rejected = false; emit transaction-committed with revision = revision | no |
| tx-reject | Candidate being parsed and validated | Idle | Validation failed | - | set rejected = true; emit transaction-rejected | no |

The faulty-input alternative is a faultOnly transition declared before the normal one so that it takes priority while the fault is active.

- Subject: Validator

| validator-accept | Ready | Ready | Candidate model parsed | - | set lastResult = 'passed'; emit validation-passed | no |

### Session: refresh on commit, then save asynchronously

onCommit re-evaluates claims, re-projects and re-lays-out the scene (the renderer receives it), then announces the model to the inspector and source editor, and starts the flush. The flush writes later and asynchronously; the save state follows its result.

- Subject: Application session
- Initial state: Saved and in sync

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| saveState | string | saved |
| rejectionsLogged | number | 0 |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| session-in-sync | Saved and in sync | no |
| session-refreshing | Refreshing the scene | no |
| session-save-pending | Save in flight | no |
| session-conflicted | Save conflict unresolved | yes |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| session-swallow-conflict | Save in flight | Saved and in sync | Save conflicted | - | set saveState = 'saved' | yes |
| session-refresh-on-rejection | Saved and in sync | Refreshing the scene | Transaction rejected | - | emit refresh-requested with revision = transaction-flow.revision | yes |
| session-on-commit | Saved and in sync | Refreshing the scene | Transaction committed | - | emit refresh-requested with revision = event.revision | no |
| session-on-refreshed | Refreshing the scene | Save in flight | Projection refreshed | - | set saveState = 'saving'; emit model-published with revision = event.revision; emit save-requested after 1 with revision = event.revision | no |
| session-on-saved | Save in flight | Saved and in sync | Save succeeded | - | set saveState = 'saved' | no |
| session-on-conflict | Save in flight | Save conflict unresolved | Save conflicted | - | set saveState = 'conflict' | no |
| session-on-rejection | Saved and in sync | Saved and in sync | Transaction rejected | - | set rejectionsLogged = rejectionsLogged + 1 | no |

### File service: replace the file or report a conflict

The HTTP hop between the persistence client, the plugin and the workspace service is abstracted. persistedRevision is the revision whose text the file on disk holds; conflict records that the last write was refused because the file on disk differed from the base hash.

- Subject: Workspace service
- Initial state: File matches the base hash

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| persistedRevision | number | 1 |
| conflict | boolean | false |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| workspace-clean | File matches the base hash | no |
| workspace-conflicted | Write refused as a conflict | yes |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| workspace-overwrite-on-conflict | File matches the base hash | Write refused as a conflict | Save requested | - | set conflict = true; set persistedRevision = event.revision; emit save-conflicted | yes |
| workspace-detect-conflict | File matches the base hash | Write refused as a conflict | Save requested | - | set conflict = true; emit save-conflicted | yes |
| workspace-write | File matches the base hash | File matches the base hash | Save requested | - | set persistedRevision = event.revision; emit save-succeeded | no |

### Persistence client: write a file, retry after a conflict

Sends the text with the base hash it last read. A conflict returns the current hash, which becomes the new base hash for one more request; an accepted write ends the flow. The retry abstracts the user's "Overwrite disk" (session.resolveConflict('overwrite'), which takes the conflict's hash as base hash and flushes again): the real persistence client and the session never retry on their own. project-session-flow models that resolution as it is.

- Subject: Persistence client
- Initial state: Idle

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| attempts | number | 0 |
| baseHash | string | h1 |
| saved | boolean | false |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| write-client-idle | Idle | no |
| write-client-awaiting | Awaiting the answer | no |
| write-client-saved | Written | yes |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| write-client-send | Idle | Awaiting the answer | Write intended | - | set attempts = attempts + 1; emit write-file-request with baseHash = baseHash, file = 'spec', name = 'exegra', text = 'spec text of revision 2' | no |
| write-client-retry | Awaiting the answer | Awaiting the answer | Write refused | attempts < 3 | set attempts = attempts + 1; set baseHash = event.current; emit write-file-request with baseHash = event.current, file = 'spec', name = 'exegra', text = 'spec text of revision 2' | no |
| write-client-done | Awaiting the answer | Written | Write accepted | - | set saved = true | no |

### Development server: accept a write or refuse it as a conflict

POST /__exegra/write: the file is replaced only when the base hash equals the hash of the text on disk; otherwise it is left untouched and the conflict carries the current hash.

- Subject: Development-server plugin
- Initial state: Ready

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| diskHash | string | h2 |
| writes | number | 0 |
| conflicts | number | 0 |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| write-endpoint-ready | Ready | no |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| write-endpoint-conflict | Ready | Ready | Write file | event.baseHash != diskHash | set conflicts = conflicts + 1; emit write-file-conflict with code = 'conflict', current = diskHash | no |
| write-endpoint-accept | Ready | Ready | Write file | event.baseHash == diskHash | set diskHash = 'h3'; set writes = writes + 1; emit write-file-ok with hash = 'h3', ok = true | no |

### Persistence client: one HTTP call per request

src/persist/client.js: each call is one fetch (request()) or the EventSource of watchProject, and each answer is handed back to its caller once, with no retry, queue or buffer. lastFile stands for the file variable of the session's flush loop, which knows which file a write answers.

- Subject: Persistence client
- Initial state: Ready

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| lastFile | string | spec |
| calls | number | 0 |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| client-ready | Ready | no |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| client-read | Ready | Ready | Read requested | - | set calls = calls + 1; emit read-project-request with name = event.name, requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-read-ok | Ready | Ready | Project read | - | emit project-files-read with layoutHash = event.layout, recorded = event.recorded, requestId = event.requestId, specHash = event.spec | no |
| client-read-failed | Ready | Ready | Project not read | - | emit project-read-failed with requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-list | Ready | Ready | Project list requested | - | set calls = calls + 1; emit list-projects-request with requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-listed | Ready | Ready | Projects listed | - | emit projects-listed with projects = event.projects | no |
| client-write | Ready | Ready | Write intended | - | set calls = calls + 1; set lastFile = event.file; emit write-file-request with baseHash = event.baseHash, file = event.file, name = event.name, requestId = event.requestId, text = event.text | no |
| client-write-ok | Ready | Ready | Write accepted | - | emit save-succeeded with file = lastFile, hash = event.hash, requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-write-conflict | Ready | Ready | Write refused | - | emit save-conflicted with currentHash = event.current, file = lastFile, requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-write-failed | Ready | Ready | Write failed | - | emit save-failed with file = lastFile, requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-watch | Ready | Ready | Watch requested | - | emit watch-project-request with name = event.name | no |
| client-notice | Ready | Ready | File changed | - | emit external-change-received with file = event.file, hash = event.hash | no |
| client-export | Ready | Ready | Export requested | - | set calls = calls + 1; emit export-request with name = event.name, requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-export-ok | Ready | Ready | Bundle exported | - | emit bundle-received with files = event.files, requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-export-failed | Ready | Ready | Export refused | - | emit export-refused-received with requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-import | Ready | Ready | Import requested | - | set calls = calls + 1; emit import-request with bundle = event.bundle, name = event.name, requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-import-ok | Ready | Ready | Bundle imported | - | emit import-done with requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-import-refused | Ready | Ready | Bundle refused | - | emit import-refused-received with requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-generated | Ready | Ready | Generated documents to write | - | set calls = calls + 1; emit generated-write-request with files = event.files, name = event.name, requestId = event.requestId | no |
| client-generated-ok | Ready | Ready | Generated documents written | - | emit generated-saved with requestId = event.requestId, written = event.written | no |

### Development server: route each endpoint to the workspace service

route() of tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js: each request becomes one call of tools/workspace.js and its result one answer (200, 409 for a write conflict, 400 for a workspace refusal); openEvents subscribes to watch() and writes each change as a server-sent event. The request guard (checkRequest) is not modelled as transitions; its refusals are errors of the contracts.

- Subject: Development-server plugin
- Initial state: Listening

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| requests | number | 0 |

| dev-write-ok | Listening | Listening | Workspace write accepted | event.caller == 'http' | emit write-file-ok with hash = event.hash, ok = true, requestId = event.requestId | no |

| dev-write-error | Listening | Listening | Workspace write failed after the replace | event.caller == 'http' | emit write-file-failed with code = 'internal', requestId = event.requestId | no |

| dev-change | Listening | Listening | Workspace change detected | - | emit file-change-notice-sent with file = event.file, hash = event.hash | no |

| dev-import | Listening | Listening | Import bundle | - | set requests = requests + 1; emit ws-import with bundle = event.bundle, name = event.name, requestId = event.requestId | no |
| dev-import-ok | Listening | Listening | Workspace import finished | - | emit import-ok with ok = true, requestId = event.requestId | no |

### Workspace service: the project files on disk

tools/workspace.js on one project. The variables are what the disk holds: diskSpec, diskLayout, recordedVersion and generatedHash stand for the files by their hashes; journalLines counts the lines of .history/journal.jsonl; specWrites, layoutWrites and imports count replacements by write() and importProject(). caller tells the endpoint's calls (http) from the command line's (cli), which reach the same functions.

- Subject: Workspace service
- Initial state: Ready

| layoutWrites | number | 0 |

### Workspace watcher: notify hash changes of tracked files

watch() of tools/workspace.js for one subscription: off until the stream subscribes, then every replacement of spec, layout or recorded results, own writes included, is notified 80 ms later (one logical tick).

- Subject: Workspace service
- Initial state: Not subscribed

| watcher-notify | Subscribed | Subscribed | Project file replaced on disk | - | set notices = notices + 1; emit ws-change after 1 with file = event.file, hash = event.hash | no |

### Project: commits, undo, redo and reload

Project in src/core/project.js seen from the texts it holds: specHash and layoutHash stand for the current texts by their hashes, undoHash and redoHash for the text one undo or redo would restore (this machine keeps one level of each). Every change is a commit that notifies subscribers (commit-notified) with its origin. Project.reload (origin external) clears both stacks.

- Subject: Transaction system
- Initial state: Ready

| --- | --- | --- |
| revision | number | 1 |
| specHash | string | none |
| layoutHash | string | none |
| undoHash | string | none |
| redoHash | string | none |
| undoDepth | number | 0 |
| redoDepth | number | 0 |

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| history-ready | Ready | no |

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| history-edit | Ready | Ready | Edit applied | - | set undoHash = specHash; set specHash = event.specHash; set undoDepth = undoDepth + 1; set redoDepth = 0; set revision = revision + 1; emit commit-notified with file = 'spec', origin = event.origin, text = event.specHash | no |

| history-undo-keeps-text | Ready | Ready | Undo requested | undoDepth > 0 | set undoDepth = undoDepth - 1; set redoDepth = redoDepth + 1; set revision = revision + 1; emit commit-notified with file = 'spec', origin = 'undo', text = specHash | yes |
| history-undo | Ready | Ready | Undo requested | undoDepth > 0 | set redoHash = specHash; set specHash = undoHash; set undoDepth = undoDepth - 1; set redoDepth = redoDepth + 1; set revision = revision + 1; emit commit-notified with file = 'spec', origin = 'undo', text = specHash | no |
| history-redo | Ready | Ready | Redo requested | redoDepth > 0 | set undoHash = specHash; set specHash = redoHash; set undoDepth = undoDepth + 1; set redoDepth = redoDepth - 1; set revision = revision + 1; emit commit-notified with file = 'spec', origin = 'redo', text = specHash | no |

### Session: open, save, conflicts and external changes

src/app/session.js from the files' point of view: init lists projects and opens one; open reads the files and takes their hashes as base hashes; onCommit marks the changed file pending and flush writes it with the base hash (a commit with origin external is not written); the answer replaces the base hash, stores a conflict or records an error; resolveConflict reloads or overwrites; onExternalChange ignores the session's own writes by hash, re-reads the project and adopts the change only when nothing is pending. textHash and layoutHash stand for the texts the session holds; reqSeq numbers its requests. persistent is false in a production build, which reads the bundled specifications and never writes.

- Subject: Application session
- Initial state: No project open

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| persistent | boolean | true |
| projectName | string | none |
| pendingName | string | none |
| baseHash | string | none |
| layoutBase | string | none |
| textHash | string | none |
| layoutHash | string | none |
| saveState | string | none |
| pendingAgain | boolean | false |
| conflictHash | string | none |
| reqSeq | number | 0 |
| readPurpose | string | none |
| recordedVersion | string | none |
| externalReloads | number | 0 |
| ownNotices | number | 0 |
| errorsLogged | number | 0 |
| projectsListed | number | 0 |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| session-closed | No project open | no |
| session-opening | Reading the project | no |
| session-open | Open and saved | no |
| session-saving | Save in flight | no |
| session-conflict | Save conflict unresolved | no |
| session-error | Save failed | no |
| session-readonly | Open read-only | no |

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| session-open-read | No project open | Reading the project | Project opened by the user | persistent | set pendingName = event.name; set readPurpose = 'open'; set reqSeq = reqSeq + 1; emit read-intent with name = event.name, purpose = 'open', requestId = reqSeq | no |
| session-open-shipped | No project open | Open read-only | Project opened by the user | !persistent | set projectName = event.name; set textHash = 'shipped'; set saveState = 'readonly'; emit project-opened with layoutHash = 'shipped-layout', name = event.name, specHash = 'shipped' | no |

| session-opened | Reading the project | Open and saved | Project files received | - | set projectName = pendingName; set baseHash = event.specHash; set textHash = event.specHash; set layoutBase = event.layoutHash; set layoutHash = event.layoutHash; set recordedVersion = event.recorded; set saveState = 'saved'; set pendingAgain = false; set conflictHash = 'none'; emit project-opened with layoutHash = event.layoutHash, name = pendingName, specHash = event.specHash; emit watch-intent with name = pendingName | no |

### Claim evaluator: evaluate every claim, synchronously

evaluateClaims of src/core/claims.js runs on the caller's thread; in the application that is the main thread, inside the commit. Trace and metric claims run engine scenarios there (claim-scenarios-run), not in the engine worker.

### Toolbar: export and import project bundles

The Export and Import handlers of src/ui/toolbar.js. Export downloads what the server reads from disk. ### Traffic recorder: count each recorded write event

Every request and answer of a write that the persistence client sees is one recorded entry. traffic-step-recorded stands for the append to the stream; no real run produces it as an event (recording the recording would feed itself), so this machine is not part of live-session.

- Subject: Traffic recorder
- Initial state: Recording

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| lines | number | 0 |

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| recorder-on | Recording | no |

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| recorder-request | Recording | Recording | Write file | - | set lines = lines + 1; emit traffic-step-recorded with event = 'write-file-request' | no |
| recorder-ok | Recording | Recording | Write accepted | - | set lines = lines + 1; emit traffic-step-recorded with event = 'write-file-ok' | no |
| recorder-conflict | Recording | Recording | Write refused | - | set lines = lines + 1; emit traffic-step-recorded with event = 'write-file-conflict' | no |

### Session: load a scenario, adopt its trace, step through it

loadSim and the cursor of src/app/session.js: a load takes a new token and hands the engine input to the runner; a trace is adopted only if its token is still the current one; step, back and run to end move stepIndex within the revealed steps. stepCount stands for the length of the finished trace.

- Subject: Application session
- Initial state: Simulation panel

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| token | number | 0 |
| adoptedToken | number | 0 |
| scenarioId | string | none |
| stepIndex | number | -1 |
| stepCount | number | 0 |
| running | boolean | false |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| sim-idle | Simulation panel | no |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| sim-load | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Scenario loaded in the panel | - | set token = token + 1; set scenarioId = event.scenario; set running = true; set stepIndex = -1; set stepCount = 0; emit engine-run-intent with scenario = event.scenario, token = token | no |
| sim-adopt-stale | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Trace handed to the session | - | set adoptedToken = event.token; set running = false; set stepCount = event.steps; set stepIndex = -1 | yes |
| sim-adopt | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Trace handed to the session | event.token == token | set adoptedToken = event.token; set running = false; set stepCount = event.steps; set stepIndex = -1 | no |
| sim-failed | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Run failed | event.token == token | set running = false; set scenarioId = 'none' | no |
| sim-step-past-end | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Step | stepCount > 0 | set stepIndex = stepIndex + 1; emit sim-step-shown with stepIndex = stepIndex | yes |
| sim-step | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Step | stepIndex < stepCount - 1 | set stepIndex = stepIndex + 1; emit sim-step-shown with stepIndex = stepIndex | no |
| sim-step-at-end | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Step | stepCount > 0 | set running = false | no |
| sim-back | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Step back | stepIndex >= 0 | set stepIndex = stepIndex - 1 | no |
| sim-run-to-end | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Run to end | stepCount > 0 | set stepIndex = stepCount - 1 | no |
| sim-reset | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Simulation reset requested | stepCount > 0 | set stepIndex = -1 | no |
| sim-seek | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Simulation seek requested | stepCount > 0 | set stepIndex = event.stepIndex | no |
| sim-project-opened | Simulation panel | Simulation panel | Project opened | - | set token = token + 1; set scenarioId = 'none'; set stepIndex = -1; set stepCount = 0 | no |

### Engine runner: one job at a time in the worker

run() of src/app/engine-runner.js: a run while another is waiting stops the worker (terminate, waiting cleared) and starts a new one; a reply is matched by id to the waiting job; when the worker cannot load, the waiting job reruns on the main thread. prevId and prevToken exist only for the defect that keeps a replaced job.

- Subject: Simulation engine
- Initial state: Ready

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| seq | number | 0 |
| waitingId | number | 0 |
| waitingToken | number | 0 |
| prevId | number | 0 |
| prevToken | number | 0 |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| runner-ready | Ready | no |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| runner-keep-stale-job | Ready | Ready | Run handed to the runner | waitingId != 0 | set prevId = waitingId; set prevToken = waitingToken; set seq = seq + 1; set waitingId = seq; set waitingToken = event.token; emit engine-run-posted with id = seq, input = event.scenario | yes |
| runner-restart | Ready | Ready | Run handed to the runner | waitingId != 0 | set seq = seq + 1; set waitingId = seq; set waitingToken = event.token; emit worker-terminated; emit engine-run-posted with id = seq, input = event.scenario | no |
| runner-post | Ready | Ready | Run handed to the runner | - | set seq = seq + 1; set waitingId = seq; set waitingToken = event.token; emit engine-run-posted with id = seq, input = event.scenario | no |
| runner-forward-stale | Ready | Ready | Trace returned by the worker | prevId != 0 && event.id == prevId | emit sim-trace-ready with steps = 3, token = prevToken | yes |
| runner-forward | Ready | Ready | Trace returned by the worker | event.id == waitingId | set waitingId = 0; emit sim-trace-ready with steps = 3, token = waitingToken | no |
| runner-refused | Ready | Ready | Run refused by the worker | event.id == waitingId | set waitingId = 0; emit sim-run-failed with token = waitingToken | no |
| runner-main-thread | Ready | Ready | Worker could not load | waitingId != 0 | set waitingId = 0; emit sim-trace-ready with steps = 3, token = waitingToken | no |

### Engine worker: run an input, reply with its trace

src/app/engine-worker.js: one run per message, answered with the same id. A terminated worker answers nothing: generation counts terminations and a run of an earlier generation is never answered.

### Camera memory: save per view, restore on switch

saveCamera and pushScene of src/app/main.js: pointer-up and wheel save the camera under the current project and view; a view switch first restores the camera stored for the new view (once per key), then saveCamera stores the current camera under the new view. A view with nothing stored therefore inherits, and stores, the previous view's camera.

### Agent command line: one command per process

runCommand of cli/commands.js for apply, verify and trace: the project is read once (loadProject, tools/workspace.js read()); apply runs Project.apply in process and writes the spec, then the layout, each with the hash read at the start; verify runs the test evidence, records the results and re-evaluates the claims; trace runs the engine in process and writes the trace file. One envelope answers the invocation. The texts apply produces are fixed here (h2, L2): the transaction itself is the edit-flow model.

- Subject: Agent command line
- Initial state: Not started

| layoutRead | string | none |
| wroteSpec | boolean | false |
| reportedOk | boolean | false |

| engineOk | boolean | false |
| traceWritten | boolean | false |

| --- | --- | --- |

| cli-loading | Reading the project | no |
| cli-applying | Writing the specification | no |

| cli-verifying | Running the evidence | no |
| cli-tracing | Running the engine | no |
| cli-done | Envelope written | yes |

| cli-apply-read | Reading the project | Writing the specification | Workspace read returned | event.caller == 'cli' && command == 'apply' | set specRead = event.spec; set layoutRead = event.layout; emit ws-write after 2 with baseHash = event.spec, caller = 'cli', file = 'spec', name = 'exegra', requestId = reqId, text = 'h2' | no |
