| sim-flow-matches-trace | invariant | Supported by external evidence | For every simulation step, the message flow names as sender the component whose behavior emitted the delivered event (outside, for a scenario stimulus or an injected fault) and as receivers the components whose behaviors fired a transition on it. |
| sim-flow-never-borrows-edges | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A simulated message is drawn along a scene edge only when that edge connects its sender's and receiver's drawn nodes; when only a relationship the view does not draw connects them the message is marked unshown, and when no relationship connects them it is marked undeclared. |
| sim-step-shows-message-moving | temporal | Supported by external evidence | Stepping a simulation in a structural view moves a marker labelled with the event's name from the sender's node toward each receiver's node, and the simulator panel states who sent the event to whom. |
| simulation-seeded-runs-reproducible | invariant | Not evaluated | The same model, scenario, faults and seed give deep-equal traces and metrics, draws included, in the Rust engine and in the Lean semantics; on a fixture that samples, two different seeds give different draws; and the seeds of a claim's runs are the successive SplitMix64 outputs of its base seed, with arithmetic mod 2^64. |
| simulation-instances-are-independent | invariant | Not evaluated | N instances of one behavior keep separate state: an effect of one instance changes no variable or state of another, every fired and ignored entry of a step names its instance, and an event routed to one instance fires on that instance only. |
| specs-declare-aspects-as-data | invariant | Not evaluated | A specification names simulation aspects with versions and gives their parameters as data; an unknown aspect or version is a validation error, and no specification text is evaluated as code. |
| aspect-runs-reproducible | invariant | Not evaluated | The same specification, aspect versions, parameters and seed give deep-equal traces, including every aspect metric. |
| refinement-agrees-at-boundary | invariant | Not evaluated | When a component has an abstract behavior and refined child behaviors, the refined trace projected onto the component's boundary is allowed by the abstract behavior, or the first diverging boundary event is reported as a violation. |
| simulation-depth-follows-collapse | temporal | Not evaluated | Collapsing a subsystem in the view runs its abstract behavior and expanding it runs its children's, and the simulator panel says which level each component ran at. |
| chaos-campaigns-reproducible | invariant | Not evaluated | The same campaign and seed give deep-equal sets of runs, and every run's check results are reported. |
| chaos-failures-only-as-declared | invariant | Not evaluated | A chaos campaign injects only the failure kinds and targets its parameters name, and records each injected failure in the trace. |
| load-matches-queueing-theory | measured | Not evaluated | Single-station M/M/1 and M/M/c queues reproduce the analytic mean waiting time and utilisation within the tolerance and number of seeded runs stated by the test. |
| load-parameters-sourced | invariant | Not evaluated | Every load parameter (arrival rate, service time, capacity) names its source, a requirement, a measurement or an assumption, and a parameter without one is a validation error. |
| cost-prices-sourced | invariant | Not evaluated | Every price names a source and a date, and a cost that would use an unsourced price is reported as not computed, naming the price. |
| cost-follows-usage | invariant | Not evaluated | A reported cost equals the recorded resource usage multiplied by the price profile, line by line. |
| results-report-ranges | invariant | Not evaluated | A result over a parameter given as a range is reported as a spread (minimum, maximum and stated percentiles) across a seeded sweep, never as a single figure. |
| statistical-claims-state-their-basis | invariant | Not evaluated | Every statistical claim result names its number of runs, seed and confidence, is never shown as verified, and a statistical claim without that basis is a validation error. |
| overlays-follow-trace | invariant | Not evaluated | Each overlay value shown at a time equals the trace's value for that component at that time, and a component without data shows nothing. |
| engines-agree-on-traces | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The Rust simulation engine, the differential oracle, native and WebAssembly, reproduces every golden trace of the conformance suite exactly, as the Lean engine does. |
| simulation-contract-pinned | invariant | Supported by external evidence | For every scenario of the golden conformance suite, the simulation engine reproduces the committed trace exactly, both from the model and from the committed engine input alone, and simulationInput derives exactly the committed input. |
| simulation-runs-off-main-thread | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The Simulation tab runs a scenario in a Web Worker: while a long run is in progress the page keeps drawing frames and reacting to input, the tab shows the run as running, and the finished trace is then stepped exactly as before. |
| ui-touch-usable-on-phone | invariant | Supported by external evidence | At a 390 by 844 touch viewport the page never scrolls horizontally, every visible interactive control is at least 44 by 44 CSS pixels, and with no panel expanded the 3D scene covers at least 60% of the screen. |
| ui-expands-to-desktop | invariant | Supported by external evidence | From 768 CSS pixels wide panels open beside the scene instead of over it, and from 1200 CSS pixels the explorer, the scene and the context panel are visible side by side without opening anything. |
| fault-model-scoped-claims | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Every behavioral or reliability claim names the fault model it is evaluated under, every failure-mode rate, duration and bound names its source, and the fault model appears in each such claim result's scope. |
| exhaustive-exploration-sound | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Exhaustive exploration within stated bounds finds the known race of a fixture protocol with a counterexample run that Lean accepts, reports its fixed version as holding for all N explored configurations, and gives the same N on every run. |
| deployment-cutover-safe | invariant | Not evaluated | Under the fault model and within the stated bounds, no request is served by a version that failed its health gate during a blue-green cutover, and old and new versions are compatible with the schema at every step of an expand/contract migration. |
| deployment-rollback-durable | invariant | Not evaluated | Under the fault model and within the stated bounds, a rollback loses no committed transaction and returns traffic only to a version that passed its gate. |
| generated-infrastructure-matches-model | invariant | Not evaluated | The generated infrastructure and pipeline realise exactly the modeled environments, slots, gates and transitions, deterministically, and any edit to the generated output is detected. |
| implementation-refines-model | invariant | Not evaluated | Every implementation trace of a checked system, projected onto the model's boundary events, is allowed by the model, or the first disallowed boundary event is reported. |
| claims-monitored-in-production | invariant | Not evaluated | Each claim with a declared observable has a generated runtime monitor, and a monitor that fires marks its claim violated with the observation; game-day results are recorded as evidence with their measurements. |
| engine-matches-formal-semantics | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The Rust engine and the Lean formal semantics give identical traces on every golden and on every input of a recorded seeded run of the input generator, whose coverage of the language's constructs is stated. |
| formal-semantics-properties-proved | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The formal semantics' step function is proved deterministic, fires at most one transition per behavior per step, and terminates every run under the step limit, with no unproved assumption in the proofs. |
| invariants-proved-unbounded | invariant | Not evaluated | A state-invariant claim reported as verified by proof has a Lean theorem, building with no unproved assumption, that the invariant holds in every reachable configuration of its scenario under its fault model. |
| contract-events-validated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | An event that names a contract has a role, and validation rejects, with the position of the event, a request whose payload has a field that is not an input of the contract, an input that is not optional and missing, or a field type that is not assignable to the input type; a response with a field that is not an output; an error without a code, or with a literal code that is not among the contract's errors; and a correlation field that is not a payload field. |
| trace-contract-conformance | invariant | Supported by external evidence | On a simulated trace, every contract event's payload values fit the declared types, an error code is among the contract's errors, and every response or error answers an earlier unanswered request of the same contract (matched by the correlation field when one is declared, otherwise by count); each violation is a failed automatic check of the scenario with its step, shown by exegra simulate and the simulator panel, which also render the contract events as a request log. |
| data-model-rules-computed | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Records and copies are validated with positions, and the six data rules (erasure point, record erasure point, no principal data in a shared copy, party fields by principal id, freshness bound, holder related to owner) are computed from the model and reported per copy, record and field, both as validation warnings and as the result of a data-model claim. |
| personal-records-state-erasure | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Every record with a field that is personal data or a credential states an erasure point or a bounded retention; a record that states neither is a finding of the record-erasure-point rule, and a data-model claim naming that rule is violated with the record as its counterexample. |
| classification-validated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A field's optional classification is one of public, internal, personal or credential; personal is stated through the personal-data class and classification personal needs that class; an unknown classification is a validation error, and a specification without classifications validates unchanged. |
| specification-copies-fresh-and-related | structural | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | Every copy of a project's specification text that Exegra declares (the session model, the command line's project, the generated documents) states a freshness bound, and its holder is related to the specification file through declared relationships; the shared copy holds no principal data. |
| invariants-proved-within-bounds | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A state-invariant claim reported by exegra prove as holding has a Lean theorem, accepted by Lean, that the verified explorer visited every configuration of its scenario reachable within the stated bound (over every order of simultaneous events and every subset of the scenario's faults) and found the invariant true in each; the result names the axioms the theorem depends on. |
| trace-claim-verified-needs-negative | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A trace claim is reported verified only when it holds on every listed scenario and each of its declared negatives, of which there is at least one, violates it. A claim with no negative, or with a negative that holds or does not finish, is inconclusive. |
| negatives-are-validated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A negative whose scenario is not one of its claim's scenarios, whose fault list is missing or empty, or that names an undeclared fault or one its scenario already activates, is a validation error; renaming a fault rewrites the negatives that name it. |
| trace-claims-state-how-decided | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A trace claim's result says what its verdict rests on: the declaration order of two unguarded enabled transitions that decided which fired, a precedes ordering that only the scenario script witnesses (not verified), a never over an occurrence only a faultOnly transition can produce (holds for runs without the enabling fault), and an implication whose consequent names no state or variable (not exercised). |
| models-property-evaluated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The models property that links a check over the behavior model to a requirement is evaluated: a requirement's result lists the claims that name it with their statuses (modelledBy, shown by claims and in the inspector), without changing the requirement's own status, and a models value that is not a requirement is a validation error. |
| claim-parameters-always-validated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The checks of claims (parameters, scenarios, negatives, models) run even when another reference of the specification is unresolved, so one error does not hide the others; a negative whose fault targets a transition of a behavior its scenario does not run, and two unguarded transitions on one event and state, are warnings. |
| own-behavioral-claims-falsifiable | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Every behavioral claim of this specification declares a negative that violates it, and every one is verified. |
| resource-queues-respect-bounds | invariant | Not evaluated | At every settled step of every format-2 run, the units a request class holds never exceed its limit, the units held never exceed capacity (except units held across a mid-run capacity cut, which are never preempted), waiting requests never exceed the class and resource bounds, bounds are enforced class by class in declaration order and then for the resource, and the request removed is the one the overflow rule names (refuse and drop: the newest; shed-oldest: the oldest of the class, or for the resource bound the oldest overall under fifo and the oldest of the lowest-priority class under priority), with the matching refused event and reason. |
| resource-utilisation-at-most-one | invariant | Not evaluated | Service demand conserves work: for every resource and run, the integral of units held over the window never exceeds the integral of the capacity in effect (while no mid-run capacity cut leaves units held above it), a completed use request held its units for exactly ceil(draw times demand factor times scale), and the sum of completed service times equals the busy integral when the window covers the run. |
| format-1-unchanged-under-format-2 | invariant | Not evaluated | An engine that implements format 2 reproduces every format-1 golden trace byte for byte from its version-1 input; simulationInput derives the same version-1 input as before for every specification that uses no format-2 construct; and for every version-1 input with integral times in [0, 2^53), no variable or bare reference named instance, no three-part reference whose second part is count, sum, min, max or instances, and a run that never queues an entry at time 2^53 or later, its lift to version 2 gives the version-1 trace once the version-2 fields are removed. |
| engine-matches-formal-semantics-v2 | invariant | Not evaluated | The Rust engine and the Lean semantics give identical version-2 traces and metrics on every version-2 golden and on every input of a recorded seeded run of the version-2 input generator, whose coverage of instances, routes, timers, resources and their overflow rules, links, workloads, distributions and resource faults is stated. |
| metric-claims-report-basis | invariant | Not evaluated | Every metric-bound claim result reports its runs, seeds, passes, N', fraction, confidence, the spread of the per-run statistic and its underpowered and truncated runs; a positive result is model-holds, never verified and never supported; samples follow the request lifecycle; an underpowered run (a quantile, mean, or ratio of counts with fewer than 30 expected on its rarer side) is left out only for an open-started class and otherwise does not satisfy the bound; it reads model-holds or fails only when the exact binomial test on the N' runs kept decides it and inconclusive otherwise; and a negative that does not fail, or a control negative that does not hold, makes it inconclusive. |
| metrics-reproduce-mm1-utilisation | measured | Inconclusive | The M/M/1 fixture (Poisson arrivals at 800/s, exponential service of mean 1 ms, one server, unit us, warm-up 20 s, horizon 220 s) reproduces utilisation 0.8 within 2% by an approx metric claim at 95% confidence over 59 seeded runs. |
| pool-classes-protect-move-db-leg-under-flood | measured | Inconclusive | On the pool fixture (tests/fixtures/queueing/pool-flood.json: a 20-connection pool with classes money, moves and page-reads in front of 4 shared database I/O channels; moves at 1,000/s; a page-read flood of 5,000/s from 2 s to 6 s), the p99 of the modelled database leg of a move over the flood window is at most 30 ms in at least 95% of runs at 95% confidence over 59 runs, in the base scenario and in its control without the flood; it fails in each of its two negative twins, and each twin fault leaves the control holding. |
| response-time-counts-failures-as-unbounded | invariant | Not evaluated | In every response-time distribution, a measured request that ended refused, timeout or error (a lost start included, by the request lifecycle), or is censored, counts as +infinity, so only ok requests are finite and a mean over a class with any such request is undefined; a claim restricted to outcomes ok reports how many requests it left out. |
| sampled-explorations-never-verified | invariant | Not evaluated | exegra prove on a format-2 scenario reports the seed first, and the scope "fault times fixed as declared" when the scenario has windowed faults, and shows a result as verified only when the engine input contains no distribution other than fixed or choice anywhere and no element that draws (no Poisson process; no batch, think, closed start, population start.at, emit size or after, timer delay or restartAfter other than fixed or choice; no uniform client pick, groups, choose or sample payload field, or random route), a check made statically on the input; choice values and any routes are enumerated as nondeterminism. |
| metrics-reproduce-mm1-mean-wait | measured | Inconclusive | The M/M/1 fixture reproduces the mean waiting time in queue rho / (mu - lambda) = 4 ms within 5%, by an approx metric claim on the median of the per-run means at 95% confidence over 59 seeded runs (the order statistics 22 and 38), with the measured gap between the median of per-run means and the analytic mean stated beside the tolerance. |
| metrics-reproduce-mm2-erlang-c | measured | Not evaluated | The M/M/2 fixture (lambda = 1600/s, mean service 1 ms, two servers) reproduces the Erlang C mean waiting time C(2, 1.6) / (2 mu - lambda) of about 1.78 ms within 5% at 95% confidence over 59 seeded runs. |
| ladder-reacts-without-flapping | measured | Not evaluated | On the ladder fixture, a behavior reading a meter of refused page reads rises to rung 1 within 10 s of the flood start (time-to-first), makes at most 4 rung transitions during the flood, and returns within 30 s after it (time-to-recover), each at 95% confidence over 59 runs. |
| retry-budget-bounds-amplification | measured | Not evaluated | On the retry fixture (2,000 client behaviors with a 500 ms timeout, a database slowdown of 5 times from 20 s to 30 s), the busiest second of queries reaching the pool during the slowdown, relative to the mean per second over 10 to 20 s (the ratio form of metric-bound), is at most 1.5 when clients retry within a 10% budget, at 95% confidence over 59 runs. |
| reconnect-stagger-bounds-handshake-wait | measured | Not evaluated | On the reconnect fixture (4,000 client behaviors on 4 hosts, host 0 crashed at 30 s with a connection-reset notice and restarted after 2 s), clients that reconnect after a uniform 0 to 5 s stagger keep the p90 wait for the host CPU (TLS handshake demand) at most 200 ms in the window 30 to 40 s, at 95% confidence over 59 runs; about 1,000 handshakes per run leave 100 samples beyond the p90. |
| address-limiter-acts | measured | Not evaluated | On the address-limiter fixture (50 attacking addresses at 100/s each, a limiter keyed on address admitting 20/s), at least 90% of attack requests are refused, at 95% confidence over 59 runs. |
| address-limiter-false-positives-bounded | measured | Not evaluated | On the address-limiter fixture, legitimate users grouped onto addresses (up to 50 behind one shared address) see a false-positive refusal ratio of at most 0.001, at 95% confidence over 59 runs. |
| bulkhead-keeps-static-served-under-db-slowdown | measured | Not evaluated | On the hold-across fixture (16 API threads held while calling the database, a database slowdown of 10 times from 20 s to 40 s, static requests at 200/s), with database calls limited to 8 threads and a maximum wait, the p99 of static requests in the slowdown window is at most 50 ms, at 95% confidence over 59 runs. |
| metric-claims-calibrated-or-unvalidated | invariant | Not evaluated | A metric claim reads unvalidated when it names no calibration, when a calibration fails on its hold-out load runs (distinct from the fitting runs, with equal workload, capacities and window; the sign-test interval of the model median over at least 6 kept runs, with no truncated run, outside the measurement band widened over repeats), when its evidence has expired at the asOf date recorded in the result or is bound to another component revision, or when a calibration tolerance exceeds the margin computed from the beta-quantile of the per-run statistic; it is flagged extrapolated outside the convex hull of the tested points with the same fault set, decided in exact rational arithmetic; and its result lists first the assumed and to-be-measured parameters, and pairs of flagged parameters, whose variation over their declared ranges flips the verdict. |
| crash-instance-loses-state | invariant | Not evaluated | A crash-instance fault stops exactly the named instance at its time: its incarnation is incremented, its timers removed, its waiting requests removed with their timeouts, its in-service requests ended with their service-done entries and its units released, its restart drawn, its notify event broadcast; every targeted entry created for the old incarnation, a reply to a call it made before the crash included, is dropped as stale; a routed entry decided for it is ignored by that behavior only, and a broadcast skips it; instance aggregates exclude it while it is down; round-robin, random, any and least-busy routes skip it; a restart edge restores only its own incarnation; a crash of a missing or already down instance does nothing; and on restart the instance is in its initial state with its initial variables. |
| distribution-lowering-bit-exact | invariant | Supported by external evidence | simulationInput lowers distributions with integer arithmetic only: the exponential and normal quantile tables are produced by the integer algorithms of docs/simulation.md and equal the correctly rounded values at the 1,045 grid points, their canonical text hashing to SHA-256 a23cd2a9566c106701375d5cf75ef982b8db6f3e52c199c47e6239649c840950; exp_fp, ln_fp and the exponential, log-normal and empirical constructions reproduce every reference output bit for bit; and a lowered value of 2^53 base units or more, a sigma above 8 or a p99 above 10^6 times the median is refused. |
| poisson-arrivals-by-inversion | invariant | Not evaluated | Poisson arrivals, constant or ramped, take exactly one arrival draw per instant, computed by inverting the integrated rate with exact integer square roots and rounding to the nearest base unit; no valid input is refused or fails for rejected candidates, and a draw whose instant falls at or after its phase end or the horizon creates nothing. |
| overlapping-faults-follow-active-set | invariant | Not evaluated | With windowed faults that overlap on one resource, the value in effect at every fault-edge is the declared function of the active set: the last active set-capacity, set-class-limit or replace-demand in the order of the input's faults list, the product of the active scale-demand factors reduced to lowest terms, and collapsed while any collapse-classes is active; demand and scale are evaluated at the grant against the faults active then; when a collapse ends, waiting requests return to their own classes in (since, seq) order; and under a collapse each request keeps its class for maxWait and metrics. |
| broadcasts-evaluated-at-delivery | invariant | Not evaluated | A broadcast reaches the instances that are up when it is delivered (keyed instances created since its creation and instances restarted since included) and skips down ones without an ignored entry; targeted entries keep the instance and incarnation fixed when they were created; routed entries are decided, with their counters, draws and incarnation, when first taken as head, and a delayed copy keeps the decision; and the next arrival instant of a workload is created exactly once, even when a fault delays or drops the first entry of the current instant. |
| request-lifecycle-defines-outcomes | invariant | Not evaluated | Every request of a class follows the request lifecycle of docs/simulation.md: it starts when its start entry is first taken as head; it ends once, at the first delivered end event with its key (a deliver step that does not fail, whether or not a transition fires), as error at once when its start entry is dropped, crashed, stale or handled by no transition, or as timeout on a closed-client timeout; it is censored otherwise; only ok requests are finite samples; and every sample count, bucket power and derived metric is computed from these outcomes. |
| temporal-claims-checked-on-all-runs | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A never, precedes or eventually claim reported by exegra prove as holding has a Lean theorem, accepted by Lean, that the verified explorer found the form true on every complete run of its scenario (every order of simultaneous events, every subset of its faults, each run ending at quiescence, at the step limit or in a failed step); a reported counterexample is a real run that violates it, and a path the bound or the state budget cuts off is reported incomplete. |
| metric-claims-in-specifications | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A specification that declares resources, workloads, request classes and a format-2 scenario derives a version-2 engine input (times in base units, rates exact, distributions lowered), and a metric-bound claim over it evaluates to model-holds, violated or inconclusive with its run count and confidence, never verified or supported. |
| runtime-data-copies-fresh-and-related | structural | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | Every copy of Exegra's runtime data that this specification declares (session model and claim results, scene, simulation run, browser preferences and camera memory, command-line outputs, generated documents, bundles, proof outputs and the production bundle's embedded specifications) states a freshness bound, its holder is related through declared relationships to the owner of every record it copies, and no shared copy holds principal data. |
| session-starts-after-engine | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | At start-up the session is started only after the engine has loaded or failed to load. |
| open-takes-disk-hashes | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | After start-up the opened project is saved and its base hash is the hash of the file on disk, so the first write is checked against what was read. |
| read-only-build-never-writes | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | A production build never asks to write a project file, even after an edit. |
| reload-adopts-disk-text | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | After a conflict resolved by reloading, the session holds the text on disk, reports saved, and the other process's change was never overwritten. |
| overwrite-writes-session-text | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | After a conflict resolved by overwriting, the file on disk holds the session's text and the session reports saved. |
| external-change-is-adopted | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | A change made on disk by another process while nothing is pending is adopted: the session ends holding the text on disk with that text as its base hash. |
| adopted-change-never-written-back | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | Text adopted from disk is never written back by the session. |
| own-write-never-reloaded | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | The notice of the session's own write never makes it reload the project. |
| undo-saved-to-disk | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | After an edit and its undo, the model holds the original text, the file on disk holds it too and the session reports saved. |
| undo-restores-earlier-text | invariant | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | Whenever a step can be redone, the model holds the text from before the undone edit. |
| accepted-writes-journaled | invariant | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | In these runs every accepted write of spec.jsonc or layout.jsonc has its journal line. |
| layout-move-writes-layout-only | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | A dragged node is saved to layout.jsonc and spec.jsonc is not written. |
| claim-results-follow-commits | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | After a semantic commit the claim results are those of the committed model. |
| export-holds-saved-text | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | An export made after an edit was saved holds the session's text. |
| import-shows-imported-text | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | After an import into the open project's name, the session shows the imported text, which is what is on disk. |
| unparseable-bundle-never-sent | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | A bundle whose specification has syntax errors is never sent to the server. |
| generated-documents-match-model | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | Documents generated from the Realisation panel are derived from the session's current model. |
| real-runs-replay-through-self-model | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The events a running Exegra really produces (requests and answers of the persistence client with the development server's own events, change notices, engine-worker messages, commits, claim evaluations, simulator controls, preference and camera storage writes) are recorded with their requestId and a logical sequence number and replayed through the behaviors of the scenario live-session; every divergence from the self-model (an event the specification does not name, an event the model does not predict at that point, a predicted event no behavior accepts, a payload value that differs, a predicted event not seen within 200 recorded events or by the end of a closed stream) is reported as a failed check at its step and listed in the simulator panel's Live mode, and a real open, edit and save replays without one. |
| sim-cursor-stays-in-trace | invariant | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | The step shown in the Simulation panel is always a recorded step of the trace (or none before the first), however often the user presses step. |
| pulse-follows-step | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | The scene pulses only steps the session has shown. |
| only-latest-run-adopted | invariant | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | The Simulation panel only ever shows the trace of the latest load. |
| interaction-state-restored | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | After a reload the session shows the selection and the view the user left. |
| camera-restored-per-view | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | Coming back to a view restores the camera the user left it with. |
| cli-success-means-all-written | invariant | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | Whenever exegra apply reports success, both files on disk hold the texts the transaction produced. |
| cli-partial-write-reported | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | When the layout write of exegra apply conflicts, the specification has already been written and the failure says so. |
| verify-records-before-reporting | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | exegra verify prints its envelope only after the recorded results are on disk. |
| session-adopts-recorded-results | temporal | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | An open session adopts the results exegra verify records and re-evaluates its claims with them. |
| trace-file-only-from-a-run | invariant | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | exegra trace writes a trace file only for a run the engine completed. |
| prove-reports-only-lean-accepted | invariant | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | exegra prove reports a claim result only when Lean accepted its theorem. |
| markdown-written-only-when-covered | invariant | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | The Markdown importer writes a specification only when its coverage check passed. |
| conformance-never-hides-mismatch | invariant | Verified within the stated model and assumptions | The conformance runner reports agreement only when no golden trace differed. |
| runtime-stores-modelled | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Every durable store Exegra's code writes (the project files and journal of the workspace service, evidence/results.json, generated/, the localStorage keys exegra.session and exegra.layout, the sessionStorage keys exegra.camera.<project>.<view>, the bundle download, and the files the command line and the tools write) is named by a record or a copy of this specification. |
| verify-reuses-fresh-outcomes-only | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A verify run takes the outcome of a test evidence entry from a recorded test run only when that run was complete, the tree did not change while it ran, and its tree stamp equals the stamp of the tree verify runs on; a reference matching no recorded test is run, never assumed to pass. |
| verify-skip-keeps-recorded-outcome | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A verify run that skips a test evidence entry keeps the outcome recorded for that entry before, and records no outcome for it where none was recorded. |
| journal-failure-reported | invariant | Supported by external evidence | When a spec or layout write replaces its file but the journal line cannot be appended, the write result says the journal line was not written, the CLI apply result lists it in journalWarnings, and the session reports it as an error; the replaced file is not restored. |
| changes-panel-pairs-by-revision | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The changes panel pairs each history entry with the log entry of its own commit, matched by revision, so the pairing stays correct after the session log has dropped its oldest entries. |
| traffic-stream-bounded-and-logical | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The traffic recorder keeps at most 5000 events, numbers them with strictly increasing logical sequence numbers, copies each payload when it is recorded, and hands the replay only the sequence number, the event id and the payload: the wall-clock time it keeps for display never reaches the core. |
| traffic-producers-name-spec-events | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The producers record the events of this specification where its event descriptions say: the development server returns, for each request that carries a request id, its own events (workspace events and the answer) stamped with that id in the order it produced them, and infers an external edit (marked inferred) for a change notice whose hash it did not write; the engine runner records each message to and from the worker; texts are named by the same sha-256 the workspace computes. |
| evidence-refs-validated-before-verify | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A test evidence reference that exegra verify would refuse as unsafe is reported by validate as an error (evidence.ref-unsafe) with its evidence id and position, so an author learns of it before verify runs. Validate and verify use the same rule. |
| engine-run-terminates-proved | invariant | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | Every run of an engine input that the engine accepts stops: its outcome is never running, and it has at most stepBound(stepLimit) steps. |
| lean-engine-is-exegra-lean | structural | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | The engine the JavaScript shell calls (engine.run at the Lean call boundary) is the function the native exegra-lean runs: runAny is Json.parse followed by Exegra.Api.call "engine.run". |
| lean-registry-names-unique | structural | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | No two functions registered at the Lean call boundary share a name, so a name selects exactly one function. |
| lean-boundary-errors-are-values | structural | Supported by external evidence | Every call through the Lean call boundary returns a reply: an unknown function, a malformed request and a refusal come back as an error value, and the boundary lists its registered functions. |
| concordance-results-are-evidence | structural | Supported by external evidence | A linked project's Concordance results (concordanceResults in project.jsonc) become derived test evidence of their claims: a pass makes a requirement supported and never verified, a fail makes it violated, a claim without an entry stays not evaluated, and a results file for another tool or specification, or an entry for an unknown claim, changes no status. A results file that names a specSha256 (the sha-256 of the specification text it was computed from) is not applied when that differs from the current text; one that names none is applied with its revision recorded as unknown, and its explanation says so. |
| pages-site-published-from-main | structural | Inconclusive | Every push to main publishes the production build to the repository's GitHub Pages site, and only after the Lean core is built and Exegra's own specification validates; the build's asset URLs are relative, so the site loads under the repository's sub-path. |
| lean-theorem-method-refuses-unsound-proofs | structural | Supported by external evidence | A lean-theorem claim is proved only when Lean reports a kernel-checked theorem that depends on no axiom beyond propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound and whose statement mentions every named implementation constant; sorry, native_decide, an axiom of its own and an off-topic statement are refused. |
| core-logic-in-lean | structural | Violated | Every implemented component of the verified kernel (tag verdict-kernel: the code that decides whether a claim holds, namely model derivation, the semantic hash, engine-input derivation, the engine, the explorer and temporal checker, claim verdicts and their helpers, and transactions) is implemented in Lean, so the claims about the kernel are theorems about the code that runs. Everything else (layout, projections, scene, generators, UI, live replay, request-log rendering, command-line arguments, workspace I/O, the JSONC text layer) is JavaScript outside the verdict path. |
| lean-core-runs-in-browser | structural | Supported by external evidence | The Lean core, compiled to WebAssembly (wasm64), runs in the application page and reproduces golden traces exactly. |
| engine-run-refines-definition | invariant | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | The engine the application runs (Exegra.Impl.runAnyValue, registered as engine.run and run by exegra-lean) returns exactly what the definition of the semantics (Exegra.runAnyValue) returns, on every parsed engine input of format 1 and format 2. |
| engine-call-computes-definition | structural | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | A call of engine.run at the Lean call boundary computes the definition of the semantics, Exegra.runAnyValue, on its parsed arguments. |
| fast-json-writer-is-json-write | invariant | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | The in-place JSON writer the engine writes traces with (Exegra.Impl.JsonOut.write') gives the text of the definition Exegra.Json.write for every value. |
| register-import-holds-every-line | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The register importer writes nothing unless the Markdown file rebuilds line for line from the generated sections with every entity marker expanded to the lines it came from, and every imported table row rendered from its properties gives the cells of its source row. |
| register-import-entities-cite-source | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Every document, section, table and row entity the register importer writes carries a source property naming the file and line it was imported from, and every row whose rule has an identifier column carries that identifier as written in ref. |
| register-import-drafts-are-marked | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Every entity the register importer takes from a draft fragment carries origin "draft" and a source in that fragment, the system notes name the fragment, and the Markdown rendered from the sections is the same with or without drafts. |
| register-import-respects-authority | invariant | Supported by external evidence | When the import map says the JSONC is the authority, the register importer writes nothing to the project's specification file, and --render produces the Markdown from the specification file alone. |
| realisation-roots-traced | invariant | Supported by external evidence | When project.jsonc declares realisation roots, traceability checks each module written <alias>:<path> against that root's directory, reports a module naming an undeclared alias as missing, and reports unclaimed files only for roots that declare include globs (minus exclude globs); with no roots declared, the traceability result is the same as without the feature. |
| external-not-run-distinct-from-fail | invariant | Supported by external evidence | External test evidence is recorded as fail only when a matched JUnit test case reports a failure whose text matches none of the root's environmental patterns; a test absent from the report, skipped, reported as an error, or failing for an environmental reason is recorded as not-run, which `verify` never counts as a failure and the claim evaluator treats as having no result. |
| integrity-rules-evaluated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Integrity rules are part of the specification and are evaluated on every model: each holds with the number of instances checked, or is violated with its counterexamples (bindings, entity ids, lines); an invalid rule is a validation error, a rule over nothing is reported vacuous, and every rule of the fixture can be made to fail by a stated mutation. |
| integrity-ratchet-on-apply | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A transaction that adds a violation of an integrity rule of severity error is refused with the counterexamples and changes nothing; violations the project already had do not block unrelated edits, a fix is accepted, and violations of warning rules are only reported. |
| integrity-proved-in-lean | invariant | Supported by external evidence | exegra prove-integrity establishes a rule that holds by a kernel-checked proof by reflection (eval_iff, decide +kernel) and a violated rule by a kernel-checked refutation from each concrete witness (refutes_sound), with Lean's standard axioms only, and reports a rule incomplete when the Lean checker and the JavaScript evaluator disagree. |
| reader-pages-come-from-model | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A reader page is derived from the model and its view alone: every row, count and section it shows is an entity of the model selected by the view's kind and conditions, grouped, classified and faceted by the view's rules, and a view without blocks reads the system, an index of its kinds and its first document. |
| reader-blocks-validated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A reader block carrying a field the meta-model does not define is refused with an error, so a page cannot silently ignore part of its definition. |
| reader-links-select-through-session | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The reader surface replaces the scene only while the current view uses the reader projection, a link on it selects its entity through the session, and the page follows a committed edit. |
| semantic-hash-ignores-presentation | invariant | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | The semantic hash of a model depends only on its schema version, its vocabulary and its top-level declarations (id, kind, data) up to order: source positions, JSON paths, nested declarations' records, references, expressions, the effective meta-model and the order of declarations do not change it. |
| semantic-hash-ignores-key-order | invariant | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | Reordering the members of objects, at every depth, in the vocabulary and in the data of every declaration, does not change the semantic hash (for values with distinct keys in every object). |
| semantic-hash-runs-definition | invariant | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | The semantic hash that runs (Exegra.Hash.semanticHash': keys converted once for sorting, the in-place JSON writer) equals the definition Exegra.Hash.semanticHash on every input. |
| semantic-hash-call-computes-definition | structural | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | A call of hash.semantic at the Lean call boundary returns the definition of the semantic hash of the declarations it is given. |
| model-references-resolve | invariant | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | In a derived model whose diagnostics contain no reference diagnostic (ref.unresolved, ref.kind), every reference names an entity of the model whose kind the reference allows. |
| model-derivation-total | structural | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | Model derivation at the Lean call boundary (model.derive) answers every argument value with a result, never with an error, and always returns. |
| model-derivation-matches-javascript | structural | Supported by external evidence | The Lean model derivation and semantic hash give the diagnostics, model and hash of the JavaScript parser they replaced on every specification the tests read (specs/exegra, specs/benchmark, tests/fixtures) and on every specification text the core tests parse, the invalid ones included. |
| app-and-cli-run-lean-engine-only | invariant | Not evaluated | The application and the command line run scenarios on the Lean engine only: no module of src/app, src/ui or cli/ loads the Rust engine or runs a scenario any other way. |
| ui-every-function-on-phone | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Every function the interface offers on a desktop (switching project and view, search, selecting and inspecting, editing, creating, connecting, deleting, undo and redo, simulation, verification, source, realisation, diagnostics, export and import) is reachable by touch at a 390 by 844 viewport. |
| implementation-machine-readiness-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A behavior declared with level "implementation" is reported with an error for each rule it breaks, and for none when it breaks none: every variable has a resolving repr; instances are {key, storedIn} with key a key field of that record; the machine says which record holds it (storage {record, key} with key a key field of the record, or instances.storedIn, and the two agree when both are present); every variable is held in a field of that record (storage.fields, default the field of its name) whose type fits its repr, every storage.fields entry names a variable and a field of the record, and no field holds two things; a stored state has a required string or enum field (storage.state, default "state") whose values, through storage.stateValues when given, distinctly cover every state; a machine with storage.creates true fills every required field of its record, while a non-creating machine is not held to this; every event field read is a declared payload field and every payload type resolves; every emit names its receiver and its event a delivery; a transaction is declared; no transition is faultOnly; every elapsed event is emitted by a timer; transitions with the same state and event are explicitly ordered (guard or otherwise on the last, otherwise only on the last); every non-terminal state has an outcome or an ignore for every event of the machine. The rules are the ready.* codes of docs/semantics.md; a machine is keyed by the whole key of its record, every key field in order, in storage.key and in instances.key (storage.key-whole, WG-4907). |
| implementation-properties-declarative | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The implementation-level properties (level, instances.storedIn, delivery, elapsed, an emit effect's to, transaction, otherwise, ignores) never change simulation: the engine input derived by simulationInput does not contain them and a specification that uses them parses and validates like any other. The one exception is the declared types of an implementation-level behavior (variable repr, payload field types), which reach the engine input only as the behavior's lowered types, never as repr. |
| codegen-consistency-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Type entities, record constraints and requirement realisation are checked as errors whether or not the specification sets system.generation: an id or scalar type has a built-in representation, an enum has values, struct fields resolve, set-null needs optional, onDelete needs refersTo, unique and index fields are declared, changes list versions 1 to schemaVersion without gaps, and a construction requirement is named in the models property of some claim. |
| generation-readiness-gated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The readiness rules (unresolved field, contract and event types, a reference without onDelete, a record without schemaVersion, a contract without realisation, a hole without an H- ref, implementedBy or postcondition, a requirement without realisation) are validation errors when system.generation is set; without it validate reports none of them and the readiness command lists them, exiting 0 either way. |
| refines-checks-machine-refinement | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A refines claim is verified only when, in the traces of the scenarios that run the implementation behavior, every initial configuration and every fired transition of it maps through its refinement mapping to the step its model machine takes or to a stutter, and at least one step matched a model transition; a step to the wrong model state, a step whose model guard is false, a wrong mapped post-variable, a moving step mapped to a stutter, different emitted events and a wrong initial state each make it violated, with the failing step as the last step of the counterexample trace. Timers the implementation sets are compared with the model's timers when the model transition sets some and are internal to the implementation otherwise; a model guard or effect that reads another behavior reads it from the same trace, through the machine that refines that behavior when it does not run itself. With refinement.timeScale, the model reads the trace time divided by it and its timer delays are compared multiplied by it. A delayed emit of the model is realised only by a delayed emit of the implementation for the same event and the payload fields the model sets, delivered after the model delay times the time scale; a timer never realises an emit, and an undelayed emit for a delayed one, or the reverse, is violated (reason emits). |
| refinement-mappings-validated | structural | Supported by external evidence | Validation refuses a refines that names a non-behavior, a refines on a model-level behavior, a refinement of a behavior that itself refines, and a refinement mapping that names an unknown state, transition or variable of either side or leaves an implementation state or transition or a model variable unmapped. |
| construct-consistency-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The cross-cutting constructs are checked as errors whether or not the specification sets system.generation: every policy, quota, retry, audit and validation names exactly one target; a validation names a declared input or field, carries exactly the parameters of its rule, fits the class of its target, and has a valid pattern and a non-empty range; a retention states exactly one of keepFor and until, a duration, fields only and always when it anonymises, declared fields, archiveTo only when it archives, and at most one per record; a quota has a positive limit, a duration window, and a declared contract error exactly when it refuses calls of a contract; a retry has positive attempts, durations, a cap only on an exponential backoff and not below the base, and a declared idempotency key; an audit captures declared fields once each, a chain field of bytes or string exactly when chained by hash; every condition, custom rule and capture parses, reads only what is in scope and types against the declared fields; and only a requirement has realisedBy. |
| construct-readiness-gated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The readiness rules of the cross-cutting constructs (a contract governed by policies without policyDefault, a policy on a transition of a model-level machine, a maxLength validation without a unit, a record with personal or credential fields and no retention, an archiving retention without archiveTo, a quota without heldBy, a retry of an event or of a non-idempotent contract without an idempotency key, a retry without a timeout, an audit that leaves a required field of its record unfilled) are validation errors when system.generation is set; without it validate reports none of them and readinessGaps lists each. |
| construction-requirement-realised-by-constructs | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A requirement realised by construction is satisfied by a claim whose models names it or by at least one construct in its realisedBy; with neither, validation reports requirement.construction-unmodelled. |
| retention-erases-records | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A retention construct that deletes a record, or anonymises every personal and credential field of it, satisfies the record-erasure-point data rule for that record; one that anonymises only some of those fields does not. |
| ui-consistency-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The user interface constructs are checked as errors whether or not the specification sets system.generation: a screen route is "/" or literal and :param segments with no parameter twice, no two screens answer the same route, auth is none, session or role:<r>, and every required input of the view-model contract is a route or query parameter; a form field fills a declared input of its contract with a control that fits its class, no input is filled twice, every required input is filled by a field or an argument, and only a select has options; an action gives an argument for every required input of its contract and none for an undeclared one; arguments and navigation parameters parse, read only route parameters, view-model outputs, the principal and (for navigation) the response, and are of the right class; a navigation gives exactly the target route parameters; accessible names are non-empty; a live binding refreshes the whole view model or a path into it, once per screen and event; and every journey step has exactly one known key with nothing else in its value (no CSS selector), names existing screens, forms, fields, events and browsers, gives every route parameter on goto, selects by a WAI-ARIA role, expects an HTTP status from 100 to 599 and scans logs with regular expressions, and a journey verifies requirements only. |
| ui-readiness-gated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The readiness rules of the user interface (a screen without a view-model contract or a renderer, a form field without a label, an action without onSuccess or onError) are validation errors when system.generation is set; without it validate reports none of them and readinessGaps lists each. A contract a form or action uses that policies govern without policyDefault is the construct rule ready.policy-default-missing, reported once. |
| client-machines-held-to-machine-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | An implementation-level behavior whose subject is a component of category client is accepted and held to the same machine readiness rules as any implementation-level behavior. |
| ui-journey-steps-checked | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Every journey step addresses elements by WAI-ARIA role and an exact or patterned accessible name, with a heading level only on a heading and within only another such element, never a CSS selector; every pattern compiles; every attribute read is aria-*, data-*, value, href, lang or dir (data-*, lang or dir on the document element); every ${name} is captured by an earlier step and every name is captured once; a no-reload check names a marker, and a request check a log, set earlier in the same browser; hosts, paths, JSON reply paths, statuses and timeouts are well formed; an API call made as a browser names one of the journey; a gotoPath may add a fragment of letters, digits and = & _ - . ~ % (a captured value may stand in it) and a path carries none inline; expectUrl takes a pattern that compiles; captureMail names the address it reads, a pattern that compiles and a browser of the journey, and binds a name captured once; and clickAction gives a per-row action the row fields its label and confirmation read, and no row to any other action. |
| ui-per-row-actions | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A per-row action repeats over a path into its screen's view model that reaches a list of a declared struct; its arguments and navigation parameters read item.<field> typed by that struct, and its label and confirmation fill in only ${item.<field>} placeholders naming fields of the rows; an action that is not per-row has no item placeholders. |
| ui-client-constructs-third-set | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A select or radio field takes options from a list in its screen's view model only through a path that reaches a list, naming struct fields of the control's class for value and a string for label; at most one screen is the catch-all and nothing navigates or goes to it; navigation addresses and parameters, toasts and derivations read only names in scope (query.<param> of the screen, form.<field> on a form, response.<output>, local.<name>), with toast placeholders that can be shown as text; a live binding's condition is a boolean over the event payload and the view model, or a membership of a value of the items' class in a view-model list, or any or all of such conditions; derived fields do not read themselves or derive in a cycle; and an action that keeps its result places an output of its contract in a declared screen-local value of the same type and does not navigate. |
| run-time-timer-delays | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A timer delay read at run time, {expr, simulate}, appears only on an implementation-level machine, its expr is an expression of a number over the machine's variables, time and the event payload, and it states the delay a simulated run uses; simulation and refines run the timer with that simulated delay, so refines compares the timer's event and payload and a model's numeric delay against the simulated one. |
| wire-naming-and-json | invariant | Supported by external evidence | system.generation.wireCase and a contract's binding.case accept exactly snake_case, kebab-case and camelCase, any other value being property.enum at its position; json is a built-in type that resolves wherever a type is named but is never the representation of an id or scalar (type.repr-json); in expressions a json value is its own class, compared only with == and != against json or null, refused by ordering, arithmetic, concatenation and boolean operators, by toasts and form controls, and no path reaches into it. |
| ui-client-constructs-fourth-set | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A contract input is encoded only as its encode allows: trim, split and as read a text input, split names a separator and is not combined with as, and empty is given only for an optional input. A form field takes an initial value only from a path reaching a value its control edits, or from the first item of a list of structs whose field equals an expression of the field’s class, and an always-derived field takes none; normalize is only on a text, textarea or password control. A form’s second call reads the first call’s response, and the outcome reads the outputs of both calls. onSuccess.view names outputs of the view model of the screen the success opens, each of its output’s class. onError prefix and announce, when given, are not empty; confirmAnnounce belongs to an action with a confirm and is not empty; alsoOn offers an action on the screens of one authentication only and reads nothing of one screen. An error message is well formed: its placeholders fit its kind, the kinds it words are errors some contract of its forms and actions lists, no two messages word the same thing for the same forms and actions, and a specification with a form or an action has a default message for refusals (a readiness gap when generation is set). |
| telemetry-logging-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | system.telemetry declares services with distinct ids and relative log paths (telemetry.service); an event's logging is {logged: true, service, level, name?, tenant?, fields} or {logged: false, reason} (event.logging-shape), names a declared service (event.logging-service) and a dotted lower-case log name unique within the service (event.logging-name), and logs only payload fields that exist (event.logging-field-unknown), are scalars (event.logging-field-type), are neither marked sensitive nor of a type that is sensitive through structs, lists and options (event.logging-field-sensitive), and are written under lower-case names that are not ts, level, service, event, correlation or tenant and not twice (event.logging-field-reserved); with system.generation every domain event states its logging (ready.event-logging-missing); an expectLog step names a logged event and its service (uiScenario.log-event) and expects only the line's tenant and allowlisted members (uiScenario.log-field); and a logged domain event is not reported as an unused event, since the running system emits it. |
| scenario-input-typed | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Every value a scenario feeds a machine has the type its event declares: a step payload and the payload of an inject-event fault are checked field by field (uuid format, integer bounds per type, enum values, required fields present, no undeclared fields, option fields absent or null), and a finding scenario.input-type is an error when system.generation is set and a warning otherwise. |
| expression-semantics-shared | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The JavaScript evaluator (src/core/expr.js) and the Lean core (Exegra.eval, called as expr.eval) give every outcome of the shared case table tests/fixtures/expr-cases.json: checked integer arithmetic within u8, u16, u32, i32 and u64 and i64 limited to +-(2^53 - 1), integer division truncating toward zero, remainder with the sign of the dividend, division by zero an error, structural equality of lists, structs and json with 1 equal to 1.0, an absent option read as null and any other absent value an error, and signed time differences. Strata runs a byte-identical copy of the table on its generated Rust and JavaScript. |
| machine-time-integer-readiness | invariant | Supported by external evidence | An implementation-level machine is reported with ready.time-epoch when it compares time with a variable that can still hold its literal initial value, ready.time-difference when it reads time - x without first establishing x <= time, ready.delay-unit when its scenarios disagree on a time unit, a fixed delay is not a whole number of milliseconds or it reads time in another unit than ms, and ready.number-bound when a literal, an integer initial value or a scenario payload value is outside its integer range; and with none of these when it breaks none. |
| union-map-types-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A type may be a tagged union (variants that are unit, struct-like or newtype, written in serde's external, internal or adjacent form, with the variant names in their own case) and a type text may be map<K, V>; validation refuses a union without variants or with a variant twice (type.union-variants), a variant field that does not resolve (type.union-field), an encoding whose tagField or contentField does not fit its tag or its variants (type.encoding), a map key that is not a string, an integer or an id or scalar over one (type.map-key), a struct that contains itself through struct fields alone (type.recursion) while allowing a cycle through a union variant, list, option or map, and a constraint (pattern, minLength, maxLength) on a type that does not take it or with an invalid regular expression (type.constraints); a union and a map value are opaque (class json) in guard expressions, and readiness (ready.*-undeclared) covers map types. |
| language-construct-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A specification may state an expression language its users write (section languages, entity language): an AST union and a value union, shapes, scopes (record-scope with system attributes, param, constant, role-test) and operators, each with its syntax (infix, keyword-infix, prefix-word, call, literal or scope form, token, precedence, associativity), operand and result shapes, null handling and one primitive of a fixed catalogue whose semantics are pinned to the Strata kernel (crates/kernel/src/expr.rs); validation refuses two operators of one name (language.operator-duplicate), a word or symbol claimed twice (language.token-clash), a token of the wrong lexical class or a form the primitive is not written in (language.syntax), a missing or misplaced precedence or two associativities at one level (language.precedence), a primitive outside the catalogue (language.primitive-unknown), operands that disagree with the primitive arity or the AST variant (language.arity), an undeclared shape (language.shape-unknown), null handling other than the primitive's (language.nulls), an operator for a variant or case the AST does not have or a second operator for one (language.variant), and a scope that is malformed, missing for an operator or read by none (language.scope); with system.generation set an AST variant or case without an operator and an operator without a primitive are errors (ready.language-variant-uncovered, ready.language-primitive-missing). |
| function-construct-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A specification may state pure functions (section functions, entity function): typed params, a returns type, an optional errors union, lets and a body in the function body language, which is the guard language extended with struct field access, if, let, match over unions, options, results and enums, struct and union constructors, list literals, the operations map, filter, find, any, all, fold, sortBy, take, concat, keys, values, get, insert, remove and format, eval and check of a declared language, call of another function, fail and try; validation refuses a body or let that does not parse (function.expr-syntax), a name, field, variant, type, function, language or operation that does not exist (function.expr-reference), an expression that is ill typed or a body that does not fit the return type (function.expr-type), a match that misses a case without _, has a case twice or an arm after _ (function.match-exhaustive), a call cycle through two or more functions, or a call of a function of itself on anything but a strict sub-value of a union parameter at one position shared by all its calls of itself (function.recursion), errors that are not a union or a fail or try outside that union (function.error-unknown), malformed or duplicate parameter and let names and parameter or return types that do not resolve (function.param), and an example that is malformed or does not evaluate to its result or error in Exegra's JavaScript evaluator (function.example-failed); with system.generation set a function without a success example or without an example of an error variant it can fail with is an error (ready.function-example-missing). |
| set-tuple-nested-types-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A type text may be set<T>, a JSON array of distinct items in order, which resolves, keys maps and closes recursion as list<T> does and has the class list in guard expressions; a scenario value of a set type that repeats an item does not have the type; a union variant may be a tuple variant (tuple: true, two or more positional fields written as a JSON array in order, as serde writes {"Cmp": ["Eq", a, b]}); generic type texts nest (map<K, map<K2, V>>, list<map<K, V>>). Validation refuses a set of an unresolved type (type.field-unresolved, type.union-field), a map keyed by a set or a bad key inside a set or another map (type.map-key), a tuple variant with fewer than two fields, also marked newtype, or with an optional field (type.union-variants), and a tuple variant under an internal tag (type.encoding). A shared<T> is a sharing hint, identical to T on the wire: it resolves, keys maps, closes recursion, has the class and holds the values of T, and validation refuses a shared of an unresolved type (type.field-unresolved) and a struct that holds itself through a shared field (type.recursion). |
| contract-errors-on-the-wire | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A contract error states its HTTP status as status (an integer from 400 to 599, contract.error-status) or as kind (a key of system.generation.errorKinds, contract.error-kind-unknown), never both (contract.error-status-kind), and otherwise takes the default system.generation.errorKind or errorStatus; errorKinds map to statuses from 400 to 599 (generation.error-kinds) and the defaults are well formed and exclusive (generation.error-status); system.generation.errorBody names a struct type whose every field but code, message and correlation is optional (generation.error-body-type), a code field of an enum holding every kind and string message and correlation fields (generation.error-body-field, generation.error-body-code), needs errorKinds (generation.error-body-kinds) and maps exactly the six runtime failures to declared kinds (generation.error-body-runtime); when generation is set an error of a served contract with no status is ready.error-status-missing, and under an error body one with no kind is ready.error-kind-missing. |
| machine-realisation-and-durability | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A machine and a contract say whether they model code that exists or the enterprise target, and a machine says where it lives: behavior.realisation (contract.deployment for a contract) is shipped or target, defaulting to target when the subject (implementedBy) component is not-implemented and to shipped otherwise, and an explicit shipped on a not-implemented component is an error (behavior.realisation-subject, contract.deployment-subject); storage.durability is record (default) or memory, a memory machine names no record, key, fields, state, creates, instances.storedIn or transaction and is held to no storage or transaction readiness rule, and an invalid combination is the error storage.durability. |
| tenant-data-fixed-rule | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A record states whether it is a platform table (scope platform, its fields its whole shape) or a platform table carrying tenant-defined data (scope tenant-meta, described by the type with metamodel tenant-schema), and may be marked realisation target; once a type has metamodel tenant-schema, validation refuses a record without a scope (record.scope-missing), a record that is not tenant-meta yet names a tenant object type or carries tenant field values, through a field of an object-id or values type or a map keyed by a field-id type (record.tenant-data-fixed), and a tenant-meta record without describedBy naming the tenant-schema type, or a describedBy on any other record (record.described-by); a specification without a tenant schema is unchanged. |
| faults-realisable-by-runtime | invariant | Supported by external evidence | In a scenario that runs an implementation-level machine, a fault the generated runtime cannot produce is reported with fault.unrealisable: any event fault on an event a timer of the scenario fires (or an elapsed event); drop-event, lose-event or a delay-event by other than a sum of retry backoff waits on an at-least-once event; delay-event, retry-event or duplicate-event on an at-most-once event; lose-event or retry-event on an event that declares no delivery; enable-transition or disable-transition on a transition of an implementation-level machine. A scenario whose faults the runtime can all produce has none. |
| machine-initial-state-reachable | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A shipped implementation-level machine (its subject states an implementation status other than not-implemented and is not a client) that a scenario runs, and that neither creates its records (storage.creates) nor names a contract that writes them (storage.createdBy), is reported with machine.initial-unreachable; a machine that does either, or of a component not built yet, is not. |
| runtime-fault-kinds-simulated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The fault kinds duplicate-event, lose-event and retry-event derive a version-2 engine input; the engine delivers an entry a duplicate-event matches and queues a copy of it by later (default 0), recorded duplicated; drops an entry a lose-event matches, recorded lost; and delays an entry a retry-event matches by the sum of the retry backoff waits after its attempts, recorded retried. |
| route-ambiguous-rule | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Two screens whose routes tie are refused with route.ambiguous: both routes are non-catch-all, have the same number of segments, agree at every position (equal literals, or a parameter on either side), and have the same number of literal segments. Screens whose routes do not tie that way are not refused. |
| contract-exposure-and-auth | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A contract states its exposure (public, internal or local) and its auth as {scheme, ...} with scheme anonymous, session, service, provisioning-token, signature, signed-address, delegation or in-process; with system.generation set a missing or prose auth is contract.auth-structured and a missing exposure contract.exposure, and always an object auth has only the members of its scheme (contract.auth-structured) and in-process is exactly the scheme of local contracts (contract.exposure); a local contract has no HTTP binding and an internal one none under the gateway prefix (exposure.gateway); a public contract not marked readOnly needs a session or visitor with csrf and origin or a verified credential (auth.public-write); an anonymous public contract is readOnly or justified and limited by a quota, an anonymous internal one readOnly and justified (auth.anonymous); the service scheme is never public (auth.service-public); every named policy governs the contract (auth.policy-unknown); a development contract names a declared condition, is never anonymous and when public names roles (auth.dev-only); and a public contract states a tenantScope other than platform (contract.tenant-scope). |
| derivation-construct-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A contract may be derived (realisation derived, with derivation {kind: pure, function} \| {kind: read, needs, load, decide, and optionally the need loop insufficient and needRounds together} \| {kind: command, needs, load, decide, commit, conflict, insufficient, needRounds, retries}, and optionally fault) or a facade (realisation facade, with a bffRoute); validation refuses a derivation on a contract that is not derived, a derived contract without one, an unknown kind, a field of another kind or a read naming only one of insufficient and needRounds (derivation.kind), a function whose parameters are not inputs (or load outputs) of the same name and type, whose result is not the outputs, whose error variants are not errors of the contract, an insufficient variant that is not a variant of decide carrying one need, or a fault that is not an error without payload (derivation.signature), a load contract that is a facade or itself, whose required inputs nothing fills, that takes the needs not exactly once, whose outputs are named like inputs or whose errors are not errors of the contract (derivation.load), the same for the commit contract with what decide gives, a conflict that is not a commit error, and an output that is neither a commit output nor a field of what decide gives (derivation.commit), need rounds (of a command or a read with a need loop) or retries missing or not non-negative integers (derivation.bounds), and a facade with implementedBy or whose bffRoute method, path and shape not exactly one served contract states (contract.facade-route); with system.generation set a derivation naming an undeclared function is an error (ready.derived-function-missing). |
