| function-language-kernel-fidelity | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The function body language states what the Strata kernel computes without stand-ins: an AST field typed string or an id or scalar over string (has_role's role-id) is read as a name, so has_role evaluates and checks; a variant, enum value or field named like a keyword (value.null, expr.if, then, else) is written after a dot, as a match pattern and before a colon, and a keyword-named field is never bound by shorthand; the built-in unit type has the one value () (null in examples), and unit is not the representation of an id or scalar and cannot be declared again; print(L, e) writes an expression as the kernel's Display writes it; format's {:?} writes a value as Rust's derived Debug of the type specc generates and {:json} as serde_json's compact text (strings escaped, f64 as ryu writes it); startsWith, endsWith, length, trim, chars, snakeCase, parseTime and the ASCII character classes behave as the kernel's Rust (str::starts_with, chars().count(), str::trim over White_Space, transition.rs snake_case, plain.rs parse_time, char::is_ascii_*); a function calls itself only on a strict sub-value of a union parameter; and the language evaluator and checker give the kernel's results and exact error texts on the shared case table. |
| capability-model-closed | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Capability, plan, role, grant, delegation, tokenScope and featureFlag are entity kinds, and a contract auth names the capabilities it requires in requires; every capability a contract, plan, role, grant, delegation, token scope or flag names is a capability (capability.unknown); with system.generation set every contract that is not local requires at least one (capability.required); every capability is conferred by some role or grant and is core or entitled by some plan (capability.unreachable); a delegation passes on only capabilities its source (role, token scope, grant or delegation) holds and a grant confers only capabilities its issuer role holds (delegation.exceeds); and a grant or delegation maxDuration is a positive duration (capability.duration). |
| example-fixture-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A specification may declare example fixtures (section exampleFixtures, entity exampleFixture: id, type, value, description) and a function example's argument, result or error may be {fixture: id} or {fixture: id, with: {dotted path: value}} instead of the value; Exegra resolves the reference, and the overrides in order, before it evaluates the example, so the example is checked exactly as if the value were written out. Validation refuses a reference to a fixture that is not declared (example.fixture-unknown); a fixture whose type does not resolve or whose value is not of its type, and a fixture that, with its overrides, is not the type of the parameter, result or error it stands for (example.fixture-type); and an override path that does not lead into the fixture's value through struct fields, map keys, list indexes and union variant names, an empty step, or a with that is not an object (example.fixture-override). |
| machine-transition-coverage | invariant | Supported by external evidence | For every implementation-level machine whose realisation is shipped, exegra cover-machines reports, per machine, the transitions and guard outcomes (true and false of each guard) covered out of the total, read from the traces of the scenarios that run it; for each item they leave out it searches bounded sequences of the machine's input events with representative payloads and waits, keeps the runs that reach it as generated scenarios cover-<machine>-<n> whose traces exercise it, and writes them with --write as CreateEntity operations tagged generated-coverage that apply accepts and that, once applied, cover the same items; a transition reached by no scenario and no searched sequence is machine.transition-unreachable, a warning, or an error under system.generation. |
| function-branch-coverage | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Exegra records which branches of each function its examples take (both branches of if, each arm of match, the value and the error path of try where the called function can fail, and fail), without changing what the examples evaluate to; exegra cover-functions reports per function the branches covered out of the total and each uncovered branch with its place in the body or let; an uncovered branch is reported and raises no readiness gap, because a rule that asks for one example per branch rewards examples that restate the function rather than its requirement; system.generation.functionBranches is still accepted and changes nothing. |
| store-export-is-byte-identical | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Exporting a specification imported into its store gives the imported text byte for byte, and its rows cover every code unit of it exactly once: for Exegra's specification, every linked project's specification, every JSONC fixture and every parse corpus case that parses, and for CRLF line endings, a byte order mark, tabs, trailing commas, comments inside and between entities, empty sections, a missing final newline and non-ASCII text. |
| store-reimport-gives-same-rows | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Importing the export of a store gives a store whose rows (entity, trivia, ref and meta) equal the original store's. |
| store-model-equals-text-model | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The model read from a store has the semantic hash, diagnostics, error count and entities of the model of its exported text, and the semantic hash recorded at import is that hash. |
| store-refuses-what-it-cannot-return | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A text that does not parse, is not well-formed Unicode or is not valid UTF-8 is refused by import with a StoreError naming the problem, and the store keeps the rows it held. |
| store-export-never-overwrites-stale-specification | invariant | Supported by external evidence | exegra store export replaces the specification only when the file is still the text the store last synchronised with, or is already the exported text; otherwise it fails with store.stale and leaves the file unchanged. |
| product-layers-checked | structural | Supported by external evidence | A project's product files (project.jsonc "products") are validated together with the platform specification: a product that imports the platform and declares only the allowed sections passes, and each of these is refused with its own named rule, whose message names the offending id or section and says how to fix it: an imported id the platform does not declare (layer.import-unknown), a redefinition of a platform entity or of another product's (layer.redefines-entity), a section a product may not declare (layer.kind-not-allowed), a platform entity used without being imported (layer.import-missing), a reference nothing declares (layer.reference-unknown), a missing header (layer.header-missing), an import of another platform file (layer.import-mismatch) a reference into another product (layer.cross-product-reference), two product files with one product id (layer.product-id-duplicate), an entity id that fails the id pattern or is missing (layer.entity-id-invalid, layer.entity-id-missing) and a platform entity that names a product entity (layer.platform-references-product). A platform entity named under any reference key of the meta-model requires an import. Transactions edit only the platform text and refuse a product's entities (layer.product-entity). |
| product-layers-leave-platform-unchanged | structural | Supported by external evidence | Adding product files to a project changes neither the platform's parsed model (every platform entity keeps its data, path and location) nor its semantic hash: validate reports the hash of the platform text alone as semanticHash, and the combined model's hash as layeredHash. |
| product-layers-no-platform-power | structural | Supported by external evidence | A product file cannot claim what only the platform may have: a role at platform level or with anonymous, service or external holders, a contract that is public with the anonymous scheme, is implemented by a platform component or carries a realisation of generated, hand or derived, a behavior at implementation level, an event that is a domain event, an elapsed-time event or states a delivery guarantee, a capability that is core and a setting that is set at or locked by the platform level are each refused with layer.platform-power, naming the entity and the fix. specc refuses the same product files with the same rule codes. |
| browser-reloads-product-files | structural | Supported by external evidence | When a product file of the open project changes, is added or is removed on disk (or the products list of project.jsonc changes), the application's session re-derives its model from the product layers on disk: the workspace watcher reports the change as the file products, the session reads the project again, and the platform text and layout in memory are kept. A product file that does not parse keeps the last valid model. |
| cli-registers-projects-from-scripts | structural | Supported by external evidence | A script can register a spec file (and product files) as a project in one command, and can run read-only commands on one without registering it: project add writes specs/<name>/project.jsonc with paths relative to the project directory, refuses an existing project unless --force, a name that is not a valid project name, a project that keeps its own spec.jsonc and files that cannot be read (writing nothing); project remove deletes the link; validate and the other read-only commands take --spec and --product inline and write nothing. |
| product-layers-typed-sections | structural | Supported by external evidence | A product file may declare the typed sections objects, policies, queries, views, workflows, segments, settings, ledgerUnits, ledgerPools, ledgerLimits, mailTemplates, noticeTemplates, tokens, triggers, automations and operations, each value of which is checked against the platform type its section maps to (object-type-def, policy-def, query-def, view-def, workflow-def, segment-decl, setting-def, ledger-unit-decl, ledger-pool-decl, ledger-limit, mail-template-decl, notice-template-decl, token-decl, trigger-def, automation-def, tenant-operation-def): a value that does not have the shape of its type is refused with layer.value-invalid, a union value the platform union lacks with layer.outside-catalogue, and a name in the product namespace that no product entity declares with layer.reference-unknown or that another product declares with layer.cross-product-reference. The typed sections are not entities of the combined model and do not change the platform semantic hash. specc reads the same table and gives the same rule codes. |
| product-layers-namespace | structural | Supported by external evidence | Every entity id of a product file begins with the namespace of its product, the product id with each hyphen replaced by an underscore followed by two underscores, and a local name; an id outside it is refused with layer.namespace-missing. A capability a product declares is confined to that product: another product file that names it is refused with layer.cross-product-reference, and a product capability is reported unreachable only when no role or grant confers it. |
| product-layers-expressions | structural | Supported by external evidence | An expression written as text in a typed section of a product file (a policy condition, a trigger condition, an operation's check, a field default or read rule, a constraint, a segment predicate, an automation's expression) is parsed and checked with the tenant rule language in the scope it is written in: a text that does not parse, or uses a name or call the language lacks, is refused with layer.expr-syntax; a record field the object type of the scope lacks with layer.expr-unknown-field; a role neither the platform nor the product declares with layer.expr-unknown-role; a param the scope does not bind with layer.expr-unknown-param; and an operand of a shape its operator does not accept, a comparison of two shapes, or a hop through a field that is not a single reference with layer.expr-type. The nine operators of the language (checked + - *, a timestamp plus or minus milliseconds, ms_between, days, hours, minutes, concat, text) are parsed and typed as the language declares them, with the sign rule for a - before a digit. Each refusal names the product entity, the place and text, and the position in the text. specc gives the same rule codes. |
| product-layers-retention | structural | Supported by external evidence | An object type of a product file that has a field with a personal declaration and no retention rule is refused with layer.retention-missing (D-6584); a type without a personal field needs none. specc gives the same rule code. |
| batched-transaction-equals-per-operation | invariant | Supported by external evidence | For the same operations, a transaction that re-derives the model once produces byte for byte the specification and layout text, the failure, the affected ids, the verdict and the diagnostics of a transaction that re-derives the model after every edit, and its index of declarations never differs from the model it describes. |
| transaction-derives-model-once | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A transaction of property edits, creations, relationships, nested declarations and replacements of nested declarations derives the model of the specification once, when its result is analysed; a deletion or rename derives it once more, before its edits, and the operations after it need no further derivation. |
| format-gaps-rules | invariant | Supported by external evidence | A contract may be a target (realisation target: specified, not implemented, served by no route, never loaded, committed or lowered onto by a derivation or facade); a derivation of a read or command may state header: true (the load with no needs first, its outputs readable by the needs function, then the needs loaded in one round that no need round counts); an untagged union (encoding tag untagged) is written as the raw JSON of its variant, read in declaration order, and validation refuses one with a tagField, contentField or variantCase, two variants written as one JSON shape or a json newtype before another variant (type.encoding); binding.suppliedBy says an input comes from the route or session and never the body, and validation refuses one naming no input or no such supplier (contract.supplied-by), and a form field or argument (form.supplied-input) or action argument (action.supplied-input) that fills one; a function body may write the JSON null where a json value is expected and nowhere else (function.expr-type), and a union variant named like a keyword (plain-json.null) after a dot of a path or in a match pattern, eval may take a lookup that following a reference reads, and sortBy orders lists item by item and unions by variant and fields, as the kernel's Value does. |
| language-checked-arithmetic | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The primitive catalogue holds checked integer arithmetic (add-checked, sub-checked, mul-checked), time arithmetic (add-checked and sub-checked on a timestamp and an int, time-difference, days-ms, hours-ms, minutes-ms) and text (concat-checked, to-text), and the language evaluator gives for them the results and exact error texts of the shared case table: every integer operand and result lies in -(2^53-1) to 2^53-1, and outside it the evaluation fails with integer-overflow (never a wrapped, saturated or rounded value), at both ends of the range and for a product beyond 64 bits; a concatenation longer than 4096 bytes of UTF-8 fails with string-too-long; a null operand gives null; an operand of the wrong kind is the error <op> does not apply to a <kind>; and the checker refuses an operand of the wrong shape (a timestamp on the right of + or -, a timestamp under *, a list under text) with <op> does not accept a <Shape> operand, giving + and - the shape of their left operand. |
| language-grapheme-emoji | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The primitive catalogue holds the grapheme and emoji functions (graphemes, is-emoji), and the language evaluator gives for them Strata's results over its shared case table: graphemes of a string is its count of extended grapheme clusters (UAX #29), so that a combining sequence, a ZWJ emoji sequence, a flag or a keycap is one; is-emoji of a string is true exactly when the string is one grapheme that is a keycap, a flag, a subdivision tag sequence, or ZWJ-joined parts each an emoji presentation or a text-default emoji followed by VS16 or a skin tone; a VS15 makes it not an emoji, and a lone regional indicator, a lone digit, a bare copyright, trademark or heart, two emoji and the empty string are not; a null operand gives null and an operand of another kind is an error. |
| language-ancestor-of | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The primitive catalogue holds ancestor-of, and the language evaluator gives for it Strata's results (WG-5510): ancestor_of(x, y, object, field) with x and y references and object and field string literals is true when x is y or one of its ancestors, the walk from y following the single reference field of the object type; it is false when the walk ends at a null field or an absent record; and it is an error, never a silent false, when the walk takes more than 32 hops, returns to a record it visited, or meets a record the lookup does not cover. Null on a null reference, an error on any other kind. |
| integrity-relations-least-model | invariant | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | For a program of derived relations that the Lean stratification check wf accepts and the checker solve solves, the solved table is exactly the least-model meaning Least: a tuple is in it iff it is in every interpretation that agrees with the lower strata and is closed under the clauses of its stratum. |
| integrity-relations-holds-by-reflection | invariant | Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code | A rule that the Lean checker eval accepts over the solved table of a stratified program holds under the least-model meaning: Sat u (Least u prog) [] rule. |
| integrity-relations-evaluated | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Derived relations are evaluated to their least models, recursion and mutual recursion included, with negation only of lower strata; negative recursion, arity mismatches, unknown relations, unbound clause variables and unknown parameter kinds are named errors that make the relation and every rule using it invalid; and every relation rule of the fixture can be made to fail by a stated mutation that the ratchet refuses. |
| integrity-relations-agree-with-lean-and-nemo | invariant | Supported by external evidence | The JavaScript relation tables equal the tables of the Lean checker and the relations Nemo derives: exegra prove-integrity proves every relation rule of the fixture under Least and refutes every mutation, and on seeded random graphs and programs the Lean tables and the Nemo relations equal the JavaScript tables. |
| admit-never-writes-a-rejected-change | structural | Supported by external evidence | The admit command never writes the specification for a rejected verdict, even with --write, and reports every reason for the rejection (each claim that moved to a worse status, each new validation error, each new integrity violation, each new claim that is not verified or proved, a refused transaction), not only the first. |
| admit-privilege-and-guarantee-changes-need-acknowledgement | structural | Supported by external evidence | The admit command reports every capability a change adds to a role, grant, delegation or token scope, every new or changed grant or delegation without maxDuration, every copy class looser than another of the same record, and every changed integrity rule, claim or retention, each as a reason that blocks admission until its code is accepted with --accept; a reason that is not acknowledgeable cannot be accepted, and an accepted reason is listed under acknowledged. |
| admit-requirement-claims-pend-on-realisation | structural | Supported by external evidence | The admit command admits a new claim that is realised by code (a requirement, or a claim of an external verification method or of external-evidence) when it names a milestone and has no result yet, and lists it under pendingRealisation; a new requirement without a milestone is rejected as admit.requirement-without-milestone; a new claim a model method can decide (structural, data-model, integrity, lean-theorem and the other methods Exegra evaluates) must still be verified or proved. |
| claim-strength-names-weak-checks | structural | Supported by external evidence | claimStrength reports a state invariant whose variables only fault transitions assign as holds-by-construction, naming the variables and those transitions; a counter bound equal to the extreme of its one scenario as restated-scenario; any other trace claim as single-trace, unless the caller supplies an exploration that found it holding (explored); and never changes a status. |
| claim-strength-grounding | structural | Supported by external evidence | claimStrength reports a claim whose behaviors branch only on payload fields of contract-less events as self-contained and names those fields, a claim over a behavior that reacts to a contract event as bound with the capabilities the contract requires, an integrity rule over the real roles as bound, and under a baseline counts an entity the change adds as anchoring nothing. |
| admit-flags-weak-new-claims | structural | Supported by external evidence | admit rejects a claim the change creates that is holds-by-construction or restated-scenario (admit.claim-vacuous), single-trace (admit.claim-single-trace) or self-contained (admit.claim-self-contained), each reason carrying acknowledgeable: true, reports the counts by check and grounding before and after, and never makes an existing claim a reason. |
| residency-rules-checked | invariant | Supported by external evidence | Region, tenantClass and residencyException are entity kinds, components state a placement and relationships the records they carry; for every tenant class whose confinement is not none, every owner of a bound record, holder of a copy of one and end of a relationship carrying one keeps a tenant of the class in its permitted regions, or validation reports residency.record-outside, residency.copy-outside or residency.flow-outside, except where an active residency exception of the class names that copy or flow; the placement, region, tenant class and exception rules are reported at their positions; and integrity rules, recursive relations included, read the residency properties. |
| pooled-quotas-executable | invariant | Supported by external evidence | quotaPool is an entity kind and a scenario lists the pools it runs; a pool runs as one machine per pooled limit and modelled member, whose transitions admit usage on headroom (within the fair share when throttled), throttle a member over its fair share at the end of the stated consecutive windows unless its exemption applies from that window, release after the stated windows under, and record an exemption from the window it is set in or the next; simulation and the Lean explorer run these machines, so a claim over them is decided by exploration and a wrong rule (eligibility effective only from the next window, or a longer over-share window) yields a counterexample; and the pool rules are reported at their positions. |
| machine-singleton-key | invariant | Supported by external evidence | An implementation-level machine that creates its records (storage.creates), has no instances.key and is run by a scenario is reported with machine.singleton-key when a key field of its record has a type with no fixed value for the one instance the runtime creates at the start: only string, uuid or an integer, or an id or scalar over one, in a field that is not optional, has one. A machine keyed by instances.key, or whose key field has such a type, is not. |
| specification-manifest-parts | structural | Supported by external evidence | A specification may be a manifest (specParts) listing part files, and Exegra reads it as the one document the parts make: array sections are concatenated in manifest order, a section that is not an array may be in one part only, and a missing part, a part that is itself a manifest, a manifest with sections of its own, a section two parts both hold and an id two parts give one section are refused with an error that names the files. The semantic hash is that of the one-file specification. exegra apply writes each changed entity back to the part that holds it, and a new entity to the part the manifest's specSections table names for its section (else beside its section, else the first part). |
| derivation-cache-transparent | structural | Supported by external evidence | A derivation served from the cache is observably identical to one computed: validate and claims print byte-identical output on a hit and on a miss, the encoding rebuilds Maps, Sets, objects without a prototype, -0, shared references and cycles exactly, and with EXEGRA_NO_CACHE set nothing is read or written. |
| derivation-cache-keyed-by-content | structural | Supported by external evidence | A cached derivation is reused only for the same input contents and the same Exegra derivation sources: changing a specification file, the file the project config names or any source under src/core or src/engine/lean is a miss, and a missing, truncated or altered entry is a miss that is replaced, never an error. |
| derivation-cache-concurrent-safe | structural | Supported by external evidence | Processes sharing one cache directory at the same time all succeed with the same output and leave only whole entries: a write is a temporary file renamed into place. |
| product-layers-share-platform-analysis | structural | Supported by external evidence | Validating several sets of product files over one platform text in one process parses the platform text once, and every validation reports exactly what a validation of that set alone reports: the same errors, warnings, gaps, semantic hash and layered hash. The shared platform analysis is read-only, so no validation can alter what the next one sees. |

Statuses are evaluation results for semantic hash 0cb40db2b60a01; see claims-report.md for scope and evidence.

## Assumptions

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write. Rationale: The correspondence between the machines and the code is maintained by hand and is not checked or generated. A behavioral claim here is true of the machines; it is true of Exegra only to the extent that this assumption holds.
- `views-derive-from-committed-model`: Every representation (scene, inspector, source editor) derives its content from the committed model snapshot and from interaction state, and from nothing else. Rationale: The equivalence claims compare the revision each view shows with the revision of the transaction system. They say nothing about a view that kept private state of the specification; the session and the panels are written not to, but that is not checked.
- `relationships-match-imports`: The relationships declared in this file match the real imports and calls of the code. Rationale: They are maintained by hand. The traceability check compares only the module paths that components declare with the files on disk, so a new import that is not declared here would not be noticed by any structural claim. Checked by a script when this was corrected (imports of src/core, src/app, src/ui, src/render, src/persist, cli/ and tools/ mapped to components by implementation.modules): 12 import pairs between components had no declared relationship (agent-cli to claim-method-registry, markdown-importer and validator; claim-evaluator and simulation-engine to simulation-contract; validator to metric-claims; markdown-importer to workspace-service; visual-interaction to persistence-client and simulation-engine; formal-semantics to simulation-contract through the differential tool). They are now declared, and the golden tools (tools/conformance.js, tools/record-goldens.js) moved from simulation-contract to test-suites so that declaring them does not close a cycle. In the other direction, 121 declared edges have no import: they are runtime wiring (the composition root main.js, the session and callbacks) or Lean and data relationships, which an import check cannot see. Those are not established by this assumption, and nothing in this file checks them.
- `tests-exercise-shipped-modules`: The core tests import and run the same modules under src/core and tools that the application and the agent command line run. Rationale: Test evidence supports a claim about the shipped code only if the test exercises the shipped code and not a copy.
- `browser-tests-run-real-application`: The browser tests drive the real application served by the Vite development server, against scratch copies of this specification and of the multiplayer test fixture (one pair per Playwright worker) (and, read-only, a project linked locally), in the locally installed Chrome. Rationale: They show behaviour in one browser on one machine; other browsers are not exercised.
- `recorded-results-reflect-current-code`: A recorded result of a test in evidence/results.json was produced by the code and the specification as they are now. Rationale: Recorded results carry an outcome and a timestamp but no hash of the code or the model; staleness is not detected.
- `filesystem-honours-atomic-rename`: Renaming a temporary file over the target replaces the target atomically on the file system holding the project. Rationale: The workspace service relies on it for atomic replacement. On Windows it retries a blocked rename and finally writes in place, which is not atomic.
- `single-workstation`: The browser, the development server and the project files run on one developer workstation: one host, one clock, loopback between browser and server, one local disk. Rationale: This is how Exegra is run today (npm run dev). It says nothing about a shared or hosted deployment.
- `workstation-rates-assumed`: The rates and durations of the workstation failure modes are the author's estimates of ordinary development use, not measurements. Rationale: No telemetry exists for the development server or the browser session. A recorded measurement would replace these values; until then they only order the modes by plausibility.
- `one-fault-at-a-time`: On a developer workstation at most one of the listed failures is in progress at a time; two independent failures overlapping within one save is not considered. Rationale: A single user drives one session; overlapping faults are rare enough to leave out of the fault model, and the model says so.
- `seeded-streams-independent`: Draws from distinct SplitMix64 streams (distinct seeds, or distinct stream names under one seed) behave as independent uniform 64-bit values for the purposes of the simulator. Rationale: Metric claims treat runs with distinct seeds as independent and identically distributed (the exact binomial test of docs/simulation.md). This rests on the statistical quality of SplitMix64 (it passes BigCrush) and of the FNV-1a and mix64 derivation of stream keys, which is tested, not proved.
- `trusted-lean-kernel`: The Lean 4 kernel of the toolchain formal/lean-toolchain pins (v4.35.0) accepts only well-typed proof terms. Rationale: Trusted base of every lean-theorem claim (WG-4792). The kernel is the one component a proof is checked by; nothing in this repository checks it. A theorem is proved only relative to it and to the three standard axioms propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound.
- `trusted-lean-compiler-runtime`: The Lean compiler translates the Lean core faithfully to C, and the Lean v4.35.0 C/C++ runtime, with the two-hunk patch formal/wasm/patches/lean-v4.35.0-runtime-wasm.patch applied, executes that C as Lean defines it. Rationale: Trusted base of the claim that the code which runs is the code the theorems are about (WG-4792). The theorems are about Lean definitions; the compiled module (src/engine/lean/) and the native exegra-lean run the C that the compiler emits (lake build for Exegra, `lean -c` of the toolchain for Init and Std), linked with the runtime. The patch fixes string_to_list_core (a 32-bit-only bug, a no-op on 64-bit) and an emscripten-only stub signature.
- `trusted-emscripten-toolchain`: Emscripten 7.0.0 (clang/LLVM, wasm-ld, binaryen, musl libc, libc++ and the generated JS loader) compiles and links the C of the Lean core and the runtime to WebAssembly (wasm64, Memory64) without changing its meaning. Rationale: Pinned in tools/build-formal-wasm.js (emsdk commit and SDK version). The same C is compiled natively for exegra-lean, and the WebAssembly engine is checked byte for byte against goldens and against the Rust engine, but that is testing, not a proof (WG-4792).
- `trusted-libuv`: libuv v1.48.0, linked into the WebAssembly module because the Lean runtime references it, does not affect the result of a call: the core reaches none of its I/O. Rationale: Pinned by commit in tools/build-formal-wasm.js; compiled from its sources with Lean's emscripten source list (no-proctitle.c). Only error-string paths are reachable from Exegra.Api (WG-4792).
- `trusted-js-glue`: The glue between JavaScript and the Lean core (src/core/lean.js, formal/wasm/glue.c, the emscripten-generated exegra-lean.mjs) passes request and reply bytes unchanged and reports failures as errors. Rationale: Small and hand-written (glue.c, lean.js) or generated (exegra-lean.mjs); tested by tests/core/lean.test.js, not proved (WG-4792).
- `trusted-wasm-engine`: The WebAssembly engine of the host (V8 in Node and Chrome, SpiderMonkey in Firefox) executes the module according to the WebAssembly specification, Memory64 and exception handling included. Rationale: Stable Safari does not ship Memory64, so Exegra's Lean core does not run there until it does (decision 2026-11-18, WG-4792).
- `trusted-os-filesystem`: The operating system and file system store and return the specification, layout and recorded-results files as written. Rationale: Every claim about Exegra's persisted state rests on it; nothing in Exegra checks it.
- `trusted-js-shell`: The JavaScript shell outside the verified kernel (the application UI, layout, projections, scene, generators, live replay, request-log rendering, the agent command line's arguments, workspace I/O, the development server and the JSONC text layer of the parser) computes what it is specified to compute, and the kernel components not yet ported to Lean do too. Rationale: These components are outside the verdict path (decision 2026-11-18, WG-4799): no claim's status is decided by them, they present, store and route what the kernel computes. The kernel components still in JavaScript are trusted, tested and not proved until their ports finish; claim core-logic-in-lean lists them.

## Views

| Id | Name | Projection | Description | Filter | Display |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| architecture | Architecture | structural | Every component and relationship of Exegra, grouped by subsystem. | - | {"showPorts":true} |
| realisation | Realisation | structural | Components coloured by how they are realised: manual, model-configured, generated, not implemented. | - | {"colorBy":"implementation"} |
| data-and-edit-flow | Data and edit flow | structural | Only relationships that carry data, edits or derived content: data-flow, submits-edits, reads, writes, projects, generates. | {"relationshipKinds":["data-flow","submits-edits","reads","writes","projects","generates"]} | - |
| semantic-core-only | Semantic core | structural | Only the components inside the semantic core and the relationships among them. | {"within":"semantic-core"} | - |
| claims | Claims | claims | Claims, their subjects, assumptions and evidence, coloured by evaluation status. | - | - |
| behavior | Edit flow behavior | behavior | The state machines of Exegra's own edit flow and the scenarios that run them. | - | - |
| references | References | references | The selected entity and everything within one reference hop of it, in either direction, whatever its kind. | - | {"depth":1} |
